# Styling Research and Classification Standard v0.5

Status: **lock suspended (LOCK-1-S) — final re-check r11 pending**. Executable procedures for a classification lane, in run order; held routes still require the Leave-in addendum.
Document version: `v0.5, amended 2026-10-14 (R10)` · Engine version: `styling-inci-v0.5-r10amend` · Lexicon: `02_evidence_lexicon.v0.5.md` (`styling-lexicon-v0.5-r7amend`)
Scope: leave-on styling products under the one category key `styling` with 12 subtypes (SD1, SD2, D1), German/EU market, plus the S0 boundary with Leave-in, Heat Protectant, Oil and Dry Shampoo.
Normative source: this Markdown file. Drafted 2026-10-10; v0.2 and v0.3 2026-10-12; v0.4 and v0.5 2026-10-13.
Governing inputs, in order of authority: `plans/styling-concept/02-rulings.md` (SD1–SD5, D1–D8b, PH1-1…PH1-16, PH2-1…PH2-12, R1-Q1…R1-Q4, R1-T, R1-H1…R1-H3, R2-Q1…R2-Q4, R2-T, R2-H1…R2-H6, R3-L, R3-Q1…R3-Q4, R3-H, R3-T, R3-H2…R3-H5, R4-L, R4-Q1…R4-Q7, R4-T, R4-H, R6-Q1, R6-T, LOCK-1-S, R7-F, R7-Q1…R7-Q3, R8-Q1, R8-Q2, R8-H, R9-Q1, R9-Q2, R9-F, R9-P, R10-Q1, R10-Q2 — **governs**) > `00_category_charter.md` > `01_property-set-v0.md` > `plans/styling-concept/01-styling-concept.md` Rev. 2.
Inherited method (reused by reference, cited "LI §x"): `docs/research/leave-in-inci/v1.0/leave-in-classification-standard.md` (Leave-In Standard v1.0). Structural precedent: `docs/research/mask-inci/v1.0/mask-classification-standard.md` (cited "Mask §x") and the engine template `docs/research/category-classification-engine-template.md`.
Evidence: `data/research/styling-inci/concept-v0/calibration-set-r2.json` (90 records), raw INCI/claims/directions in `samples/*.json` and `round2/`; the frozen round-2 cohort `data/research/styling-inci/v1.0/r2/cohort.json` (139 records) with both lanes' outputs (`blind-lane-A-r2/`, `blind-lane-B-r2/`) and `round-2/diff.json`; the frozen round-3 cohort `r3/cohort.json` (143 records) with `blind-lane-A-r3/`, `blind-lane-B-r3/` and `round-3/diff.json`; the frozen round-4 cohort `r4/cohort.json` (143 records) with `blind-lane-A-r4/`, `blind-lane-B-r4/` and `round-4/diff.json`. Counts marked **(node)** were recomputed by a scratch script — on 2026-10-10 over the raw INCI under lexicon v0.1, where marked 2026-10-12 over the round-2 cohort and lane outputs, and where marked 2026-10-13 over the round-3 or round-4 cohort, lane outputs and the round's `diff.json`; the scripts are not committed and no frozen record was edited.

## Version

**Historical v0.5 baseline (2026-10-13) — round-4 rulings folded in; amended by R6/R7/R8 above. Deterministic rules change (evidence-gate input 1, name and lead rules, the care-half list, the straightening extension, the secondary-job order, the registry override (d)) and lexicon families change (FR-2, NF-U, HU-1, CA-1 placements; lexicon v0.5), so this is a version bump; every record a change touches is re-derived in the final confirmation round (rerun obligation, §14), never patched — **round 6**, since the round-5 blind run was aborted after the Codex review and never scored. The final round is judged under R3-L as narrowed by R4-L: only differences in user-facing fields block the lock (§14.1).**

### Amendment to v0.5 (R10, 2026-10-14)

Source: R10-Q1 and R10-Q2 in the governing ledger. The final re-check `r11/` uses `styling-inci-v0.5-r10amend`; formula families, lexicon membership and `styling-lexicon-v0.5-r7amend` stay unchanged. Frozen rounds remain provenance. Scorers pin the exact standard/ledger git revision and read rule quotes from that revision (R9-P).

| Change | Ruling | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Collect every eligible job signal from the confirmed name and all C1/C2 claim types; fill secondary slots with STRONG signals in source order before WEAK signals, excluding the primary and duplicate jobs and retaining the formula gate. A bare hold mention is WEAK; RG3-16 strength/number evidence defines a STRONG hold signal. Primary selection and hold-level mapping stay unchanged. | R10, 2026-10-14 — R10-Q1 | standard §6.10; property set §2.12 |
| Add „Struktur“ / „strukturiert“ to the texture vocabulary. A structured-look description is a WEAK texture signal; Bali Gents Haarpuder supplies it at C2. | R10, 2026-10-14 — R10-Q2 | standard §6.10; property set §2.12 |

### Amendment to v0.5 (R9, 2026-10-14)

Source: R9-Q1 and R9-Q2 in the governing ledger. Targeted `r10/` re-check uses `styling-inci-v0.5-r9amend`; lexicon membership and its stamp stay unchanged. Earlier frozen rounds remain provenance; this amendment does not close the lock.

| Change | Ruling | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Any C1/C2 claim line (including `promise`, `finish` and `other`) may supply an explicit secondary job for every product; pure feel-only lines without a result never do. | R9, 2026-10-14 — R9-Q1 | §6.10; property set §2.12 |
| Several result adjectives: the one matching the named look/subtype leads; otherwise the first. `sleek` is the smooth/straight overall look (shape); `shine` is gloss (surface). NEQI Diamond Glass All ⇒ primary `sleek`, secondary `shine`. | R9, 2026-10-14 — R9-Q2 | §6.10; property set §2.12 |

### Amendment to v0.5 (R7 rulings, 2026-10-13)

Source: LOCK-1-S, R7-F and R7-Q1…R7-Q3 in the governing ledger; independent-review corrections M2, M3, L2 and L5. Lock is suspended pending the targeted `r8/` re-check by both lanes. Earlier frozen rounds remain provenance; round-7 scoring keeps its historical classifications.

| Change | Ruling | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Skip a no-job lead for the next eligible C1/C2 promise line's vocabulary job; if none supplies a job, emit `primary_job: null` + internal `primary_job_unresolved`, without inference from subtype, formula or typed finish/other lines. | R7-F | §4.2, §6.10, emission schema; property set §1 / §2.12 |
| An adjective before a job phrase is not a separate job: Wellaflex „für schwungvolle, definierte Locken“ ⇒ primary `definition`. | R7-Q1 | §4.2, §6.10; property set §2.12 |
| Product-name curl/wave words supply `definition`; descriptive sentences require an explicit define/shape-curls-or-waves promise. „Definition“/„Define“ for a non-curl look supplies none (Bali Gents; Balea Sea Salt Care & Define). Preserve the Beach Waves texture and curl-revival refresh rules. | R7-Q2, R6-T, R4-Q2 | §4.2, §6.10; property set §2.12 |
| Every brand line / collection name is excluded from job words, including Frizz Ease, Traumlocken, Volume Lift, Diamond Sleek, Glow & Shine and Nutricurls; only the product's own name words decide. | R7-Q3 | §4.2, §6.10; property set §2.12 |
| Worked rows: Maria Nila secondaries `volume`, `hold_fix` from C2 „Halt 3/5“; Brilliantine primary `shine` from its one-job C1 name; Balea MEN Pomade has no `definition` secondary. | M2, RG3-16, R4-H (4)/(5) | §11.3, §11.8, §11.9 |
| Suspend the status headers; enumerate the §4.3 (i′) smoothing-film subset as Polysilicone-29 and Silicone Quaternium-18, excluding other SI-1 members. | LOCK-1-S, L2, L5 | standard, charter, property set and lexicon v0.5 |

### Amendment to v0.5 (2026-10-13, R6)

Source: the final confirmation-round owner rulings R6-Q1 and R6-T in the ledger. These are an amendment to the existing v0.5 semantics: the historical targeted `r7/` re-check re-stamped only the affected packets `styling-inci-v0.5-r6amend`; frozen earlier rounds remain provenance. The R7 amendment above is re-checked in `r8/` under `styling-inci-v0.5-r7amend`.

| Change | Ruling | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **Heat-activated smoothing-film blow-out route:** an aqueous spray or lotion whose main active is a heat-activated smoothing silicone film, whose care actives are only at or after the first preservative, and which has no fixative-class polymer, routes to held Styling `smoothing_styler`; the named Ultimate owner override retains FX-1 r8. This replaces D3's NEQI result and R2-Q4. | R6-Q1 | §4.3, §4.5, §4.6, §5, §11.4 |
| **Brand-line prohibition:** Frizz Ease, Glow & Shine and Nutricurls are never job words. | R6-Q1 | §4.2 |
| **Optional/conditional extra application steps do not widen the main required zone.** | R6-T (1) | §6.12 |
| **Required sequential sites produce `all`.** | R6-T (2) | §6.12 |
| **Definition needs an explicit curl/wave promise to define or shape.** A result phrase such as „definierter Look" is not the `definition` job. | R6-T (3) | §6.10 |
| **No vocabulary job in the eligible C1/C2 lead or other C1/C2 promise lines:** emit `primary_job: null` (no main job is shown) + internal `primary_job_unresolved`; never infer a primary from subtype, formula, or typed finish/other lines. The owner-ruled route stands; eligible secondaries are scored independently. | R7 representation fix, 2026-10-13 | §6.10, §8; property set §2.12 |

### Amendment to v0.5 (2026-10-13, Codex review)

Source: the Codex review of the round-5 freeze and the v0.5 documents (2026-10-13), and the rulings R3-H5 and R4-H in the ledger. The review found two S0 packets that showed hold grades (`gard-haarspray-extrastark-pumpspray`: confirmed name „Haarspray extrastark (Pumpspray, ohne Treibgas)"; `got2b-spruehkleber-freeze-haarlack`: C1 promise „für bis zu 72h ultrastarke Styles", both unredacted) and two whose INCI flags carried prior-round editorial labels („repaired by the round-4 freeze, P3-3"), plus text in this standard, the lexicon, the property set and the charter that contradicted R3-H2, R3-H4 (c)/(e), R3-H5, R4-Q4, R4-L and R4-H. **The round-5 blind run was aborted and never scored;** `r5/` stays committed as superseded, and the final confirmation cohort is `r6/` (`build/freeze-cohort-r6.js`: same 143 records and roles, new opaque ids, fixes P5-1…P5-3). No deterministic rule and no lexicon family changes: no version bump (engine `styling-inci-v0.5`, lexicon `styling-lexicon-v0.5`, edited in place).

| Change | Ruling | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **S0 masking covers every §7.4 strength word as a hold grade, also inside compounds:** „extrastark", „ultrastarke", „superstark", „megastark", „sehr stark" written as one word or hyphenated, „Extra Strong", any „krass…", a strength word qualifying the styles or the hairdo („ultrastarke Styles", „starke Frisur"), and a strength run after the product noun in the name („Haarspray extrastark") ⇒ `[CLAIM]`, in names, promise and heat lines and directions. A strength word grading another result noun („maximales Volumen", „Ultra Volumen") stays visible (R2-Q3, RG-13). The freeze's leak check now reads **every string field of every packet**, including compound stems (fix 1; round-6 freeze P5-1) | R2-Q3, §7.4 (Codex finding 1) | §10.2 |
| **No editorial or round label in any packet field:** rule and patch ids, round numbers, freeze/repair provenance and lexicon versions are stripped from every S0, F/U and claims string (e.g. INCI flag „… (repaired by the round-4 freeze, P3-3)" ⇒ „… (repaired)"; status `resolved_by_freeze` ⇒ `resolved`); the leak check scans all string fields (round-6 freeze P5-3) | PH2-6, R2-Q3 (Codex finding 7) | §10.2 |
| **Lexicon v0.5 aligned (edited in place):** the D7 fixative limb reads T_S, not T_LI; the straightening limb 3′ needs the fixative only, whatever LI COND reads (thin-care fixative-led smoothing sprays) | R3-H4 (e), R4-Q4 (Codex finding 2) | lexicon v0.5 §0.2, §2 |
| **Wella EIMI „Haltegrad"/„Haltestufe" is a 1–4 scale everywhere** (1–2 ⇒ 1, 3 ⇒ 3, 4/4+ ⇒ 4; „Haltestufe" = „Haltegrad"): A3's sentence that Glam Mist, Sugar Lift and Flowing Form read `null` „until a re-capture" („no brand maximum, words decide") is replaced — their numbers decide (Glam Mist „Haltegrad 1" ⇒ 1, Sugar Lift „Haltegrad: 3" ⇒ 3, Flowing Form „Haltegrad: 2" ⇒ 1); property set §5.3 and §5.6 aligned | R3-H5 (Codex finding 3) | §2.2 A3, §7.3; property set §5.3, §5.6 |
| **Flowing Form is a held `smoothing_styler` everywhere** (property set §2.1 and charter §5 still said „review") | R3-H2 (Codex finding 4) | property set §2.1, §9 item 25; charter §5 |
| **§13.4 is resolved by R4-H** (items 1–5); the Brilliantine registry row and §11.8 follow R4-H (4): its C1 pack name „brilliantine" is a one-job name (shine) and meets §4.1 input 1 under R4-Q1 ⇒ `shine_finish` by the letter (3.1 D2), not an evidence-gate override; §4.1 (a) no longer lists it; the round-6 freeze's R4-Q1 hand table reads it `one_job` (round 5: `open`) | R4-H, R4-Q1 (Codex finding 5) | §4.1, §4.2, §4.6, §7.5, §11.8, §13.4 |
| **Agreement on deterministic F fields is „resolved, not identical"**: the sentence „deterministic F fields must reach 100 %; a disagreement is a lexicon defect" is replaced — every disagreement is a proven lane error or a closed lexicon/rule gap (fixed in the lexicon, never per product), and only user-facing fields block the lock | R3-L, R3-H4 (c), R4-L (Codex finding 6) | §10.2; property set §7 |
| **Property set §5.6 has 20 binding worked rows**, not 17 (counted) | — (Codex finding 8) | §7.8 |
| Other leftovers aligned: the v0.3 → v0.4 changelog row on R3-Q4 („above T_LI", „Wella Flowing Form unchanged (review)") marked superseded; §14 / §14.1 forward references to „round 5" now name round 6; the governing-input list names R3-H5 and R4-H; lexicon status line and v0.5 notes; property set status, §7 and §9 items 31–33; charter status, §5 PH2-1 consequence and D7 text, §15.2 and the phases table | R3-H2, R3-H4 (e), R4-Q4, R4-H | changelog v0.3 → v0.4, §14, §14.1; lexicon; property set; charter |

### Changelog v0.4 → v0.5 (2026-10-13)

Source: `data/research/styling-inci/v1.0/round-4/diff.json` (`findings_register` RG4-1…RG4-27 with `proposed_fix` and impact class, `packet_issues` P4-1…P4-4, `owner_questions` Q1–Q7, `lane_errors` E4-1…E4-5, `lock_criteria`) and `report.md`; rulings R4-L, R4-Q1…R4-Q7 and R4-T in the ledger. Blind round 4 ran on v0.4 as amended / lexicon v0.4. R4-T approves RG4-1…RG4-27 and P4-1…P4-4 as proposed; RG4-1 is implemented as R4-Q1, RG4-3 as R4-Q3, RG4-4 as R4-Q2, RG4-5 as R4-Q5, RG4-6 as R4-Q4, RG4-9 as R4-Q7, and RG4-2, RG4-7, RG4-8 as R4-Q6. The I-3 items are calculation-log fixes under R4-L: text fixes, no further round on their account.

| Change | Ruling | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **Lock scope: only differences in user-facing fields block the lock** — route and recommendability (incl. held vs committed and the evidence gate), subtype, hold level, weight, finish, flexibility, target zone, application moment, primary and secondary jobs, heat protection, user-shown notes. Differences confined to the **calculation log** (reason code, rule path, internal review triggers, confidence, internal flags, lexicon placements that move no value) do not block: they are fixed in the text and documented, without a further round (example: Bali Curls Sleek Stick — every user field agrees, only reason code and rule path differ) | R4-L | §14.1, §10.2 |
| **Evidence-gate input 1 can be met by the name:** a C1/C2 promise line, **or** a C1/C2 confirmed name whose job words name exactly one job (the §4.2 step-1 test). A name with several job words or none does not meet it; a retailer title (R3-Q2) never does. With no C1/C2 promise line the claim-keyed fields take their no-claim values (hold, finish, flexibility „keine Herstellerangabe" / `unbekannt` unless captured at C1/C2) + `claim_authority_gap`. American Crew „FIBER" ⇒ committed `molding`; Kérastase „CURL MANIFESTO REFRESH ABSOLU Second day curl refreshing spray" ⇒ `leave_in`; American Crew Pomade still needs German directions (P4-1) | R4-Q1 (= RG4-1) | §4.1, §4.2, §4.4, §4.6, §6.10, §8, §9 (SFS-29) |
| **„Beach Waves" / „Strandwellen" / „beachy" name the salt-spray texture look**, not curl definition (`li_function: no`); „Wellen-Spray" / „Wellenauffrischer" (reviving waves) stay curl words. **„Glaze", „Topcoat", „Gloss" are shine words.** Slogans („Big Hair Day", „Salon Blow-Out") name no job. alverde Beach Waves Spray ⇒ committed `salt_spray` (3.4 (ii)); Syoss Glaze Topcoat ⇒ committed `shine_finish` (3.5) | R4-Q2 (= RG4-4) | §4.2, §5, §6.8, §6.10, §10.2, §4.6, §9 (SFS-30) |
| **Only a care verb or care noun is the care half of an R1-Q3 tie:** pflegt/Pflege/Care, Kur, Balsam, Treatment, Feuchtigkeit (spendet Feuchtigkeit), nährt, repariert, „stärkt" as a verb, entwirrt. Result phrases („strong hair", „gepflegte Locken") and line prefixes („Nutri-") are not. Aussie Work That Curl ⇒ `leave_in` | R4-Q3 (= RG4-3) | §4.2, §9 (SFS-31) |
| **The straightening limb 3′ also covers a fixative-led smoothing spray with thin care:** a fixative-class polymer above T_S + a smoothing/straightening sale ⇒ Styling `smoothing_styler` whatever the conditioning reads; held under PH1-16 when the Leave-in letter would claim it. Balea Glattspray Anti-Frizz ⇒ held `smoothing_styler` | R4-Q4 (= RG4-6, extends R3-Q4) | §4.2, §4.3 3.4 (i), §4.6, §5 |
| **Owner-registry rows keep ruling the records they name:** a fourth §4.1 override case, (d) a named D6/D7 border route where the letter differs, until the Leave-in addendum releases or re-rules it. Bali Curls Strong Hold Flaxseed Gel (D6), Kevin.Murphy Killer.Curls and John Frieda Lockencreme Traumlocken (D7) stay held. `li_function` reads the lead promise only (RG3-19): „or confirmed name" is deleted from §4.2 except where the name is the step-1 lead | R4-Q5 (= RG4-5) | §4.1, §4.2, §4.6, §11.19 |
| **Registry identity:** every §4.6 row carries brand and pack (or brand-page) name, plus the old retailer title where the name changed, so a blind lane can match it (HASK „curls defining cream" ⇒ `leave_in`, as ruled) | R4-Q6 (RG4-2) | §4.6 |
| **Routes reached by 3.4 (ii) or 3.5 are never held** (supersedes the §4.5 FX-4 sentence): the FX-4 clause applies only where the Leave-in letter runs (3.4 (i), 3.4 (iii), owner-named routes). Barber Club Pomade, Redken Wax Spray, taft x Gliss Shine Coat stay committed | R4-Q6 (RG4-7) | §4.5 |
| **R3-Q3's „names that name both jobs" means names that join care and styling as the product's two jobs** („Pflege & Styling 2in1/3in1"); a care word beside a styling product noun („Finishing Spray Glow & Care") is a call-out. R1-Q3 (care + curl definition ⇒ `mixed`) wins over R3-Q3. Balea Finishing Spray Glow & Care stays committed `shine_finish`; John Frieda Wellenauffrischer stays manual review | R4-Q6 (RG4-8) | §4.2, §4.6 |
| **A product-type noun in the name names the job; a shine or strength qualifier is the finish or grade, also inside a compound** (as „Haarspray Glanz"); a true job word beside it (Texture, Volume, Curl) makes two jobs ⇒ the lead line decides. Taft Gel Wax Glanz ⇒ `molding`, finish „glänzend", D8b flag kept internally; got2b Texture Clay ⇒ main job `hold_fix` | R4-Q7 (= RG4-9) | §4.2, §4.4, §6.10, §7.5, §4.6 |
| A technology, formula or complex label („…-Technologie", „-Formel", „-Komplex") is a call-out, never a finish word or a job (SFS-15 extended) | RG4-10 | §6.8, §6.10, §9 (SFS-32), §11.7 |
| §11.2 Balea Pro Traumlocken: finish `natürlich` (glänzend + „natürlicher Glanz", RG-10), claims in the round-4 order | RG4-11 | §11.2 |
| „Fixierung" with a strength word reads as „Halt" („leichte Fixierung" ⇒ 1); a fixing verb without a strength word („fixiert die Frisur") states `hold_fix` as a secondary job and sets no level | RG4-12 | §7.4, §6.10 |
| Tie-breaks by form as applied: a powder sold as an aerosol spray ⇒ `texture_spray`; a volume/texture lead with a teasing step ⇒ `texture_spray`, not `hairspray` (the hairspray anchor needs a hold lead) | RG4-13 | §5 |
| Moment and zone cases: stroking over dry hair with no shaping act ⇒ `dry_finish`, flyaways ⇒ `surface`; „Ansatz … bis in die Längen" is a range ⇒ `all`; „vom Ansatz her" with no other site ⇒ `roots` | RG4-14 | §6.11, §6.12 |
| A token that may hide a flag sets that flag to `unknown` (never `false`) + `flags_incomplete` | RG4-15 | §6.6, §10.1, §11.5 |
| Brand scales: got2b six-mark display ⇒ words decide (the 1–6 row gives the same level); Taft gels: „Haltegrad x/5" recorded, words decide | RG4-16 | §7.3 |
| Secondary jobs: take the first two eligible candidates (name job words first, then C1/C2 promise lines in packet order), apply the formula gate, refill from the next eligible candidate; humidity and duration lines never state a job, an explicit anti-frizz word in them does | RG4-17 (closes RG2-16's „open“); historical order, refined by R9-Q1 / R10-Q1 | §6.10 |
| `heat_activated`: the record carries the unblinded value; the blind lanes see the claims packet, so they apply the raise themselves (C1/C2 activation ⇒ `yes`; activation only at C3/C5 ⇒ `unresolved`). The „blind reads F/U only" sentence is dropped | RG4-18 (replaces RG2-26's blind-only reading) | §6.13, §10.2 |
| A T_LI with cationics below it is plausible (PH1-13 reads them); `implausible` only when the marker sits above an oil/ester/fatty-alcohol base that no rule reads below T_LI | RG4-19 | §3.2 |
| Historical NEQI rows (outcomes superseded by R6-Q1): All ⇒ letter `styling_result` ⇒ 3.4 (iii) ⇒ `leave_in` (reason: the letter's pair rule, RG3-14); Ultimate ⇒ the §4.1 (c) override, letter column `mixed` | RG4-20 | §4.6, §11.4 |
| `inci_conflict` is the packet name of the two-list state (= `provisional_formula_conflict` at tier 2); a record with two blocks emits one `boundary_reason` — `formula_conflict` wins, the other block is listed as a trigger; the John Frieda Ansatz-Booster notes are re-derived from the list of record | RG4-21 | §2.3, §4.0, §6.5, §6.7, §7.6 |
| A definition word in the confirmed name is the definition half, as a care word in the name is the care half | RG4-22 | §4.2 |
| Text printed on the German-market pack is C1 whatever its language; `non_german_wording` stays a flag | RG4-23 | §2.2 A13 |

### Changelog v0.3 → v0.4 (2026-10-13)

Source: `data/research/styling-inci/v1.0/round-3/diff.json` (`rule_gap_register`, `packet_issues`, `owner_questions`, `lane_errors`, `lock_criteria`, `smoothing_styler`) and `report.md`; rulings R3-L, R3-Q1…R3-Q4, R3-H and R3-T in the ledger. Blind round 3 ran on v0.3 / lexicon v0.3. R3-T approves RG3-1…RG3-24 and P3-1…P3-7 as proposed, except: RG3-2 is implemented as R3-Q3, RG3-3 as R3-Q2, RG3-5 is superseded by R3-Q4 and RG3-8 is replaced by R3-H.

| Change | Ruling | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **Lock criterion „lanes agree" means resolved, not identical:** v1.0 may lock when every remaining A/B disagreement is resolved — a lane error proven against a clear rule, or the rule gap behind it closed — **and** a final confirmation round shows only lane errors and no new rule gaps | R3-L | §14.1, §10.2 |
| **`smoothing_styler` market-size exception:** it may lock below 5 products (agreed in round 3: KMS Thermashape 2-in-1 Spray committed, Color Wow Dream Coat held; Elnett 3 Tage Glatt joins once its German directions are captured) | R3-Q1 | §14.1, §4.8, §13.1 item 1 |
| **Shop-title fallback:** when neither the pack nor the brand's German page shows the name, the product-type noun of the retailer's title (Hitzeschutzspray, Öl, Serum, Haarspray, Gel, Wachs …) serves as the confirmed-name job word for §4.2 step 1 and D2, review `name_from_retailer_title`; every other word of a retailer title never counts (R2-H1); pack names are captured so the fallback is rarely needed | R3-Q2 (= RG3-3), P3-7 | §4.2, §4.3 3.1, §4.4, §4.6, §8, §9 (SFS-27), §11.21 |
| **A one-job name wins over a lead line that joins that job with care** (care in the line is a call-out, SFS-15); `mixed` stays for names that name both jobs („Pflege & Styling 2in1/3in1"); the Taft „Textur und Pflege" example is deleted from the co-equal list; R1-Q3 / R2-Q1 (care + curl definition ⇒ `mixed`) unchanged | R3-Q3 (= RG3-2) | §4.2, §4.6, §9 (SFS-26), §11.18 |
| **D7 middle band extended to straightening/smoothing creams:** fixative-class polymer above T_LI + substantive conditioning + sold as a straightening/smoothing styler ⇒ Styling `smoothing_styler`, before the mixed stop (as PH1-12), held under PH1-16/PH2-2 until the Leave-in addendum, which must carry the extension; Wella Flowing Form unchanged (review). RG3-5's T15 wording is not adopted *(superseded in part, kept for the record: the fixative is read against T_S, R3-H4 (e); Wella Flowing Form ⇒ held `smoothing_styler`, R3-H2; the straightening limb needs no substantive conditioning since R4-Q4)* | R3-Q4 (supersedes RG3-5) | §4.2, §4.3 3.4 (i), §4.4, §4.5, §4.6, §5, §11.17 |
| **Conflicting C1/C2 hold strengths ⇒ the middle level, rounded up; never empty.** Covers two strength statements on one source and pack vs manufacturer page (replaces PH1-8's „keine Herstellerangabe" outcome); both statements kept; review `hold_claim_conflict` stays internal; C3 never enters; no C1/C2 strength statement ⇒ `null` as before (D8) | R3-H (replaces RG3-8 and PH1-8's outcome) | §2.2 A1, §7.2, §7.8, §8, §9 (SFS-28), §11.19 |
| Name job words: a curl/wave noun (Locken-, Curl, Wellen-) is a curl job word (definition, or refresh when it revives curls); a salt noun names texture; a product noun used as a form in a compound with another product noun (Wax Spray, Sprühwachs, Gel-Spray) names the form, not a job | RG3-1 | §4.2 |
| The lead is the first sentence of the chosen line that states a job; later sentences of that line are secondary claims | RG3-4 | §4.2, §11.20 |
| §11.11 marker corrected: Benzyl Alcohol r8 after Parfum r6 is an allergen declaration, never T_LI ⇒ T_LI `none_visible`; the reading is stated in §3.2 | RG3-6 | §3.2, §11.11; lexicon v0.4 §0.2 |
| Shaping acts add einarbeiten, durcharbeiten, mit den Fingern verteilen und formen; a paste, wax, cream or powder worked into dry hair by hand is `dry_restyle` | RG3-7 | §6.11 |
| „extra Halt" / „Extra Hold" / „Max Hold" (extra, ultra, mega qualifying Halt directly) ⇒ 4 | RG3-9 | §7.4 |
| Taft Paste re-read closed: a five-mark Henkel display whose fifth mark reads 5+ ⇒ x/5 ⇒ 4; the hold claim carries the display description | RG3-10, P3-5 | §2.2 A3, §7.2, §7.3 |
| A negated hold claim (kein Halt, ohne Halt) is not a hold claim for D8b rule 1 | RG3-11 | §7.5 |
| A care product noun in a C2 descriptor line (Pflegecreme, Pflegespray) is not a care job unless it is the confirmed name; moisture-locking („schließt Feuchtigkeit ein") is the moisturise care job | RG3-12 | §4.2, §11.3 |

Source: `data/research/styling-inci/v1.0/round-2/diff.json` (`rule_gap_register`, `packet_issues`, `owner_questions`, `lane_errors`, `s0_redaction_check`) and `report.md`; rulings R2-Q1…R2-Q4 and R2-T in the ledger. |---|---|---|
| The care half of a care + curl-definition tie is a **care job the product promises** (spendet Feuchtigkeit, pflegt, repariert, entwirrt; „Pflege"/„Care"/„Detangling" in the name); a result adjective („gepflegte Locken"), a feel line or an ingredient call-out is not. The definition half needs „definiert"/„Definition"; reviving or reactivating curls is refresh. Narrows R1-H2; D6 (PH1-13), T15 and D7 then run as before | R2-Q1, RG2-1 | §4.2, §4.6, §9, §11.13 |

| Blind S0 masking keeps job nouns and masks only grading words (exact keep/mask lists); the unmasked confirmed name goes into the claims packet; only affected records are re-frozen and re-run | R2-Q3, RG2-3, P2-1 (refines PH2-6) | §10.2 |

| A strength phrase that is the lead's subject („Intensiver Halt für …") states `hold_fix` | RG2-4 | §6.10 |
| A curl/wave noun with a shaping or setting verb states curl definition (`li_function: yes`, primary `definition`); name job words naming the same job count once | RG2-5 | §4.2, §6.10 |
| Schwung, Sprungkraft, Spannkraft, Fülle ⇒ `volume`; Elastizität is a flexibility word | RG2-6 | §6.9, §6.10 |
| Taming flyaways / abstehende Haare / Babyhaare ⇒ `frizz_control`; `sleek` only for a smooth result of the whole style | RG2-7 | §6.10 |
| `dry_restyle` needs a named shaping act; spraying or distributing on dry hair without one ⇒ `dry_finish` | RG2-8 | §6.11 |
| Directions with a shaping step but no moisture state, drying or heat step ⇒ `dry_restyle`, moderate | RG2-9 | §6.11 |

| „natürlich aussehendes Finish" / „natürlich wirkender Look" ⇒ `natürlich`; halbmatt, seidenmatt ⇒ `matt` | RG2-11 | §6.8 |

| „Kontrolle" with a strength word reads as „Halt" (mittlere Kontrolle ⇒ 2); krass ⇒ 3; ultra krass / krasser Halt at the top of a got2b display ⇒ 4 | RG2-18 | §7.4, §13.2 |

| T_LI vacuity: essential oils at/after `Parfum` or in the allergen block are closed set (b) | RG-1 | §3.2; lexicon §0.2 |
| Spray-only directions ⇒ `dry_finish` (moderate) | RG-2 | §6.11 |
| `identity_status` describes product and formula identity only; a directions or claim gap is an evidence-gate fact | RG-7 | §2.3 |
| Equal alternatives: the first-named moment is primary; §11.8 corrected | RG-8 | §6.11, §11.8 |
| `roots` when the Ansatz is the only or first-named site, incl. an either/or | RG-9 | §6.12 |
| matt + natürlich ⇒ matt; glänzend + natürlich ⇒ natürlich; natürlich needs Finish/Look/Glanz next to it | RG-10 | §6.8 |
| Primary job = first job-bearing phrase; product nouns and strength phrases are not job-bearing; shaping ⇒ `hold_fix` | RG-11 | §6.10 |
| Secondary jobs from hold-strength, anti-frizz, shine and definition words, in claim order | RG-12 | §6.10 |
| A strength word in a name counts only when it qualifies hold or the product as a whole; name words never become hold claims otherwise | RG-13, P-1 | §7.4, §2.2 A3 |
| „Kleber" is a rigidity word only, no longer a strength word | RG-14 | §6.9, §7.4 |
| Water-based-serum exception gets a test and a trigger | RG-15 | §4.3 3.1, §8 |
| Malic Acid → PH-1; Polysilicone-9 → SI-4; PQ-69 → FX-R (ambiguous hold group) | RG-16, R1-T | lexicon v0.2; §4.3, §6.2 |
| A2 release test: a dated C1 pack capture with the record's GTIN, newer than both lists | RG-17 | §2.2 A2 |
| `tail_marker_vacuous` / `_implausible` fire only when a border rule on the path reads T_LI | RG-18 | §8 |
| Untested gaps carried to round 2 | RG-20 | §13.2, §14 |
| Kevin.Murphy Killer.Curls released by the capture ⇒ D7 `curl_cream`, held | RG-21 | §4.6 |
| Claim-capture rules implied by packet issues: house-brand stamp only per A5; name-derived flexibility words become flexibility claims; editor annotations are never claim text | P-2, P-4, P-6 | §2.2 A3, A5 |
| Particulate cohort count: 11 confirmed (property set's 10 omitted the lexicon's Cellulose placement) | — (housekeeping) | §6.1; property set §2.3 |
| Contradictions not resolvable from the ledger | — | §13.2 |
| Name with two job words does not decide the lead (Elnett ⇒ pack front, PH1-14 kept); the v0.1 „Pflegecreme" adjective clause is retired under R1-Q3; Polysilicone-9 is FX-U (ambiguous hold, D6 hold limb unresolved ⇒ review) | R1-H1, R1-H2, R1-H3 | §4.2, §13.2; lexicon v0.2 |

Packet issues P-1, P-3, P-5, P-7, P-8 are data re-captures (handled outside this file); they imply no new rule beyond those listed.

### v0.1

**v0.1 (drafted 2026-10-10, ruled 2026-10-11) — first standard.** It operationalises the ruled property set and charter. It adds no property and reopens no ruling. On 2026-10-11 Nick ruled the six proposals P-S1…P-S6 as PH2-1…PH2-6 and six further points as PH2-7…PH2-12 (`plans/styling-concept/02-rulings.md`); the body below states the ruled rule, and §12 records the mapping.

**Marking convention.**
- `[D6]`, `[PH1-13]` … — the passage encodes that owner ruling.
- `[LI §x reused]` — a Leave-in rule adopted unchanged (PH1-8, PH1-2, PH1-3, PH1-4, PH1-15 tell us which ones).
- `[operational]` — a mechanical reading that follows directly from a ruled or reused rule (usually LI §1.1); calibration may test it.
- `[PH2-n]` — the passage encodes a Phase 2 ruling (2026-10-11). The six former proposals P-S1…P-S6 are ruled as PH2-1…PH2-6 (§12); no proposal is open.
- `[R1-Qn]`, `[RG-n]`, `[P-n]` — the passage encodes a round-1 ruling (2026-10-12): an owner answer (R1-Q1…R1-Q4) or an item of the technical bundle approved by R1-T (rule gap RG-n, packet issue P-n, `round-1/diff.json`).
- `[R2-Qn]`, `[RG2-n]`, `[P2-n]` — the passage encodes a round-2 ruling (2026-10-12): an owner answer (R2-Q1…R2-Q4) or an item of the technical bundle approved by R2-T (rule gap RG2-n, packet issue P2-n, `round-2/diff.json`).
- `[R2-Hn]` — the passage encodes a post-recheck clarification of v0.3 (2026-10-12, R2-H1…R2-H6; amendment table above).
- `[R3-L]`, `[R3-Qn]`, `[R3-H]`, `[RG3-n]`, `[P3-n]` — the passage encodes a round-3 ruling (2026-10-12, folded in 2026-10-13): the lock reading (R3-L), an owner answer (R3-Q1…R3-Q4), the hold-conflict rule (R3-H) or an item of the technical bundle approved by R3-T (rule gap RG3-n, packet issue P3-n, `round-3/diff.json`).
- `[R4-L]`, `[R4-Qn]`, `[RG4-n]`, `[P4-n]` — the passage encodes a round-4 ruling (2026-10-13): the lock scope (R4-L), an owner answer (R4-Q1…R4-Q7) or an item of the register approved by R4-T (finding RG4-n, packet issue P4-n, `round-4/diff.json`).

**Superseded text, named so nobody applies it.** Property set v0's `drying_alcohol_load` (high/present/none) and `washability` are replaced by PH1-3 and PH1-4; the property-set file already reflects this. The charter's former step 5 ("Styling never fills the heat role") is superseded by PH1-15. The concept Rev. 2 still carries that line (charter §13 item 9); this standard follows PH1-15. Where the calibration file's `route`, `v1_subtype`, `application_moment` or `proposed_finish` disagree with a rule below, the rule governs and the record is re-derived in the blind round, never hand-edited (Mask rerun obligation).

**What this draft is not.** Not calibrated, no repeatability claim, no catalog, Supabase, intake, matcher or copy change (charter §16).

---

## 1. Purpose and the lane run order

This standard turns one exact German-market styling product (exact pack, exact formula version) into an auditable research record. A lane runs these steps in this order and never skips ahead:

| Step | What | Section | Inputs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Identity and formula freeze; directions and claim capture | §2 | sources |
| 2 | Read both tail markers | §3 | frozen INCI |
| 3 | S0 routing gate | §4 | frozen claims (C1/C2 only), directions, INCI |
| 4 | Subtype (only if `route = styling` or a held Styling lean, §4.5) | §5 | promise, directions, format |
| 5 | Properties (F, U, C kinds) | §6 | frozen packet |
| 6 | Hold level normalisation, D8b flag, neutral fact | §7 | frozen claims, F values |
| 7 | Derived echoes and cautions (computed last, never reviewed per product) | §6.16 | the above |
| 8 | Review triggers and the record envelope | §8, §10 | everything |

### 1.1 The conservative-failure invariant `[LI §1.1 reused]`

Every hard rule here — threshold, closed list, gate, marker convention — fails toward **human review** or toward **the anchor's conservative value**, never toward a recommendation, an upgrade or a value that reads better for the user than the evidence supports (LI §1.1, Mask §1.1). Consequences carried through this file:

1. An implausible or unresolved input can neither qualify nor disqualify (LI §3.1.1 clauses 5–6; Mask §3.1.1).
2. "Unresolved" always means review, never a default good answer.
3. S0 fails toward `provisional_boundary` and the product's current category, never toward a confident new route (charter §5).
4. Never encode unresolved uncertainty as an extreme (Mask §1.1): an unresolved reading never produces `weight: rich` or `light`, a hold level, or `provides_heat_protection: true`.

**Conformance of the Styling additions.** Every `[operational]` rule and every ruled proposal in §12 was checked against this bar: each one either fails to review / the conservative value, or resolves only on converging evidence.

### 1.2 Separation of evidence kinds (property set §0, §7)

| Kind | Source | May set |
|---|---|---|
| **R** routing | S0 (§4) | `route`, `styling_subtype` |
| **F** formula-blind | frozen INCI only | `result_mechanisms`, `hold_route`, `weight`, `alcohol_note`, `ingredient_flags`, the buildup note's formula limbs, the F half of `provides_heat_protection`, molding `base`, curl-cream `conditioning_co_load` |
| **U** usage | frozen directions transcription | `application_moment`, `target_zone`, `heat_activated` (directions half) |
| **C** claim | C1/C2 claims only (LI §2.4.1) | `chaarlie_hold_level`, `flexibility`, `finish`, `primary_job`/`secondary_jobs`, gel `cast_expected`, molding `form_label`, `captured_claims`, the C half of `provides_heat_protection` |
| **D** derived | the above | echo fields, cautions, hold display, neutral fact |

Subtype, product name and format **never set an F value** (LI G9 parity). One bounded reading uses format: the lexicon FX-2c check may read the identity `format` (thin spray or foam with no RH-1 member) to confirm that a generic Acrylates Copolymer is a fixative; it can only resolve that one membership, and without corroboration the value falls to `ambiguous` + review, never to a better reading. F never sets a C value and C never sets an F value. The only crossings are the three in property set §7 (heat-activation raise; job gate; D8b / finish sanity / weight-claim conflict as review notes that move no value) plus PH1-15's two-part heat test (§6.7).

---

## 2. Identity, formula freeze and claim capture (lane step 1)

### 2.1 Reused unchanged `[PH1-8; LI §2.4, §2.4.1, §2.4.2 reused]`

| LI rule | What it fixes for Styling |
|---|---|
| LI §2.4 G1 checklist | exact product, brand, name, market, pack size, GTIN (or a documented gap), dated exact-market INCI, normalised fingerprint, **verbatim directions with tier**, `claims[]`, conflicts preserved |
| LI §2.4 mandatory directions capture | Verbatim, source-stamped, G1 source hierarchy (pack → DE manufacturer → exact-GTIN DE retailer → other DE/EU retailer). Missing directions are a documented gap: U fields go `unresolved`, never an INCI guess. **Directions are not claim-gated** (LI §2.4.1 last paragraph) — a C3 directions text is valid for U fields, with confidence `moderate` |
| LI §2.4 mandatory claim capture | `claims[]` is part of the freeze; "not captured" ≠ "does not exist"; lanes never re-research a claim; a claim found mid-classification reopens the freeze |
| LI §2.4.1 rules 1–7 | Only C1 (current DE pack) and C2 (manufacturer DE page) create a claim. **Rule 6:** a retailer's page for its **own** private label is C2 (named: dm ↔ Balea/alverde, Rossmann ↔ Isana), recorded `claim_tier_basis: house_brand`, routed to review for ownership confirmation. **Rule 7:** a non-German manufacturer page counts as `C2_cross_market_verified` only with identity verified (identical INCI or a DE-confirmed shared GTIN) **and** a DE retailer corroborating. Rule 2: C1 vs C2 conflict ⇒ preserve both, take the more conservative reading, review |
| LI §2.4.2 / T18 | Same-market INCI set conflicts: ≥ 3 identical lists incl. one GTIN-anchored DE retailer ⇒ convergent list is the record (`verified_with_minor_source_difference`, confidence −1, review); otherwise affected fields `unknown`, `formula_source_conflict`, no downstream use. Cross-market differences are identity questions (rule 7), never convergence counts |
| LI §2.4 packet requirements | Sources stamped C1–C5; normalisation never splits inside an ingredient name (lexicon §0.4) |

### 2.2 Styling additions

| # | Rule | Basis | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | **Conflicting C1/C2 hold strengths ⇒ the middle level, rounded up; never empty `[R3-H]`.** When a pack (C1) and the manufacturer page (C2) disagree on the strength, or two C1/C2 strength statements of one source normalise (§7.2–§7.4) to different levels, `chaarlie_hold_level` is the middle of the two levels, rounded up (2 vs 4 ⇒ 3; 3 vs 4 ⇒ 4). Both statements are kept in the trace; review `hold_claim_conflict` stays internal. **This replaces PH1-8's outcome** (a pack-vs-page conflict used to give `null`, „Halt: keine Herstellerangabe"). C3 retailer chips never enter the calculation, and a product with no C1/C2 strength statement still reads `null` (D8, §7.2 step 6) | PH1-8 as replaced by R3-H | `bali-curls-strong-hold-flaxseed-gel`: C2 „Strong Hold" (⇒ 3) and C2 „Extra-starker Halt" (⇒ 4) on one source ⇒ **4** (§11.19) |
| A2 | **Two INCIs under one GTIN (same German market) ⇒ apply LI T18 first.** (1) **Tier 1 convergence:** ≥ 3 independent captures return the identical list in identical order, ≥ 1 of them a GTIN-anchored German retailer capture, manufacturer formula markers matching where visible ⇒ the convergent list is the formula of record, outliers kept as demoted captures, `identity_status: verified_with_minor_source_difference`, confidence −1 on every affected dimension, review `formula_or_identity_conflict`, and classification proceeds normally. (2) **Only when convergence fails ⇒ tier 2:** `identity_status: provisional_formula_conflict`, S0 `provisional_boundary` (`boundary_reason: formula_conflict`), review `formula_source_conflict`, no Styling value committed, until a **C1 pack capture** identifies the unit on sale or a later capture set reaches tier 1. **Release test `[RG-17]`:** a **dated C1 pack capture carrying the record's GTIN, newer than both conflicting lists**, is the formula of record ⇒ `identity_status: verified_with_minor_source_difference`, review `formula_or_identity_conflict`, and classification proceeds (the freeze re-runs; any other gate still applies — e.g. a missing-directions evidence gate). Cross-market differences are identity questions (LI §2.4.1 rule 7), never convergence counts | PH1-8 (P3 example), LI §2.4.2 T18, RG-17 | `bali-curls-leave-in-spray-curl-defining`: only two captures, and they disagree — dm.de (GTIN 4262391990490; Hydrolyzed Corn Starch r3, no care) vs bali-care.com/de-de (same GTIN; olivate, Cetyl Alcohol, proteins). Tier 1 is not reachable on this evidence ⇒ **stays blocked** (`formula_conflict`) until a C1 pack capture, or further independent captures that make one list reach the full tier-1 bar. *Round 1 (RG-17):* the frozen packet now carries a C1 current list for this record and for `cantu-shea-butter-extra-hold-edge-stay-gel`; if that capture meets the release test, both are re-frozen as `verified_with_minor_source_difference` (Cantu then still stops at the evidence gate: no directions) |
| A3 | **Typed claim capture.** Each `claims[]` entry carries `type` ∈ {promise, hold, flexibility, finish, cast, duration, humidity, heat_protection, heat_activation, hair_type, free_from, other}, `raw` (verbatim DE), `tier`, `source`, `date`; hold entries add `number`, `scale_as_written`, `words`. The confirmed **product name is a C1/C2 claim** once a pack image or the manufacturer page shows it (charter §7); the **full pack name** is kept (no word dropped). Where neither shows it, a retailer title is a C3 capture, and only its product-type noun may stand in for the name, in the §4.2 step-1 lead and D2 (`name_from_retailer_title`, `[R3-Q2]`) — **and only when that noun agrees across the retailer titles captured; if they differ, no fallback, review `retailer_title_type_noun_conflict` `[R3-H4 (d)]`**. A pack photo counts as the pack under A11. **Name-derived claim entries `[P-1, P-2, RG-13]`:** a rigidity or flexibility word of §6.9 in the confirmed name („Kleber", „Freeze") is added as a `flexibility` claim with `from_product_name: true`; a strength word in the name is added as a `hold` claim **only** under the §7.4 name test (it qualifies hold or the product as a whole — never „Ultra" in „Ultra Volumen"). **Editor annotations are never claim text `[P-6]`:** capture notes („(no gloss/matt claim)", „matt: '…'", a hold text „null") go to a separate note field; an empty claim is dropped, never recorded as a claim. **A hold entry's `words` come only from its own `raw` `[P2-5]`:** a word with no claim line of its own is either captured as its own claim with provenance (e.g. a dm „Halt" attribute as a C2 `house_brand` hold claim, PH2-7) or dropped (`balea-haargel-hair-jelly`: claim „flexiblen Halt", `words` also „soft" with no line — re-captured). **P2-5 applies to every record `[P3-2]`:** each strength phrase found on the page gets its own C1/C2 hold line; words with no line are dropped (round 3: 16 hold entries still carried a strength word not in their own text, node, `round-3/diff.json` `packet_checks.hold_words_not_in_text` — the Wella EIMI records Glam Mist, Sugar Lift, Flowing Form and Smooth Thermal Image were named for re-capture). *Round-4 freeze (node, `r4/FREEZE-NOTES.md` §7):* every hold entry keeps only words found in its own text (47 entries corrected); a strength phrase found verbatim in another C1/C2 line of the same record became its own hold line (5: Alcina Styling Mousse, NIVEA MEN Active Craft, Taft Power Haargel, Wella Ocean Spritz, Wella Smooth Thermal Image); strength phrases found in no captured C1/C2 line were dropped. **For the Wella EIMI records the brand number decides `[R3-H5]`** — „Haltegrad"/„Haltestufe" is a 1–4 scale (§7.3), so no recaptured strength word is needed: Glam Mist „Haltegrad 1" ⇒ 1, Sugar Lift „Haltegrad: 3" ⇒ 3, Flowing Form „Haltegrad: 2" ⇒ 1. *Superseded (v0.5 before the 2026-10-13 amendment): „no brand maximum, words decide", so these three read `null` until a re-capture.* **A hold claim read from a printed display carries the display description `[P3-5]`** — marks, filled marks, labels as printed — in the claims packet, so a lane sees what was read (`taft-styling-paste-forming-halt-5plus`: four filled dots and a larger fifth circle „5+", §7.2 step 2). **Every promise line carries its `position` `[P4-3]`** — `pack_front` (any line on the pack front: headline, badge or front bullet), `headline` (a headline elsewhere: pack back, page title block), `bullet` (a bullet list off the pack front, e.g. a retailer or house-brand bullet), `body` (running text) — where the capture records it; where it does not, `position: unknown` (the freeze never guesses a position). **Heat sentences inside a promise line are typed `[P4-2]`:** a C1/C2 promise line that also states protection from heat carries that sentence as its own `heat_protection` claim (same tier, source and date); the promise line keeps its full text | charter §7, property set §2.16, P-1, P-2, P-6, P2-5 | `wellaflex-schaumfestiger-locken-und-wellen-starker-halt`: name „Starker Halt" (C1) + 3 of 5 dots (C1) |
| A4 | **House-brand structured attributes.** On a house brand's own retailer page (rule 6), the retailer's structured product fields („Halt: sehr starker Halt") are the brand owner's product data ⇒ C2 `house_brand`, review `[PH2-7]`. **Filter chips and attributes on third-party products are C3/C4 traces and never create a claim** | LI §2.4.1 rule 6; charter §6 | `balea-styling-spray-ultra-volumen` dm attribute ⇒ C2 house-brand; `color-wow-dream-coat-supernatural-spray` Douglas „Haltgrad" chip ⇒ C3 trace |
| A5 | Retailer-exclusive brands are **not** house brands unless ownership is documented. **The `C2_house_brand` stamp is applied only to the brands LI rule 6 names (dm ↔ Balea, alverde; Rossmann ↔ Isana) or a brand whose retailer ownership is documented in the record `[P-4]`**; every other retailer-page claim is stamped C3 at capture, never C2 | charter §13 item 5, P-4 | Langhaarmädchen ⇒ C3 (`langhaarmaedchen-lockenspray-halt-sprungkraft`: round 1's C2 stamp is re-stamped C3) |
| A6 | A reformulation reopens the record; frozen records are never rewritten. The GTIN may survive it | charter §8 | Syoss Curl Cream Gel, Syoss Max Hold Wax, John Frieda Volume Lift (three formulas) |
| A7 | Possible duplicates are resolved by GTIN before calibration, never by name | charter §8 | Balea „Finishing Spray Glow & Care" vs „Glow&Shine Finishing Spray" |
| A8 | One product = one route; other uses are recorded only | PH1-7 | `balea-men-pomade-haar-bart-2in1`: beard use recorded, one Styling `molding` record |
| A9 | **Claim host rule (C2 vs C5) `[R2-H6]`.** The host decides the tier, not the domain ending: a **manufacturer's German-language storefront for the German market** counts as C2 when the brand's .de domain points to it (redirects there or names it as the German site). English or other-market manufacturer pages stay **C5** (LI §2.4.1 rule 7 still applies to them), and a German translation of a non-German page is not a German page. A line counts as C2 only where it is **found on the German page**; lines found only on the other-market page stay C5, even on the same record. A retailer page stays C3 (C2 `house_brand` only under A5) | R2-H6; LI §2.4.1 rules 1, 3, 7 | `kevin-murphy-killer-curls`, `kevin-murphy-powder-puff-volume`: kevinmurphy.com.au/de/de (kevinmurphy.de redirects there) ⇒ **C2**. `redken-one-united-all-in-one-multi-benefit-treatment`: lines found on redken.eu/de-de ⇒ C2 with that source; the rest of the redken.co.uk lines ⇒ C5. `evo-head-mistress-cuticle-sealer`: evohair.com/us ⇒ C5 (no German page found). `wella-eimi-nutricurls-fresh-up`: lines found on wella.com/professional/de-DE ⇒ C2 (previously mis-stamped C2 from lookfantastic.de, a C3 retailer) |
| A10 | **INCI page error `[R2-H1]`.** When a manufacturer page prints an INCI that is evidently another product's list — it contradicts the page's own formula text (named actives absent, a different product type's architecture) while independent captures agree with each other and with that text — the printed list is recorded as a **page error**, not as a capture for LI T18 convergence; the consistent list is the formula of record (its own tier unchanged), `identity_status` is not downgraded, and the record carries review `formula_source_page_error` until a pack INCI (C1) is captured | R2-H1 | `wella-eimi-nutricurls-fresh-up`: wella.com DE prints a wax/pomade list (Ceresin, Petrolatum, Carnauba, iron oxides; no jojoba, no wheat) under a page text naming wheat bran extract and jojoba oil; the 29-item retailer list (lookfantastic.de, also beautyflash.co.uk) matches that text ⇒ formula of record (C3), page list kept as `inci_page_error`, review `formula_source_page_error` |
| A11 | **Retailer-hosted pack photos `[R3-H4 (f)]`.** A photo of the pack hosted by **any German retailer** (dm, Douglas, Flaconi, easycosmetic …) counts as **C1** when the **GTIN** or the **exact pack name and size** match the record; this extends the dm-hosted reading used since round 1. A photo of another size (a travel or mini pack), or one whose size cannot be read when no GTIN is matched, does not confirm the name: the record keeps its retailer-title name under A3 / R3-Q2 | R3-H4 (f) | `bumble-and-bumble-brilliantine`: Douglas pack photo „brilliantine", 50 ml = the record's 50 ml ⇒ C1. `redken-styling-beach-spray`: easycosmetic photo „BEACH SPRAY", 150 ml ⇒ C1. `color-wow-dream-coat-supernatural-spray`: Douglas photo of the 50 ml mini against the record's 200 ml, no GTIN ⇒ not C1 |
| A12 | **Directions from the brand's own German advice article `[R3-H3]`.** Where no pack back and no product-specific manufacturer directions are in reach, a product-specific how-to in the manufacturer's own German article counts as **C2 directions**, review `directions_from_brand_article`. A generic line copied across a product family (the archived Elnett product-page „Anwendung", word for word the Elnett hairspray line) is not this product's directions | R3-H3 | `loreal-paris-elnett-stylingspray-hitzeschutz-3-tage-glatt`: loreal-paris.de „Haar dauerhaft glätten" (towel-dry after washing, ca. 30 cm, comb through, blow-dry, optional flat iron) ⇒ C2 directions (§11.7) |
| A13 | **German-market pack text is C1 whatever its language `[RG4-23]`.** A promise line printed on the German-market pack in English (or any other language) is a C1 line; `non_german_wording` stays a capture flag and changes no tier | RG4-23 | `aussie-leave-in-creme-work-that-curl` („5 days of curl definition … and strong hair*"), `hask-lockencreme-curl-care`, `cantu-shea-butter-extra-hold-edge-stay-gel`, `cantu-lockenspray-comeback-curl-next-day` ⇒ C1 (both round-4 lanes) |
| A14 | **Directions must come from a G1 tier `[P4-1]`.** The LI §2.4 G1 directions hierarchy is pack → German manufacturer → exact-GTIN German retailer → other German/EU retailer. A page outside it — a non-German manufacturer page (C5) — is not captured directions: the record carries `directions_status: missing` (the text is kept in the record's history, never in a packet), the evidence gate fails on input 2 and the U fields read `unresolved` until German directions (pack back or a German retailer) are captured | P4-1; LI §2.4 | `american-crew-pomade`: directions from americancrew.com (C5) ⇒ `missing` in the round-5 freeze; under R4-Q1 its name „Styling Pomade" could meet input 1, so input 2 is its remaining block |

### 2.3 Identity states

`verified` · `verified_with_minor_source_difference` · `provisional_formula_conflict` · `provisional_identity_conflict` · `insufficient_information` (LI §2.4). **`inci_conflict` `[RG4-21]`** is the packet name of the two-list state: two INCI lists captured for one product that do not reach T18 tier 1 (§2.2 A2); a lane reads it as `provisional_formula_conflict` (S0 `provisional_boundary (formula_conflict)`), and the S0 packet ships the second list as `identity.inci_alt`. **`identity_status` describes the product and formula identity only** (exact product, GTIN or a documented gap, INCI set). **A directions or claim gap never changes it**; it is carried by the S0 evidence gate (§4.1) with the missing input named `[RG-7]` (e.g. `balea-styling-finishing-stick`, no directions ⇒ `verified` + `provisional_boundary (evidence_gate)`). A conflict affecting one property makes **that property** `unknown`; it blocks the whole record only when the dominant architecture is unreadable (LI §2.4). Any state other than the two `verified` ones sends S0 to `provisional_boundary` (§4.1).

---

## 3. Evidence scale and the two tail markers (lane step 2)

### 3.1 Scale `[LI §3.1 reused]`

E0 claim · E1 formula observation or directions transcription · E2 any value read from the formula (the ceiling) · E3–E5 exact-product testing (reachable, unobserved). The assignment ladder of LI §3.1 binds every field (lexicon §0.1). Confidence (`high`/`moderate`/`low`, Mask §4.1) is **internal only** — never projected, never user-facing (LI G14).

### 3.2 Markers `[PH1-1, PH2-9]`

Every record states **T_S** (Styling marker: Mask §3.1 classes — fragrance, declared allergens/aroma chemicals, preservatives, boosters in a pair, chelators) and **T_LI** (LI §3.1.1 capped-preservative list plus other Annex V preservatives). Exact class lists: lexicon §0.2. **T_S is the full Mask rule `[PH2-9]`**, as approved under PH1-1 — the earliest of preservative, perfume, declared allergens/aroma chemicals, preservative boosters in a pair and chelators; not the ledger's short form „first preservative or perfume“. **T_S feeds every Styling property; T_LI feeds only the S0 border rules** (LI G0/T15, D6/R2, D7, the PH1-16 double-claim check). Each record carries `tail_marker: {T_S: {inci, rank, status}, T_LI: {inci, rank, status}}` with `status` ∈ `plausible · unresolved · absent` for T_S and LI's `plausible · implausible · vacuous · none_visible` for T_LI.

### 3.3 Unresolved or absent T_S `[operational — Mask §3.1.1 consequences + §1.1]`

Mask §3.1.1 says an unresolved marker may neither qualify nor disqualify, caps confidence and routes to review. The deterministic reading for a lane:

1. Read the list twice: **R1** with the marker as listed, **R2** with the next ordinally consistent T_S member.
2. Species ranked between the two markers are `unpositioned`. They are recorded (E1) and credited to **nothing** that improves a value: not to `result_mechanisms`, not to `hold_route: present`, not to the neutral fact, not to the heat test.
3. Each F property is computed under R1 and R2. **Same value ⇒ that value**, confidence ≤ `moderate`, trigger `tail_marker_unresolved`. **Different values ⇒** `hold_route: ambiguous`; `weight: unknown`; mechanisms = the R1 set with the `unpositioned` members listed; review.
4. **Absent** (no T_S class anywhere) ⇒ the whole list is above-tail, confidence ≤ `moderate`, trigger `tail_marker_absent` (Mask §3.1).

Cohort (node): 9 of 70 Styling records have a non-plausible T_S (8 unresolved, 1 absent). On all of them R1 and R2 give the same `hold_route` and `weight`; no value rests on this rule today.

**Marker-class readings `[RG2-23]`** (lexicon v0.4 §0.2): an Annex V antioxidant (sulfites, e.g. Sodium Bisulfite) sets neither T_S nor T_LI unless the list declares it „as preservative"; Benzyl Alcohol ranked above `Parfum` reads PR-1 (a preservative), not a declared allergen, so it never fires the third limb (i) there. **Benzyl Alcohol at or after `Parfum` is an allergen declaration and never T_LI `[RG3-6]`:** it is T_S class (b) and LI T17 removed the declared allergens from the LI marker, so T_LI is the first LI-list preservative elsewhere on the list, or `none_visible` (§11.11: `schwarzkopf-taft-foehn-spray-volumen-halt-3`, Parfum r6, Benzyl Alcohol r8 ⇒ T_LI `none_visible`).

T_LI keeps LI §3.1.1 clauses 1–6 **unchanged**, including clause 5 (implausible marker cannot disqualify) and clause 6 (vacuous marker cannot qualify). **Reading of clause 6 closed set (b) `[RG-1]`:** an essential or botanical fragrance oil declared at or after `Parfum`/`Fragrance`, or among the declared allergens, is a fragrance declaration; the same oil above `Parfum` is outside the set (lexicon §0.2). **Reading of clause 5 (implausible marker) `[RG4-19]`:** a T_LI with cationic conditioners or cationic polymers listed below it is **plausible** — PH1-13 already reads those cationics below T_LI down to the yardstick (D6 limb 2), so their position says nothing against the marker. T_LI is `implausible` only when it sits above an oil, ester or fatty-alcohol base that no rule reads below T_LI (`schwarzkopf-professional-osis-curl-retouch`: Phenoxyethanol r4 above Stearamidopropyl Dimethylamine r6 and Behentrimonium Chloride r7, with Cetearyl Alcohol r3 above it ⇒ `plausible`; the route is `leave_in` either way).

### 3.4 Below-marker candidates `[Mask E13; LI §3.1.1 clause 3 reused]`

A species at or below T_S never makes the marker implausible and earns nothing. Where crediting it would change a value, the record carries `candidate_below_tail` (species, rank, marker, marker rank) and routes to review. A species that would change nothing is recorded as E1 only. Cohort (node): 17 of 70 Styling records carry at least one value-changing candidate (e.g. `balea-locken-revitalizing-spray`: Coconut r13 + Shea r14 behind Sodium Benzoate r4 would turn `light` into `rich`).

### 3.5 Boundary position `[Mask E1 reused]`

A load-bearing member at `T_S − 1` counts normally but caps that value's confidence at `moderate`.

---

## 4. The S0 routing gate (lane step 3)

### 4.0 Outputs

`route` ∈ `styling · leave_in · oil · heat_protectant · dry_shampoo · provisional_boundary`. A `provisional_boundary` carries exactly one `boundary_reason`:

| `boundary_reason` | Meaning | Remedy |
|---|---|---|
| `evidence_gate` | A step-1 input is missing (no C1/C2 promise, no directions, incomplete INCI) | evidence work (a fresh capture), never a ruling — Mask's `insufficient_information` distinction |
| `claim_conflict` | Two C1/C2 sources disagree on the lead promise | review |
| `formula_conflict` | T18 tier 2: a same-market INCI set conflict, incl. two INCIs under one GTIN (§2.2 A2), that does not reach tier-1 convergence | a C1 pack capture, or a capture set that reaches T18 tier 1 |
| `mixed_promise` | Step 2 `mixed` after tie precedence (§4.2), D7 did not fire | review |
| `li_g0_provisional` | LI G0's own `provisional_boundary` (LI §2.3.2 clauses 2/4) | review |
| `pending_leave_in_addendum` | The PH1-16 hold (§4.5): Styling S0 says `styling`, the locked Leave-in G0 would also claim the product | released by the Leave-in addendum |

Every `provisional_boundary` **stays in its current category** and routes to review (charter §5).

**One reason per record `[RG4-21]`.** When two blocks apply to one record, `formula_conflict` wins (it is released only by a capture that settles the formula); the other block is listed as a review trigger (e.g. `s0_evidence_gate` for missing directions). `cantu-shea-butter-extra-hold-edge-stay-gel` (two INCI lists and no directions) ⇒ `provisional_boundary (formula_conflict)` + trigger `s0_evidence_gate`.

### 4.1 Step 1 — Evidence gate

S0 runs only when all three hold:
1. a **C1/C2 primary promise** (pack, manufacturer DE page, house brand on its own retailer page, or `C2_cross_market_verified`): **a C1/C2 promise line, or a C1/C2 confirmed name whose job words name exactly one job** (the §4.2 step-1 test) `[R4-Q1]`. A name with several job words, or none, does not meet it; a retailer title — including its product-type noun under the R3-Q2 fallback — never does. When the name alone meets input 1 (no C1/C2 promise line), the name is the lead promise, the claim-keyed fields take their no-claim values (hold `null` „Halt: keine Herstellerangabe", finish and flexibility `unbekannt`, unless a C1/C2 hold, finish or flexibility line is captured) and the record carries `claim_authority_gap`. Cases (round-4 freeze): `american-crew-fiber` (C2 brand-page name „FIBER": one product noun, molding) ⇒ input 1 met ⇒ committed `molding`, recommendable, no hold or finish shown; `kerastase-curl-manifesto-refresh-absolu` (C1 „CURL MANIFESTO REFRESH ABSOLU Second day curl refreshing spray": curl + refreshing name the same job, curl revival) ⇒ input 1 met ⇒ `leave_in`; `langhaarmaedchen-lockenspray-halt-sprungkraft` (C1 „LOCKENHALT SPRAY Halt & Sprungkraft": curl, hold and Sprungkraft — several jobs) ⇒ not met; `american-crew-pomade` (C2 „Styling Pomade": one job) ⇒ met, but blocked on input 2 (P4-1); `redken-styling-beach-spray` (C1 „BEACH SPRAY BEACHY TEXTURE SPRAY": one job, texture, R4-Q2) ⇒ met, but blocked on input 2 (paraphrased directions); `living-proof-full-dry-volume-texture-spray` (retailer title only) ⇒ not met;
2. **captured directions** (any G1 tier, verbatim; a page outside the G1 hierarchy — a C5 non-German manufacturer page — is not captured directions, §2.2 A14 `[P4-1]`);
3. a **complete INCI** with `identity_status` `verified` or `verified_with_minor_source_difference`.

Any input missing ⇒ `provisional_boundary (evidence_gate)`; two C1/C2 sources leading with different promise classes ⇒ `provisional_boundary (claim_conflict)`; a formula conflict ⇒ `provisional_boundary (formula_conflict)`.

**Tail-only INCI defects `[RG2-24, P2-3]`.** An INCI defect confined to the tail — at or below T_S: a truncation inside the allergen block, a split fragrance or oil token, a missing separator in the fragrance block — **does not block S0**; the record carries review `inci_tail_defect` and input 3 counts as met. A defect above T_S blocks (`evidence_gate`). The S0 packet carries `identity.inci_flags`, so a lane sees the defect and its position (§10.2). Round-2 cases: `jean-und-len-sea-salt-spray` (allergen block continues past the capture), `balea-locken-revitalizing-spray` („CITRONELLOL SODIUM CHLORIDE" r15, T_S Sodium Benzoate r4), `cantu-lockencreme-kokos` („SIMMONDSIA CHINENSIS (JOJOBA)" r18 / „SEED OIL" r19, T_S Parfum r5; re-joined by the round-3 freeze, P2-4) — none blocks. **Only incompleteness blocks `[RG3-15, P3-3]`:** above T_S, S0 is blocked by a truncation, a missing separator that merges **two** INCI names, or an unreadable token. A missing space or slash **inside one** INCI name that reads as exactly one known name („PerseaGratissima Oil", „IsostearylPalmitate", „VPVA COPOLYMER") is a transcription artifact the freeze repairs (P2-4 pattern: as-written kept, flagged with its rank; lexicon v0.4 §0.4 item 3); it never blocks (round 3: `john-frieda-frizz-ease-zauberformel-seiden-finish-creme` stopped in lane B on „PerseaGratissima Oil" r3; it passes the gate). **Item-count mismatch flags `[RG3-22, P3-6]`:** the freeze resolves every „capture_item_count_N_vs_split_M" flag against its source, or records the missing or extra item and its rank, so a lane can tell whether the gap sits above T_S (round 3: `syoss-hitzeschutzspray-keratin-heat`, `taft-styling-paste-forming-halt-5plus`, `got2b-spruehkleber-freeze-haarlack`). `neqi-diamond-glass-styling-spray-all` is **not** a tail-defect case: the re-check found its list complete (pack and neqi-hair.com agree, 14 items, ending „Glycerin"), so the truncation flag is withdrawn and nothing blocks it `[R2-H4]` (§4.6; §13.2 item 2).

**Leave-on and rinse-out directions `[RG2-20]`.** When the directions offer both a leave-on and a rinse-out use, the **first-named use decides the category**; the other use is recorded only (PH1-7: one product = one route). E.g. `redken-one-united-all-in-one-multi-benefit-treatment` („1. Variante: … Nicht ausspülen" first) ⇒ the leave-on use decides; `guhl-locken-pflege-und-styling-3in1` names „Spülung: … ausspülen" first (pack back „3in1 ANWENDUNG: 1 - Spülung … 2 - Kur … 3 - Lockencreme"), and R2-H2 rules it a care product (below; §4.6).

**A care cream that can also be left in stays care `[R2-H2]`.** A care product (conditioner, mask, Kur) whose directions also offer a leave-in curl-cream use is a care cream left in — a Leave-in-type use — and **stays in its current category**; it is not a Styling S0 route. Only a fixative-class polymer (lexicon FX-1…FX-4) above the tail or the D7 middle band (§4.3 3.4 (i)) makes such a product Styling. The leave-in use is recorded for the Leave-in addendum (PH1-16). Signals that identify the care product: the first-named use is rinse-out (RG2-20), the manufacturer shelves it with conditioners/masks, and the hold is stated to come from care, not fixation. Case: `guhl-locken-pflege-und-styling-3in1` (pack back directions start with Spülung; guhl.com files it under „Spülungen, Kuren & Seren" and says „Der Halt entsteht dabei durch Pflege, nicht durch Fixierung."; no fixative-class polymer) ⇒ care, stays in its current category. S0 never emits a Styling route or profile for it: the record stays `provisional_boundary` (which keeps a product in its current category, §4.0) with the R2-H2 reading in its rationale (§4.6, §11.15).

**Owner-named routes: a ruled destination is not permission to emit a profile.** A product whose route an owner ruling names (§4.6 registry) records that route as `ruled_route`. The ruling overrides **only promise-evidence failures** — the questions an owner ruling actually answers (which category the product's promise belongs to):

| Overridden by an owner ruling | Cohort cases |
|---|---|
| (a) input 1 missing: **no C1/C2 primary promise** (C3/C5 claims only) | Color Wow Dream Coat (D2-named); John Frieda Lockencreme Traumlocken (D7-named) — its (a) basis is gone since a C1 line was captured, see (d). *Bumble and bumble Brilliantine is no longer a case `[R4-H (4)]`:* its C1 pack name „brilliantine" names one job (shine) and meets input 1 under R4-Q1 |
| (b) `claim_conflict`: two C1/C2 sources lead with different promise classes | Elnett Stylingspray 3 Tage Glatt (PH1-14) |
| (c) a step-2 `mixed` reading (§4.2) | NEQI Diamond Glass Ultimate: its explicit R6-Q1 owner route overrides the step-2 stop and routes held `smoothing_styler`; the former D3/T15 outcome is superseded (§4.6, §11.4) |
| (d) **a named D6/D7 border route where the letter differs `[R4-Q5]`**: a registry D6 or D7 row rules the route of the record it names, until the Leave-in addendum releases or re-rules it | Bali Curls Strong Hold Flaxseed Gel (D6: the letter's one-job hold name gives `li_function: no` ⇒ 3.4 (ii) ⇒ committed `gel`; the row holds it); Kevin.Murphy Killer.Curls (D7: the lead hold line gives `li_function: no` ⇒ 3.4 (ii); the row holds it as `curl_cream`); John Frieda Lockencreme Traumlocken (D7: its (a) basis is gone since a C1 line is captured, and the letter stops at LI G0 on the uncorroborated acrylates, FX-2c; the row holds it as `curl_cream`) |

In those four cases the product takes the ruled route (then the PH1-16 hold, §4.5, still applies); under (a) its claim-keyed fields take their no-claim values and it carries `claim_authority_gap`; every override carries `ruled_route_override`.

**Never overridden.** Input 2 (no directions), input 3 (incomplete INCI, or `identity_status` other than `verified` / `verified_with_minor_source_difference`), a formula conflict (§2.2 A2, LI T18 tier 2, PH1-8) and an identity conflict keep blocking value emission even for a named product: S0 emits `provisional_boundary` with that `boundary_reason` (`evidence_gate` or `formula_conflict`), the `ruled_route` is recorded as the destination once the block clears, and **no Styling value is emitted** (§4.7). Example: `syoss-curl-control-hydrating-spray` is ruled Leave-in (PH1-13) but stays blocked on its DE identity (dm.at INCI).

### 4.2 Step 2 — Promise class and the Leave-in-function tag

**Promise class** (structured judgment, C1/C2 only; the confirmed product name is C1/C2 positioning):

| Class | Anchor |
|---|---|
| `styling_result` | The lead promise is a visible styling result: shape/hold, curl or wave definition, volume, texture, sleekness, shine/finish (charter §2) — **including** reviving curls or a style between washes (the `refresher` job) |
| `care` | The lead promise is conditioning, repair or moisture |
| `heat_protection` | The lead promise is protection from heat (e.g. „Hitzeschutzspray") |
| `refresh` | The lead promise is **hair freshness / oil absorption / cleansing** between washes (the Dry Shampoo sense; charter §2 "refresh/cleansing (dry shampoo)") — never curl revival |
| `mixed` | The lead claim states two classes as **co-equal jobs** („Styling und Schutz in einem") **and** the tie-precedence rule below does not assign a class. A care or curl word **in the product name** never settles a care-vs-definition tie (R1-Q3 below; `[R1-H2]` retires the v0.1 „Pflegecreme" adjective example), and a secondary bullet („Hitzeschutz bis 220 °C" under a volume promise), an ingredient call-out („mit Arganöl") or an adjective on the result („gepflegte Locken", R2-Q1) does **not** make a promise mixed. **A C1/C2 promise covering both a care job and curl definition is always `mixed` (R1-Q3 as narrowed by R2-Q1, below).** A protection lead that names heat among other protections is `heat_protection`, not `mixed`; it is `mixed` only when it joins protection with a styling or care job `[RG2-19]` |

**Tie precedence (part of class assignment, applied before any `mixed` reading) `[charter §5 Dry Shampoo row; concept §2 S0 table; dry-shampoo/decision.md]`.** When the co-equal lead jobs are **refresh in the Dry Shampoo sense** (hair freshness, oil absorption, cleansing between washes) **and** a volume/texture styling result, the class is **`refresh`**, not `mixed`: the charter's Dry Shampoo boundary rule says "tie ⇒ Dry Shampoo", and the Dry Shampoo decision already owns volume/texture as a refresh product's styling direction (`styling_effect: volume_texture`), so such a product is a Dry Shampoo with a volume direction. **R8-Q1 (2026-10-14): a C1/C2 promise of refresh between washes in that Dry Shampoo sense alongside volume also establishes this tie when the confirmed name or first lead names volume first. Read the confirmed name and all C1/C2 promise lines for the tie before accepting the one-job name as the class; the refresh promise need not be in the same lead line.** L'Oréal Elvital Big Hair Day „Erfrischendes Volumen Spray“, with C1 „Bis zu 48h frischeres Haargefühl“, therefore has class `refresh` ⇒ 3.2 ⇒ `dry_shampoo` (volume direction; §4.6). The record keeps `promise_tie: refresh_plus_volume_texture` in its S0 rationale. Curl revival is never Dry Shampoo refresh (it is `styling_result`, the `refresher` job), so this rule never applies to a curl refresher. Every other co-equal pair stays `mixed`.

**Lead promise `[RG-5 as adjusted by R1-Q3; R1-T]`.** A packet may list several C1/C2 promise lines without marking the headline. The **lead promise** — the line that sets the class subject to tie precedence above — is picked in this order:
1. the **job word of the confirmed product name**, when it names exactly one (Lockencreme ⇒ definition; Hitzeschutzspray ⇒ heat protection; Finishing-Spray / Glanzspray ⇒ finish). **A name with two or more job words does not decide; go to step 2** `[R1-H1]` (Elnett „Stylingspray Hitzeschutz 3 Tage Glatt" ⇒ pack front „Hitze Styling-Spray, glatt 3 Tage, starker Halt" ⇒ styling result, as PH1-14 rules). **Product noun Puder/Powder `[R8-Q2]`:** Puder/Powder names `volume`, including Haarpuder and Volumenpuder; it never defaults to `hold_fix` or `texture`. Texture/grip promised in a C1/C2 line is an eligible secondary under §6.10. Redken „Powder Grip“ itself names two jobs — Powder = `volume`, Grip = `texture` — so step 2/3 decides the lead; other product nouns retain their mappings. **Counting the name's job words `[RG2-21, RG2-5]`:** the product-type noun (Haarspray, Gel, Wachs, Puder, Schaumfestiger) names the job when it is the only job noun, and a shine or strength qualifier in the same name is not a second job (`schwarzkopf-taft-haarspray-glanz-halt-4` „Haarspray Glanz Halt 4" ⇒ one job, hold, so `hairspray`, as §5 says); name job words that name the same job count as one (Curl + Defining + Shaper on a curl product; „Glanz-Spray … Silky Shine"). **Which name words are job words `[RG3-1]`:** a **curl or wave word** in the product's own name (Locken-, Curl, Curls, Wellen-, Wave/Waves) is a curl job word `[R7-Q2]` — definition, or refresh when the product revives curls; a **salt noun** (Salzspray, Sea Salt Spray) names texture; a **product noun used as a form in a compound with another product noun** (Wax Spray, Spray Wax, Sprühwachs, Gel-Spray) names the form, not a job — go to step 2/3. Round-3 outcomes (historical, subject to the R7 amendment above): `bali-curls-volumizing-curl-spray-refreshing` („Volumizing Curl Spray": two job words ⇒ first C2 line, revival ⇒ held `refresher`); `wellaflex-schaumfestiger-locken-und-wellen-starker-halt` (name has hold + definition; the C1 phrase „für schwungvolle, definierte Locken“ ⇒ primary `definition` under R7-Q1 ⇒ 3.4 (iii)); `kevin-murphy-killer-curls` (two job words ⇒ the C2 hold line leads ⇒ primary `hold_fix`; the route stays D7 `curl_cream`, held); `jean-und-len-sea-salt-spray` (salt noun ⇒ texture); `redken-wax-spray` (Wax Spray names the form ⇒ first C2 line „Texturierendes Sprühwachs" ⇒ texture ⇒ `texture_spray`, §5). **Product noun vs qualifier, also inside a compound `[R4-Q7]`:** a product-type noun in the name (Wachs/Wax, Paste, Clay, Gel, Pomade, Fiber, Haarspray) names the job (molding or hold); a **shine or strength qualifier** in the name (Glanz, Glow, Shine, Strong, Power) is the finish or the grade, **never a second job — also inside a compound** (as „Haarspray Glanz"). Two product nouns of the same job count once. A **true job word naming a different job** beside the product noun (Texture, Volume, Curl; Grip beside Powder) makes two jobs ⇒ the name decides nothing ⇒ the lead line decides (step 2/3). The RG3-1 form compound stays for a product noun joined with a dispensing-form noun (Wax Spray, Sprühwachs, Gel-Spray: the spray is the form). Cases: `schwarzkopf-taft-gel-wax-glanz-halt-2` („Gel Wax Glanz": Gel and Wax name one job, Glanz is the finish) ⇒ `molding`, primary `hold_fix`, finish `glänzend`, and its D8b flag is kept internally (R4-Q7; §7.5); `got2b-styling-paste-istyler-texture-clay` („Styling Paste … Texture Clay": Paste/Clay + the job word Texture ⇒ two jobs) ⇒ the C2 line „… ultra krassem Halt" leads ⇒ primary `hold_fix`, `texture` secondary; `balea-haarwachs-glow-wax` (Glow is a qualifier) ⇒ `hold_fix`. **Look names and shine nouns `[R4-Q2]`:** „Beach Waves", „Strandwellen", „beachy" name the **salt-spray texture look** ⇒ `texture` (`li_function: no`), not curl definition; RG3-1's wave nouns cover curl/wave definition and revival products („Wellen-Spray", „Wellenauffrischer"), not beach-look names (`alverde-beach-waves-spray-seaside-balance` ⇒ texture ⇒ 3.4 (ii) ⇒ committed `salt_spray`). „Glaze", „Topcoat" and „Gloss" are shine job nouns (with the R2-Q3 list: Glanz, Shine, Gloss, Finish) ⇒ `syoss-glaze-topcoat` („Glaze Topcoat": one job, shine; R3-Q3 lets the one-job name win) ⇒ `styling_result` ⇒ 3.5 ⇒ committed `shine_finish`. Slogans in a name („Big Hair Day", „Salon Blow-Out") name no job and are skipped (RG2-17). **Shop-title fallback `[R3-Q2]`:** the confirmed name is the pack name (C1) or the brand's German-page name (C2). When **neither** shows the product name, the **product-type noun of the retailer's product title** (Hitzeschutzspray, Öl, Serum, Haarspray, Gel, Wachs …; dm „syoss **Hitzeschutzspray** Keratin") serves as the confirmed-name job word for this step and for D2 (§4.3 3.1), with review `name_from_retailer_title`, **provided the type noun agrees across the retailer titles captured `[R3-H4 (d)]`** — two titles with different type nouns (e.g. „Hitzeschutzspray" vs „Haarspray") give no fallback: the name decides nothing, the lead falls to steps 2–3, review `retailer_title_type_noun_conflict` (R1-H1's two-job-word rule covers two nouns in **one** name). **Every other word of a retailer title never counts** (R2-H1, the C3 rule): qualifiers, variant names, ingredient words and any second noun stay C3 traces. The real pack names of the products concerned are captured (29 confirmed names were retailer-title only in round 3, node, `round-3/diff.json` `packet_checks.confirmed_name_tier_C3`; P3-7, R3-T), and a captured pack or brand-page name replaces the fallback (the round-4 freeze replaces it from `capture/packnames-r7.json` wherever A3/A11 confirm a name, §4.6). Cases: `syoss-hitzeschutzspray-keratin-heat` ⇒ heat protection ⇒ 3.3 (§11.21); `loreal-elvital-haaroel-oel-magique-alle-haartypen`, `john-frieda-haarserum-frizz-ease-der-baendiger`, `garnier-fructis-sleek-and-stay-heat-activated-serum` ⇒ „Öl"/„Serum" ⇒ D2 by the letter (§4.6);
2. else the **first C1 pack-front line** — the first C1 line with `position: pack_front` `[P4-3]`; where no C1 line carries a position (`unknown`), the first C1 line in packet order, as in rounds 1–4 `[R4-H (3)]`;
3. else the **first C2 line** (packet order).

**R7 job reading (R7-F, R7-Q1, R7-Q2).** If the eligible lead supplies no primary-vocabulary job but a later eligible C1/C2 promise line does, that line's first job is primary in the §4.2 order; only when all such lines supply none is `primary_job: null` emitted (§6.10). An adjective before a job phrase is not a separate job: Wellaflex „für schwungvolle, definierte Locken“ promises `definition`, not an earlier volume job. A curl/wave word in the product's own confirmed name supplies `definition` („Killer.Curls“, „Curl Cream“, „Lockencreme“, „Locken & Wellen“), subject to the existing Beach Waves texture exception and explicit curl-revival `refresh` reading. In descriptive sentences, definition needs an explicit promise to define/shape curls or waves; „Definition“/„Define“ for a non-curl look never supplies it (Bali Gents Sea Salt Spray „Griffigkeit und Definition“; Balea Leave-in Spray Sea Salt Care & Define). This does not bypass the one-job name test, R4-Q7 qualifiers, care + definition tie, formula checks or registry rulings.

**When the name alone meets the gate `[R4-Q1]`** (§4.1 input 1, no C1/C2 promise line), the name's one job is the lead and steps 2–3 have nothing to read.

**Line vs sentence `[RG3-4]`.** The lead is the **first sentence of the chosen line that states a job**; later sentences of the same line are secondary claims. `wella-eimi-smooth-thermal-image`: the first C2 line „Unser EIMI Thermal Image 2-Phasen-Spray schützt Ihr Haar bei der Verwendung von heißen Geräten. Phase 1 pflegt das Haar und schützt es vor Hitze bis zu 220°C. Phase 2 glättet Ihr Haar …" ⇒ lead = its first sentence, protection from heat ⇒ `heat_protection` ⇒ 3.3 (§11.20).
