**A line with no job word is skipped `[RG2-17, R7-F]`:** at steps 2 and 3, a lead line that states no job word („Haarwachs-Stift für trendige Styles", „Praktische Anwendung") is passed over and the next C1/C2 line with a job word is the lead (`balea-haarwachs-styling-stick`, sealed round-2 slot 2: past „Haarwachs-Stift für trendige Styles" and „Praktische Anwendung" to „Für ein glattes & glänzendes Finish", the reading both lanes took in round 2 ⇒ `shine_finish`). **The name step comes first `[R2-H3]`:** RG2-17 applies only at steps 2 and 3; when the confirmed name names exactly one job (step 1, RG2-21), the name decides and later lines with a more specific job word are secondaries. `balea-haarwachs-styling-stick` (pack name „Styling WAX STICK", dm title „Haarwachs Styling Stick": one product-type noun, Wachs/Wax) ⇒ lead = molding ⇒ **`molding`**, not `shine_finish`; „glattes, glänzendes Finish", „Ideal für den Sleek-Look" and „auch gegen Babyhaare" are secondary jobs and uses (§4.6, §11.16).

The lead is **co-equal (`mixed`)** when it joins two classes itself with „und", „&", „+" or a „2in1/3in1" that names both (GUHL „Pflege & Styling 2in1/3in1"). **A one-job name wins over a lead line that joins that job with care `[R3-Q3]`** (same logic as R2-H3): when step 1 decides — the confirmed name names exactly one job — a care word joined to that job in a later or co-equal C1/C2 line is a call-out (SFS-15), not a second class. `schwarzkopf-taft-texturspray-aloe-boost`: name „Texturspray"; the only C1 line „Textur und Pflege für mühelose Undone Looks und natürlich aussehende Wellen" ⇒ `styling_result` ⇒ Styling `texture_spray` (§11.18). `mixed` stays for **names that themselves name both jobs** („Pflege & Styling 2in1/3in1"). R1-Q3 / R2-Q1 are unchanged: a C1/C2 promise covering a care job **and curl definition** is `mixed` whatever the name (next paragraph). **What „names that name both jobs" means `[R4-Q6, RG4-8]`:** names that join care and styling as the product's two jobs („Pflege & Styling 2in1/3in1"). A care word beside a styling product noun („Finishing Spray Glow & Care") is a call-out, not a second job: `balea-finishing-spray-glow-und-care` ⇒ one job, finish/shine ⇒ committed `shine_finish`. **R1-Q3 wins over R3-Q3:** a one-job name does not break a care + curl-definition tie — `john-frieda-traumlocken-belebender-wellenauffrischer` (name: refresh; line: care + definition) stays `mixed` ⇒ manual review. Packet side: each promise line carries its `position` (`pack_front`, `headline`, `bullet`, `body` or `unknown`, P4-3; §2.2 A3).

**Care + curl definition ⇒ `mixed`, whatever the name `[R1-Q3]`.** A product whose C1/C2 promise covers **both care and curl definition** is `mixed` ⇒ manual review. **The product name never breaks that tie** in either direction: a care noun in the name (Pflege-Spray, Kur, Balsam) does not make the product `care`, and a curl noun does not make it `styling_result`. Examples: Garnier Fructis Locken Methode Feuchtigkeitsboost Pflege-Spray („Feuchtigkeit und definierte Locken") ⇒ `mixed` (review); GUHL Locken Pflege & Styling 2in1 / 3in1 ⇒ `mixed` (review). Each such product is decided by hand — GUHL 3in1 was: a care product, not Styling, staying in its current category `[R2-H2]` (§4.1). The lead-promise order above still picks the headline for every other purpose (subtype job, `primary_job`, §6.10). **PH1-12 is unchanged:** for a `mixed` product the D7 middle-band test runs first (stop paragraph below).

**What counts as each half `[R2-Q1, RG2-1]`** (narrows R1-H2; SFS-15 governs adjectives and call-outs):
- **Care half = a care verb or care noun that states a job `[R4-Q3]`** (the list is closed): **pflegt / Pflege / Care, Kur, Balsam, Treatment, Feuchtigkeit (spendet Feuchtigkeit / moisturises; „Feuchtigkeits-"), nährt / nourishes, repariert / repairs, „stärkt" / strengthens as a verb, entwirrt / detangles („Detangling")** — in a C1/C2 promise line or in the confirmed name. **Not** the care half: a **result phrase** describing the hair („strong hair", „gesunde Locken", „gepflegte Locken") and a **product-line prefix** („Nutri-" in „Nutricurls"). Cases: `aussie-leave-in-creme-work-that-curl` (C1 „5 days of curl definition (plus 48-hour frizz protection) and strong hair*": „strong hair" is a result phrase ⇒ no care half ⇒ not `mixed` ⇒ `leave_in`); `redken-one-united-all-in-one-multi-benefit-treatment` („Treatment" in the name is a care noun ⇒ the care class stays, `leave_in`); `wella-eimi-nutricurls-fresh-up` and `wella-eimi-smooth-perfect-me` (the „Nutri-" prefix is not care; „Balsam-Lotion" in a name is the care noun Balsam) — their routes do not move. **Not** the care half either: an adjective describing the styled result („gepflegte Locken", „geschmeidige Locken"), a feel line, or an ingredient call-out („mit Squalan und Jojoba-Öl angereichert"). **Feel lines never count `[R2-H5]`**, whatever their position: „Verbesserte Geschmeidigkeit", „für ein geschmeidiges Haargefühl", „Geschmeidigkeit und verbesserte Kämmbarkeit" describe how the hair feels, not a promised care job (§11.2). A care job must also be promised **at C1/C2**: a care word found only in retailer text (C3) is not the care half `[R2-H1]`. R1-H2's „a care word in the name or an adjective" now reads „a care word in the name". **Care product nouns and moisture-locking `[RG3-12]`:** a care product noun inside a C2 descriptor line („Definierende Pflegecreme", „Pflegespray") is **not** a care job unless it is the confirmed name; moisture-locking („schließt Feuchtigkeit ein") **is** the moisturise care job. Round-3 cases: `maria-nila-curlicue-cream` (C2 „Definierende Pflegecreme …", confirmed name „Curlicue Cream") ⇒ no care job ⇒ `styling_result` (§11.3); `wella-eimi-nutricurls-curl-shaper` (moisture-locking + definition) ⇒ `mixed` ⇒ D7 first (PH1-12) ⇒ held `curl_cream`.
- **Definition half `[R6-T (3), R7-Q2]` = an explicit promise to define or shape curls or waves in a descriptive sentence:** „definiert Locken“, „Definition der Locken“, or a curl/wave noun with a shaping or setting verb (`[RG2-5]` below). **Reviving or reactivating curls is refresh, not definition** (the `refresher` job, `styling_result`). **A curl/wave word in the product's own confirmed name is the definition half `[RG4-22, R7-Q2]`; „Definition“/„Define“ for a non-curl look is not**, as a care word in the name is the care half (`hask-lockencreme-curl-care` „curls defining cream"; its route is ruled by its registry row, §4.6).
- When the promise is not `mixed` on this reading, the existing border rules decide on the ingredients: D7 (3.4 (i)), LI G0 / T15 (3.4 (iii)) and D6 with the PH1-13 effective-amount test. Examples (R2-Q1): `redken-refreshing-curl-mist` („REVITALISIERT UND REAKTIVIERT LOCKEN" and „Für langanhaltend gestylte und gepflegte Locken": revival + a result adjective ⇒ `styling_result`) ⇒ D6 ⇒ Styling `refresher`, held (§4.6, §11.13); `wella-eimi-nutricurls-fresh-up` — **corrected by R2-H1:** its promise lines are on wella.com/professional/de-DE (C2), where the product is „EIMI Fresh Up"; „Curl Refreshing Detangling Spray" and „entwirrend" are retailer text only (C3), so no care job is promised at C1/C2 and the promise („Frische deine Locken auf und erhalte Definition, Glanz und Anti-Frizz-Effekt.") is **not** a care + definition tie; the border ingredient checks (D6 / T15) decide in round 3 (§4.6, §11.14). Unchanged: Garnier Locken Methode Pflege-Spray („Feuchtigkeit und definierte Locken") and GUHL 2in1 stay `mixed`; GUHL 3in1 is ruled a care product by R2-H2 (§4.1) and stays in its current category. **R8-H / R7-Q2:** Moroccanoil „Glättende Creme (Smoothing Lotion)“ (`moroccanoil-glaettende-creme-smoothing-lotion`) promises „Definition“ for „krauses und widerspenstiges Haar“ and separately says „Ideal für lockiges Haar“; that hair-type sentence does not turn the earlier generic definition into an explicit curl-definition promise. There is no care + definition tie. Its damp-hair smoothing/care promise goes through the existing border rules (3.4 (iii), LI G0/T15) ⇒ `leave_in` (§4.6).

**Leave-in-function tag** (input to step 3.4 (ii)): `li_function: yes` when the C1/C2 lead promise states one of LI §2.1's own functions (the confirmed name counts only where it **is** the lead, §4.2 step 1 or §4.1 input 1; `[R4-Q5]` deletes v0.4's „or confirmed name") — **conditioning/care, detangling, smoothing/anti-frizz, curl support (definition or revival of curls/waves), heat-styling support** — and `no` when it states only hold, volume, texture, molding/shaping, or shine/finish. **A curl or wave noun with a shaping or setting verb states curl definition `[RG2-5]`** („Locken formen", „Curl Shaper", „Styling-Spray für Locken über Nacht", „Wellen über Nacht") ⇒ `li_function: yes`, primary `definition` (§6.10); shaping without a curl/wave noun is `hold_fix` (`li_function: no`). **`li_function` is what scopes the Leave-in border check `[PH2-1]`** (step 3.4 (ii)). **It reads the lead promise only `[RG3-19]`:** a later C1/C2 line naming a Leave-in function (anti-frizz, care) never sets it.

**Anti-frizz on dry, finished hair is the shine-finish job `[R1-Q2, RG-6]`.** Flyaway or frizz control promised for **dry, styled hair** is part of the `shine_finish` job (§5 anchor „shine, flyaway control on dry hair") ⇒ `li_function: no`, so 3.4 (ii) routes it to `styling` directly and the PH1-16 hold does not apply (§4.5). The product's **main directions** decide („Nach dem Styling … auf das trockene Haar sprühen"); a **side tip** („bei krausem Haar vorher ins handtuchtrockene Haar") does not. Anti-frizz promised **for damp hair**, or **sold as care**, keeps `li_function: yes` and the full Leave-in check. Missing directions cannot establish the dry-hair reading: the product stops at the evidence gate (§4.1). Outcomes: Balea Glow & Shine Finishing Spray and Feschi Smooth & Shine ⇒ `shine_finish`, not held (Feschi once its evidence gate passes); Balea Finishing Stick stopped at the evidence gate in v0.2 (no directions); the round-2 capture carries C1 pack directions, so it now reads `shine_finish` (§4.6). A care-ingredient test was considered and set aside by the owner (R1-Q2). **Edges, baby hair and brows `[RG4-26]`:** sleeking baby hair, edges or brows on dry hair is a styling look ⇒ `li_function: no` ⇒ 3.4 (ii) (`bali-curls-sleek-stick-brows-and-edges` „Sleek Stick Brows & Edges" ⇒ committed `gel` via 3.4 (ii); round 4 lane A reached the same route via 3.4 (iii) `excluded_styling_first`).

**`mixed` stop, with the PH1-12 / R6-Q1 exceptions.** The stop applies to the combinations left after tie precedence. If the class is `mixed`, run the D7 middle-band test (step 3.4 (i)) and the R6-Q1 heat-activated smoothing-film test (step 3.4 (i′)) **before** stopping. If either fires ⇒ `styling`, subtype `curl_cream` or `smoothing_styler`, even with a care or heat co-promise. Otherwise ⇒ `provisional_boundary (mixed_promise)`. `[PH1-12, R3-Q4, R4-Q4, R6-Q1]`

**Brand-line words are not job words `[R6-Q1, R7-Q3]`.** Every brand line or collection name used across several products of a brand is excluded, not only the named examples: „Frizz Ease“, „Traumlocken“, „Volume Lift“, „Diamond Sleek“, „Glow & Shine“, „Nutricurls“ … never supplies a job at the name step, primary-job step or a secondary-job candidate. Only the product's own name words or an explicit C1/C2 job promise count: in „Traumlocken Lockencreme“, `Lockencreme` supplies definition, not `Traumlocken`.

### 4.3 Step 3 — Pair rules, in fixed order

The charter's "exactly one pair rule applies" is implemented as an ordered selection, so a product with a secondary heat claim and an aqueous formula is not tested twice. `[operational]`

**3.1 Oil — name rule `[D2]`, scoped to shine and anti-frizz products.** **Scope:** this step runs **only** when the C1/C2 lead promise is shine, finish, anti-frizz/flyaway control or a smoothing finish (class `styling_result` with a shine, sleek or frizz-control lead; D2 rules "the Oil ↔ Styling boundary **for shine and anti-frizz products**"). Any other lead promise skips 3.1, whatever the name: a protection-first „Serum" (class `heat_protection`) goes on to 3.3 (Heat Protectant), a curl-definition or care-led „Serum" goes on to 3.4/3.6. Within scope: sold as „Öl" or „Serum" (confirmed C1/C2 name — or, when no C1/C2 name exists, the product-type noun of the retailer title under the §4.2 shop-title fallback, review `name_from_retailer_title` `[R3-Q2]`) ⇒ `oil`, whatever the formula. Exception: a water-based „Serum" that is really a leave-in conditioner ⇒ go to 3.4 (LI G0 decides). **Exception test `[RG-15, as amended by R2-Q2 / RG2-2]`:** `Aqua` above T_S (aqueous, lexicon §0.3) **and** either an LGN pair above T_LI **or** a cationic conditioner or cationic polymer (lexicon CA-1…CA-5) above T_LI **or below it down to — not including — the first pH adjuster (lexicon PH-1) listed after T_LI** (fallback `Parfum`; no yardstick ⇒ review `care_yardstick_missing`) — the D6 dose rule of PH1-13 ⇒ go to **3.4 (iii)** (LI G0 decides), skipping 3.4 (ii) even when the promise is shine-only (`li_function: no`); trigger `water_based_serum_exception` (plus `care_decided_below_preservative` when the cationic sits below T_LI). Otherwise a „Serum" in scope is `oil`; real oil and silicone serums stay Oil by name (D2). Cases: `balea-professional-haarserum-glow-und-shine` (Behentrimonium Chloride r5 above T_LI ⇒ `leave_in` under both readings); `balea-haarserum-anti-frizz-glatt-und-glossy` (R2-Q2: Aqua r1, Cetearyl Alcohol r3, Behentrimonium Chloride r12 just below T_LI Ethylhexylglycerin r11 and above the yardstick Citric Acid r20; damp-hair directions; „ohne Silikone" ⇒ exception ⇒ 3.4 (iii) ⇒ `leave_in`, matching the registry, §4.6). Every other shine/finish/anti-frizz name (Glanzspray, Shine Spray, Finishing Spray, Brilliantine, Anti-Frizz-Spray) continues.

**3.2 Dry Shampoo `[charter §5; dry-shampoo/decision.md]`.** Class `refresh` — including a refresh + volume/texture tie, which §4.2's tie precedence already assigned to `refresh` — **or** directions/promise positioned on oil absorption or cleansing ⇒ `dry_shampoo`. Texture/grip with no refresh positioning ⇒ continue. The formula (starch powder, silica) never decides this boundary.

**3.3 Heat Protectant `[charter §5; heat-protectant/decision.md]`.** Class `heat_protection` ⇒ `heat_protectant` (protection-first, even with a fixative: got2b Schutzengel, VP/VA r2). A protection lead that names heat among other protections (Feuchtigkeit, Umwelt) is class `heat_protection` (§4.2, `[RG2-19]`): `osis-super-shield` („multifunktionales Schutzspray … vor Umweltbelastungen, Hitze und Feuchtigkeit zu schützen") ⇒ `heat_protectant`. A heat claim that is **not** the lead promise does not select this pair; it is captured and handled by PH1-15 (§6.7).

**3.4 Leave-in** — class `styling_result` or `care`, formula **aqueous** (lexicon §0.3). Sub-steps in order:

- **(i) D7 middle band `[D7, PH1-12; extended by R3-Q4 and R4-Q4]`.** Limbs 1 and 2 and the curl sale limb 3 ⇒ `styling` `curl_cream`; limb 1 and the straightening sale limb 3′ ⇒ `styling` `smoothing_styler`, **whatever limb 2 reads** (R4-Q4):
  1. a fixative-class polymer (lexicon FX-1…FX-4) **above the tail, read against T_S `[R3-H4 (e)]`** (R3-Q4: „above the tail") — generic Acrylates Copolymer only when FX-2c corroborated; an uncorroborated one cannot establish this limb (review);
  2. a **substantive conditioning system** — LI G0's conditioning half reads not thin (LI COND ≥ `moderate`, the T15 reading). **Never the R2 care test** (PH1-13: R2 "is not to be used as the T15 thin test");
  **A vacuous T_LI does not block limbs 1 and 2 `[R3-H4 (e)]`:** limb 1 reads T_S and limb 2 reads LI COND, so neither waits on T_LI (`kms-thermashape-straightening-creme`: T_LI Benzoic Acid r16 vacuous, T_S Benzoic Acid r16 plausible; VP/VA r5 above it and LI COND not thin ⇒ both limbs met, as R3-Q4 names it). `tail_marker_vacuous` still fires when another border rule on the path reads T_LI (PH1-16, §8);
  3. **curl sale ⇒ subtype `curl_cream`:** sold as a curl or styling cream or balm („Lockencreme", „Curl Cream", „Styling Cream/Balm … Locken"; also „Curl Shaper", „Defining Cream", „Gel Cream" on a curl product `[RG3-21]`, e.g. `wella-eimi-nutricurls-curl-shaper` „Curl Shaper (Defining Gel Cream)"). A curl cream **without** a fixative stays under T15 ⇒ Leave-in, even when named „Lockencreme" (Cantu Kokos, HASK, Bali Hydrating);
  3′. **straightening sale ⇒ subtype `smoothing_styler` `[R3-Q4, R4-Q4]`:** sold as a straightening/smoothing styler („Straightening Creme", „Glättungscreme", „Glattspray", a lead „zur Glättung …"). **Since R4-Q4 this limb needs limb 1 only — a fixative-class polymer above T_S — and fires whatever the conditioning reads** (limb 2 substantive or thin), so a fixative-led smoothing spray with thin care is covered too. Owner's reason: a straightening product routed as a care Leave-in could be recommended to curly users who do not want their curls straightened. Like the curl limb, it runs **before the mixed stop** (PH1-12) and the route is **held** under PH1-16 / PH2-2 until the Leave-in addendum, **which must carry this extension**. Cases: `kms-thermashape-straightening-creme` (Isoamyl Laurate r2, Triheptanoin r3 conditioning base, VP/VA Copolymer r5 above T_S Benzoic Acid r16; C2 „ZUR GLÄTTUNG VON LOCKEN MIT GLÄTTEISEN ODER FÖHN") ⇒ held `smoothing_styler` (§11.17); **`wella-professionals-eimi-flowing-form` ⇒ held `smoothing_styler` `[R3-H2]`** (C2 name „Glättungscreme / Anti-Frizz Smoothing Balm"; PVP r2, Polyquaternium-37 r3, Behentrimonium Chloride r5, Guar HPTC; directions blow-dry with a brush + flat iron; „Haltegrad: 2"). R3-H2 withdraws R3-Q4's sentence „Wella Flowing Form ⇒ review"; **`balea-glattspray-anti-frizz` ⇒ held `smoothing_styler` `[R4-Q4]`** (house-brand C2 „Glattspray für die Haare", „Wirkt Frizz entgegen"; VP/VA Copolymer r3 above T_S Parfum r7; thin care — Cetrimonium Chloride r5 alone, no fatty alcohol; directions: towel-dried hair, optional blow-dry with a paddle brush and flat iron; round 4: both lanes `li_g0_provisional`, the gap PH2-1 had accepted for Flowing Form; held because the Leave-in letter would claim it, same owner's reason as R3-Q4); products with a leading fixative and care but **no** straightening/smoothing sale keep their current route.
  Scope: the curl limb 3 needs both limbs 1 and 2 (the middle band); the straightening limb 3′ needs limb 1 only (R4-Q4). RG3-5's proposed T15 wording for smoothing creams (supporting fixative ⇒ `in_category`) is **not** adopted; R3-Q4 supersedes it.
- **(i′) Heat-activated smoothing-film blow-out sprays and lotions `[R6-Q1]`.** Before LI G0/D6, route an **aqueous** blow-out or smoothing spray/lotion to `styling` `smoothing_styler` when all hold: (1) its main active is a heat-activated smoothing-film former — a member of the enumerated SI-1 heat-activated smoothing-film subset, **Polysilicone-29 or Silicone Quaternium-18 only** (lexicon v0.5 §1B; other SI-1 members do not satisfy this limb); (2) its care actives are only at or after the first preservative (so it has no effective pre-preservative care system). The lead smoothing-film former is not itself counted as disqualifying care in this new test; all other care actives are. Read the first preservative/concentration-capped LI cutoff (lexicon §0.2), not the earlier perfume marker T_S; existing D6/R2 care remains unchanged; (3) no FX-1…FX-4 fixative-class polymer is present. This is the subtype's original heat-activated coating job, not an inference from a brand line. The route is held under §4.5 / PH1-16 / PH2-2 until the Leave-in addendum carries it. The registry's three owner-named R6-Q1 outcomes include NEQI Ultimate as an explicit override: it retains FX-1 VP/Methacrylamide/Vinyl Imidazole Copolymer r8 and is not evidence for relaxing limb (3). **R6-Q1 replaces D3's and R2-Q4's NEQI Leave-in outcomes.**
- **(ii) Border scope: the Leave-in check applies only where the promise overlaps Leave-in's own definition `[PH2-1]`.** Class `styling_result` with `li_function: no` — the C1/C2 promise is volume, hold, texture, shaping/molding or shine ⇒ `styling` **directly, with no Leave-in check** (no T15, D6 or D7). Leave-in's own definition (LI §2.1: care, detangling, smoothing/anti-frizz, curl support, blow-dry/heat support) does not cover such a product, whatever conditioning the formula carries (e.g. Balea Föhnlotion Volume Effect and the 9 "care + no polymer" volume/hold/molding products). Promises that overlap Leave-in (`li_function: yes`) keep the full check: (i), (iii), D6. **Accepted consequences (PH2-1):** Garnier Fructis „Leave-In Spray" Diamond Sleek ⇒ `leave_in` (smoothing promise, real care, no fixative); Wella EIMI Flowing Form ⇒ `provisional_boundary` (D7 covered curl creams only; **superseded by R3-H2:** the straightening limb 3′ routes it to held `smoothing_styler`).
- **(iii) LI G0 unchanged `[D3, T15; LI §2.3, §2.3.2 reused]`**, read with the **Styling** fixative-class list (FX-1…FX-4, PH1-16), T_LI, and LI §2.3.2's precedence (positioning half C1/C2 only):
  - `excluded_styling_first` (fixative-class route, thin conditioning, C1/C2 positioning on durable hold or texture) ⇒ `styling`;
  - `in_category` (incl. T15 `incidental_film`: a conditioning film with a supporting fixative; the historical NEQI example is superseded by R6-Q1) ⇒ run **D6** below; if D6 does not fire ⇒ `leave_in`;
  - LI G0 `provisional_boundary` ⇒ `provisional_boundary (li_g0_provisional)`.
- **D6 — the exception `[D6, PH1-10, PH1-13]`.** All three ⇒ `styling`:
  1. **no real hold**: no FX-1…FX-4 member above T_LI. PQ-11/-16/-46/-69 (FX-R; PQ-69 by R1-T), FX-U, natural films, salts and particles are not real hold (D6 clarification, PH1-6). An uncorroborated generic Acrylates Copolymer (lexicon FX-2c) **or Polysilicone-9 `[R1-H3]`** above T_LI leaves this limb **unresolved** ⇒ D6 neither fires nor is excluded on it; the product goes to `provisional_boundary (li_g0_provisional)` + review `acrylates_copolymer_function_unresolved` (FX-2c) or `hair_fixing_silicone_unresolved` (Polysilicone-9) (§1.1 item 1);
  2. **no real care** by the PH1-13 effective-amount rule (R2): oils, esters, butters/hydrocarbons, plain silicones and fatty alcohols count **only above T_LI**; cationic conditioners, cationic polymers (incl. PQ-11/-16/-46) and amino silicones count above T_LI **or** below it down to — not including — the first pH adjuster (lexicon PH-1) listed after T_LI (fallback `Parfum`; no yardstick ⇒ review `care_yardstick_missing`). Humectants, panthenol, proteins, extracts, starch and volatile carriers never count. Any care decided below T_LI carries `care_decided_below_preservative`; a cationic below the yardstick carries `care_below_dose_yardstick`. "Real care" = any care, not COND ≥ moderate (a detangling spray counts). Oils count, as in Leave-in (PH1-10);
  3. a C1/C2 **styling-look promise**.
  - Format `[PH2-3]`: D6 applies to **any format**, not only sprays; format never routes. Example: Balea Locken Crunchgel (starch gel, no real care, no fixative, „Haargel für definierte Locken") ⇒ `styling`.

**3.5 Non-aqueous, not „Öl"/„Serum" `[operational — LI G0 + D2]`.** Class `styling_result`, water absent, only in the tail, or declared below a leading VC-1 propellant (lexicon §0.3, since v0.3, `[RG2-23]`) ⇒ `styling`. LI G0 could only return `excluded_anhydrous` (oil/serum destination), and D2 reserves the Oil boundary for products sold as „Öl"/„Serum" (Balea MEN Pomade, Elnett Haarspray, got2b Curlz, taft x Gliss Shine Coat).

**3.6 Anything else** (class `care` with no Leave-in claim, an unforeseen combination) ⇒ `provisional_boundary`, review. Never a confident route by default.

### 4.4 Step 4 — Names route in exactly two places `[D2, D7]`

D2 (Oil, only for products whose lead promise is shine, finish, anti-frizz or a smoothing finish — §4.3 step 3.1) and D7 (the middle band: curl/styling creams and balms, and since R3-Q4 straightening/smoothing stylers, §4.3 3.4 (i)). When no C1/C2 name exists, D2 and the step-1 lead read the retailer title's product-type noun only (§4.2, R3-Q2). Nowhere else does a name route **on its own**, and a name never stands in for formula evidence (an F value). The one other use of a name is as **promise evidence**: the job word of the confirmed name picks the lead promise (§4.2 step 1, RG2-21, R2-H3, R4-Q7; e.g. Balea „Styling WAX STICK" ⇒ `molding`), and a C1/C2 name that names exactly one job meets the evidence gate's promise input when no promise line exists (§4.1, R4-Q1; never a retailer title). Formula-type nouns in a name are never read as ingredients: Taft „Gel Wax" has no wax or gel former; got2b „Texture Clay" has no clay; Taft Volumen Schaumfestiger „Halt 4" has no synthetic polymer. Retailer facets are never evidence (alverde Beach Waves „ohne Alkohol" with Alcohol denat. r3; Color Wow „silikonfrei" with Polysilicone-29 r3).

### 4.5 Step 5 — The PH1-16 double-claim hold `[PH1-16, PH2-2]`

After steps 1–4 say `styling`, run the **locked** Leave-in G0 letter once more: LI L5 only (no FX-4), no D6, no D7, T_LI. If it would return `in_category` or `provisional_boundary` — i.e. the Leave-in engine could also claim the product — the S0 output is `provisional_boundary (pending_leave_in_addendum)` with `lean_route: styling` and the lean subtype. Products that reach `styling` through LI G0 `excluded_styling_first` (with LI L5), through 3.4 (ii), or through 3.5 are not double-claimed and are not held.

Under PH2-1, a product whose promise names no Leave-in function (`li_function: no`) is never Leave-in-claimable: LI's `in_category` needs an LI §2.1 function, and LI's `provisional_boundary` is reserved for genuine evidence conflicts (LI §2.3.2 clause 4), which a hold or volume promise is not.

What reaches the hold: every R6-Q1 heat-activated smoothing-film route (3.4 (i′), including the three owner-named cases), every D6 route, every D7 route (including the R3-Q4 straightening limb as extended by R4-Q4), every Styling route **reached by 3.4 (i), 3.4 (iii) or an owner-named route** that rests on an FX-4 member alone where LI L5 sees none, and every owner-named Styling route that the Leave-in letter would claim (§4.6). **Routes reached by 3.4 (ii) or 3.5 are never held `[R4-Q6, RG4-7]`** — the FX-4 clause applies only where the Leave-in letter runs; it supersedes v0.4's unqualified FX-4 sentence. `loreal-men-expert-barber-club-styling-pomade` (VP/DMAEMA r8, 3.4 (ii)), `redken-wax-spray` (3.5) and `schwarzkopf-taft-x-gliss-silky-shine-shine-coat` (VCap/VP/DMAEMA r5, 3.5) stay committed.

**Scope `[PH2-2]`.** The hold covers **every Styling route the unchanged Leave-in letter would also claim** — all D6 and D7 moves, the R6-Q1 smoothing-film routes and the owner-named routes — decided by re-running the locked letter as described above; it is not limited to the products charter §13 item 1 names.

**Carriage `[PH2-2]`.** A held record gets the **complete** Styling profile (every F, U, C and D field), marked `provisional`, with its lean subtype. It **counts toward the PH1-11 "≥ 5 per subtype" criterion, reported separately** from committed records. It is **not matchable or recommendable** until the Leave-in addendum is approved.

### 4.6 Registry of owner-named routes (they override the procedure where it would differ, within the §4.1 limits: promise-evidence failures only, never identity or formula blocks)

**Reason codes `[RG3-14]`.** Where the letter column reads „same", the record carries **the letter's pair rule** as its reason and the ruling as `ruled_route`; the reason is the ruling itself (`ruled_route_override`) **only where the ruling overrides** the letter (§4.1 (a)–(c)). **Every row names the `sample_id` it rules `[RG3-3]`** and, **since v0.5, the brand and the confirmed pack or brand-page name of each record, with the old retailer title where the name changed `[R4-Q6, RG4-2]`**, so a blind lane can match a row by brand + name without the opaque id (round 4: lane B missed the HASK row, which still read „HASK Curl Care", RG4-2); a product line named without a variant rules only the record listed. Tier labels: *pack* (C1), *brand page* (C2), *house-brand page* (C2 `house_brand`), *retailer title only* (C3), *non-German page only* (C5).

### 4.7 Emission contract `[LI §2.3.1 reused]`

| Outcome | Emitted |
|---|---|
| `styling` | full record (§10) |
| `leave_in` · `oil` · `heat_protectant` · `dry_shampoo` | identity block, formula record, `route` + rationale, both markers; Styling properties **optional and marked `informational_and_non_authoritative: true`**; no subtype, no fit, no cautions |
| `provisional_boundary (evidence_gate / formula_conflict)` | identity block, formula record, the missing input, a capture work order; for an owner-named product (§4.6) also its `ruled_route`. **No Styling values** — a ruled destination does not lift an identity, INCI, directions or formula block (§4.1) |
| `provisional_boundary (claim_conflict / mixed_promise / li_g0_provisional)` | as `leave_in` above, plus both readings |
| `provisional_boundary (pending_leave_in_addendum)` | `[PH2-2]` the complete Styling profile marked `provisional`, `lean_route` and lean subtype; not matchable until the Leave-in addendum is approved (§4.5) |

**Committed Styling routes: 47.** Per subtype (node): hairspray 6 · molding 10 · blowdry_lotion 8 · mousse 5 · gel 5 · shine_finish 4 · hair_powder 3 · texture_spray 2 · salt_spray 2 · refresher 1 · curl_cream 1 · **smoothing_styler 0**. Held: curl_cream 3 · refresher 2 · gel 2 · smoothing_styler 2 · salt_spray 1. This is the PH1-11 coverage picture under the rules as ruled; held records are reported separately from committed ones (PH2-2; §13 item 1).

**Counts note.** The table was annotated by hand (promise class and Leave-in function), not by script, and it already assumed the readings later ruled as PH2-1…PH2-3 and PH2-10. It has **not** been re-derived after the Phase 2 rulings; the per-path and per-subtype counts, and the routing of `langhaarmaedchen-lockenspray-natur-lockenwunder` (Leave-in under PH1-13 R2 / PH2-11; a `provisional_boundary` stress case in the calibration file, so outside the 70 Styling records), are **to be re-derived in Phase 3 under PH2-1/PH2-2**. No count here is a result.

**Round 1 (2026-10-12).** Blind round 1 re-derived the routes on the frozen v1.0 cohort (107 records) under v0.1; the per-subtype coverage both lanes agree on is `round-1/report.md` T9 (computed by `diff.js`), and it supersedes this table as the current picture. The v0.2 rulings move these round-1 outcomes, to be confirmed in round 2: Kevin.Murphy Killer.Curls ⇒ held `curl_cream` (RG-21); Balea Glow&Shine Finishing Spray ⇒ committed `shine_finish` (R1-Q2); Garnier Locken Methode Pflege-Spray ⇒ `mixed_promise` (R1-Q3); the hold levels of Taft Styling Balm Locken (⇒ 2; the tier question of its „Halt 3" was closed by the round-2 C1 capture, §7.3) and Taft Gel Wax (⇒ 1) under R1-Q1 (§7.3).

**Round 2 (2026-10-12).** Blind round 2 ran v0.2 on the frozen round-2 cohort (139 records: 116 calibration, 15 negative control, 8 unseen). Coverage where both lanes agree on route kind and subtype is `round-2/report.md` T11 (from `diff.json` `coverage_per_subtype_agreed`): committed / held — gel 5 / 2 · curl_cream 1 / 5 · mousse 5 / 0 · blowdry_lotion 9 / 0 · smoothing_styler 0 / 1 · hairspray 7 / 0 · shine_finish 6 / 0 · salt_spray 5 / 1 · texture_spray 5 / 0 · hair_powder 8 / 0 · molding 10 / 0 · refresher 1 / 4. It supersedes the round-1 picture. The v0.3 rulings move these round-2 outcomes, to be confirmed in the next blind round: Redken Refreshing Curl Mist ⇒ held `refresher` (R2-Q1); Balea Anti-Frizz Serum ⇒ `leave_in` (R2-Q2); NEQI Diamond Glass All ⇒ `leave_in` (historical R2-Q4 outcome, superseded by R6-Q1; its INCI re-check found the list complete, R2-H4); Balea Overnight Wellenspray ⇒ committed `gel` (RG2-13); Syoss Curl Cream Gel ⇒ `gel` (RG2-14; round 2 split `gel` vs `curl_cream`); got2b gotGloss and taft x Gliss Shine Coat ⇒ committed `shine_finish` (RG2-15, P2-2); OSiS Sparkler and Balea Finishing Spray Glow & Care are re-run on the R2-Q3 packet. The R2-H rulings add: Balea Haarwachs Styling Stick ⇒ `molding` (R2-H3; round 2 read `shine_finish`); GUHL 3in1 ⇒ care, current category (R2-H2); Wella EIMI Fresh Up ⇒ no longer owner-named, the border checks decide (R2-H1). `smoothing_styler` stays short (no committed record; Color Wow held): R2-T approved a targeted capture for it before blind round 3.

**Round 3 (2026-10-12).** Blind round 3 ran v0.3 on the frozen round-3 cohort (143 records: 139 carried, 4 new smoothing candidates; no unseen slots). The lanes agree on 2 865 of 3 003 core cells (95.4 %) and on the route of 135 of 143 records (8 route splits) (node, `round-3/diff.json`). Coverage where both lanes agree on route kind and subtype, committed / held (node, `coverage_per_subtype_agreed`): gel 7 / 2 · curl_cream 1 / 5 · mousse 5 / 0 · blowdry_lotion 9 / 0 · smoothing_styler 1 / 1 · hairspray 7 / 0 · shine_finish 9 / 0 · salt_spray 5 / 1 · texture_spray 4 / 0 · hair_powder 8 / 0 · molding 12 / 0 · refresher 1 / 4 — 10 of 12 subtypes reach 5 when held records count, 8 of 12 on committed records alone. It supersedes the round-2 picture. The v0.4 rulings move these round-3 outcomes, to be confirmed in the confirmation round 4 (R3-L): taft Texturspray Aloe Boost ⇒ committed `texture_spray` (R3-Q3) and Redken Wax Spray ⇒ `texture_spray` (RG3-1, RG3-18), which together lift `texture_spray` above 5; Bali Volumizing Curl Spray ⇒ held `refresher` (RG3-1); KMS Thermashape Straightening Creme ⇒ held `smoothing_styler` (R3-Q4); syoss Hitzeschutzspray ⇒ `heat_protectant` (R3-Q2); Wella Smooth Thermal Image ⇒ `heat_protectant` (RG3-4); Bali Flaxseed Gel ⇒ hold level 4 (R3-H); Taft Paste ⇒ 4 (RG3-10); KMS 2-in-1 Spray ⇒ 4 (RG3-9). **`smoothing_styler`** has 2 agreed records (KMS 2-in-1 Spray committed, Color Wow held); with KMS Straightening Creme (R3-Q4) and Elnett 3 Tage Glatt once its directions are captured (P3-4) it would reach 4, below 5 — R3-Q1 rules it may lock below 5 as a documented market-size exception (§14.1). **Amendment (R3-H2, R3-H3):** Wella EIMI Flowing Form joins as a held `smoothing_styler` (R3-H2), and Elnett's C2 article directions are captured (R3-H3), so `smoothing_styler` would count 5 if round 4 confirms them (KMS 2-in-1 Spray committed; Color Wow, KMS Straightening Creme, Elnett, Flowing Form held).

**Round 4 (2026-10-13).** The confirmation round ran v0.4 as amended on the frozen round-4 cohort (143 records). The lanes agree on 2 923 of 3 003 core cells (97.3 %), on the route of 138 of 143 records (5 route splits) and on route + reason + subtype of 134 (node, `round-4/diff.json`). The 80 core-field disagreements on 23 records are 9 lane errors (E4-1…E4-5) and 71 cells behind 10 register items; no disagreement is unclassified. Coverage where both lanes agree on route kind and subtype, committed / held (node, `coverage_per_subtype_agreed`): gel 7 / 2 · curl_cream 1 / 5 · mousse 5 / 0 · blowdry_lotion 9 / 0 · smoothing_styler 1 / 4 · hairspray 7 / 0 · shine_finish 9 / 0 · salt_spray 4 / 2 · texture_spray 6 / 0 · hair_powder 8 / 0 · molding 11 / 0 · refresher 1 / 5 — **12 of 12 subtypes reach 5 when held records count**, 8 of 12 on committed records alone (short committed: curl_cream, smoothing_styler, salt_spray, refresher). `smoothing_styler` counts 5 agreed (KMS 2-in-1 Spray committed; Color Wow, KMS Straightening Creme, Elnett 3 Tage Glatt, Flowing Form held), so R3-Q1's exception is not needed on the incl.-held count. The v0.5 rulings move these round-4 outcomes, to be confirmed in round 5: American Crew FIBER ⇒ committed `molding` (R4-Q1); Kérastase Curl Refresh ⇒ `leave_in` (R4-Q1); alverde Beach Waves ⇒ committed `salt_spray` (R4-Q2; salt_spray 5 / 1); Syoss Glaze Topcoat ⇒ committed `shine_finish` (R4-Q2); Aussie Work That Curl ⇒ `leave_in` (R4-Q3); Balea Glattspray Anti-Frizz ⇒ held `smoothing_styler` (R4-Q4; smoothing_styler 1 / 5); HASK ⇒ `leave_in` in both lanes once its row is matched (R4-Q6); Taft Gel Wax Glanz ⇒ `molding` and got2b Texture Clay ⇒ primary `hold_fix` (R4-Q7).

## 5. Subtype assignment (lane step 4)

Subtypes describe **how and when** a product is used (charter §3). They never set an F value (§1.2). Run in order; the first rule that decides, decides.

1. **D7 fired ⇒ `curl_cream`** (§4.3, 3.4 (i), curl sale); **its straightening limb or the R6-Q1 heat-activated smoothing-film route fired ⇒ `smoothing_styler`** (3.4 (i)/(i′); R4-Q4 also covers a fixative-led smoothing spray with thin care). `[D7, R3-Q4, R4-Q4, R6-Q1]`
2. **Directions lead with edges, baby hair or brows (`target_zone: hairline`) and the product is a gel-type shaping product ⇒ `gel`.** Edge control is a zone, not a subtype. `[D1]` **Brow use is recorded (PH1-7); the hair use decides the route and the subtype `[RG2-27]`** (`got2b-kleber-4-brows-and-edges-2in1-stylinggel`, `bali-curls-sleek-stick-brows-and-edges`).
3. Otherwise match the **job** (from the C1/C2 lead promise) and the **moment** (from the directions, §6.11) against the anchor table. Where the job and moment leave two subtypes open, the **product form breaks the tie** (property set §2.2). A name never decides (`[D1]`, step 4 of S0). **The moment breaks a tie only between two subtypes the job leaves open `[RG2-15]`**; it never overrides the job. **Form = what the product is as applied `[RG2-14]`:** a gel or jelly (carbomer- or polymer-thickened, self-described „Gel", a cast expected) is `gel` even when sold as a cream (Power Flex; `syoss-curl-cream-gel-3in1`, „das Gel … in das nasse Haar einkneten", a film to scrunch out); the capture `format` field alone is never a tie-breaker.

| Family → Stage-5 role | Subtype | DE label | Job anchor | Moment anchor | Tie-breaks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A Formen → `styling` | `gel` | Haargel | hold + definition, sleek looks; edges (`hairline`); **setting sprays and lotions for heatless sets (curlers, braids, overnight): hold + definition, damp or dry, shaped by a tool `[RG2-13]`** (`balea-overnight-wellenspray`) | damp or dry, shaped by hand or by a tool | vs `curl_cream`: gel/jelly/spray-gel form ⇒ `gel` (`balea-styling-creme-power-flex`: sold as „Styling Creme", a hydroalcoholic PVP/carbomer gel ⇒ `gel`; `langhaarmaedchen-lockenspray-halt-sprungkraft`: liquid curl-hold spray-gel ⇒ `gel`) |
| | `curl_cream` | Lockencreme | soft definition + frizz control with a real hold route | damp | membership only via LI G0 `excluded_styling_first` (T15) or D7; a curl cream with no fixative is Leave-in |
| | `mousse` | Schaumfestiger | volume + hold, wave/curl lift | damp, before drying | vs `blowdry_lotion`: foam form ⇒ `mousse` (`balea-ansatzvolumen-booster`); foam only — a heatless setting spray is `gel` (RG2-13) |
| | `blowdry_lotion` | Föhnlotion & Föhnspray | volume/hold created by blow-drying; root lift (`target_zone: roots`); **incl. damp volume sprays whether or not a blow-dry is named `[RG3-18]`** (`moroccanoil-volumizing-spray`, `wella-eimi-sugar-lift`) | damp, before drying (usually `before_heat`) | absorbs Föhnspray and root lift (D1) |
| | `smoothing_styler` | Glättungsstyler | sleek result made with heat — **a sleek/straight result must be the job `[RG2-15]`**; also every product the D7 straightening limb routes (R3-Q4: `kms-thermashape-straightening-creme`, held; R4-Q4: `balea-glattspray-anti-frizz`, held) | damp or before heat | vs `shine_finish`: a pre-heat/damp moment ⇒ `smoothing_styler` **only when the job is sleek** (`color-wow-dream-coat-supernatural-spray`); **a shine lead stays `shine_finish` whatever the moment** (`got2b-gotgloss-glanz-spray`, `schwarzkopf-taft-x-gliss-silky-shine-shine-coat`); not sold as „Öl"/„Serum" (D2) |
| B Finish → `finish` | `hairspray` | Haarspray & Haarlack | fixes the finished style — **the anchor needs a hold lead `[RG4-13]`** | `dry_finish` | vs `shine_finish`: a hold lead ⇒ `hairspray` even with a shine word (`schwarzkopf-taft-haarspray-glanz-halt-4`); vs `texture_spray`: a volume/texture lead with a teasing (toupieren) step ⇒ `texture_spray`, not `hairspray`, even when sold as „Volumen-Haarspray" (`redken-root-tease`) `[RG4-13]` |
| | `shine_finish` | Glanz- & Anti-Frizz-Finish | shine, flyaway control on dry hair — incl. anti-frizz on dry, finished hair by the main directions (R1-Q2, §4.2) | `dry_finish` / `dry_restyle`; a shine lead applied before heat stays here (RG2-15) | D2: not „Öl"/„Serum" |
| C Textur & Volumen → `styling` | `salt_spray` | Salz- & Beach-Spray | beachy, piecey texture — incl. products named „Beach Waves" / „Strandwellen" / „beachy" (the texture look, not curl definition `[R4-Q2]`; `alverde-beach-waves-spray-seaside-balance`) | damp or dry, scrunched | a salt-free beach spray is still `salt_spray` (the salt mechanism is an extra, §6.15) |
| | `texture_spray` | Texturspray | dry grip, airy volume, matte texture | `dry_restyle` | vs `hair_powder`: spray form ⇒ `texture_spray` — **form as applied decides: a powder dispensed as an aerosol spray is `texture_spray` `[RG4-13]`** (`balea-pure-styling-volumenpuder-spray`); vs `molding`: **a wax or paste in spray form ⇒ `texture_spray` `[RG3-18]`** (`redken-wax-spray`, „Texturierendes Sprühwachs") |
| | `hair_powder` | Haarpuder | root grip, matte volume | dry, roots | powder form |
| D Modellieren → `styling` | `molding` | Wachs, Paste & Pomade | restylable shape, separation; men's creams | `dry_restyle` | wax, paste, clay, fiber, pomade, cream are one subtype; `form_label` is display only (D1); a wax or paste sold in spray form is `texture_spray` (RG3-18) |
| E Auffrischen → `styling` | `refresher` | Locken-Refresh | revive curls/waves between washes | the lead promise is revival; directions on dry hair | D5: refresh is a day-2 *instruction* reusing the gel; a `refresher` product is an optional extra (matching policy, not S0) |

Confidence: `high` when promise and directions agree; `moderate` when the form broke a tie or directions are retailer-only. A held record (§4.5) carries its **lean** subtype.
