## 6. Properties (lane step 5)

Every property object carries the LI §4 / Mask §4 evidence record: `value`, `decision_type` (`deterministic` · `structured_judgment` · `claim_gated` · `transcription`), `confidence` (internal), `evidence_level`, `formula_observations[]` (with ranks and both markers), `supporting_signals[]`, `counter_signals[]`, `review_status`. Section numbers follow the property set's order.

### 6.1 `result_mechanisms` (F)

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| Values | Set of `fixative_film · natural_film · wax_lipid · particulate · salt · silicone_coat · humectant_only · none`, each with members `[{inci, rank}]` |
| Decision | **Deterministic lexicon membership above T_S** (lexicon §1, §2): FX-1…FX-4 ⇒ `fixative_film`; NF-1, NF-U (aqueous only) ⇒ `natural_film` (NF-U adds `natural_film_unsettled`); WL-1…WL-8 ⇒ `wax_lipid` (WL-1, WL-2, WL-8 tagged `structuring`); PT-1, PT-2 (non-aqueous only) ⇒ `particulate`; SA-1 ⇒ `salt`; SI-1 ⇒ `silicone_coat`; HU-1 alone ⇒ `humectant_only`; nothing ⇒ `none` |
| Never | RH-1 rheology, VC carriers, EM-1 emulsifiers, CA-1/CA-2 monomeric cationics, FX-R, FX-U and an uncorroborated generic Acrylates Copolymer (lexicon FX-2c) (they act through `hold_route`), SA-2 Sodium Chloride, SI-2/SI-3 |
| Counter-signals | §3.3 (unresolved T_S), §3.4 (`candidate_below_tail`), §3.5 (boundary position), NF-U |
| Evidence / confidence | E2. `high`: plausible T_S, members clearly above it; `moderate`: boundary member, unresolved/absent marker, NF-U; `low`: INCI source conflict |
| False signals | Names („Clay", „Wax", „Fiber"); retailer chips; tail heroes |
| Consumers | subtype extras (§6.15), D8b set (§7.5), job gate (§6.10), cautions |
| Cohort (node, 70) | fixative_film 38 · wax_lipid 28 · natural_film 11 (v0.2; **10** under v0.3, end of this cell) · particulate 11 · silicone_coat 6 · salt 5 · humectant_only 3 (fixative_film was 39 before the FX-2c condition; `john-frieda-frizz-ease-traumlocken-lockencreme` drops out). **Particulate 11 re-confirmed (node, 2026-10-12)** over the raw INCI: `balea-pure-styling-volumenpuder-spray`, `schwarzkopf-taft-texturspray-aloe-boost` (Cellulose r7, the lexicon's T4 PT-1 placement), `living-proof-full-dry-volume-texture-spray`, `schwarzkopf-taft-haarpuder-volumen`, `got2b-styling-puder-volumen-powderfull`, `balea-haarpuder-volumen-pomegranate`, `kevin-murphy-powder-puff-volume`, `balea-men-pomade-haar-bart-2in1`, `nivea-men-styling-cream-active-craft-stylers`, `moroccanoil-dry-texture-spray`, `john-frieda-volume-lift-pushed-up-dry-texture-spray`. The property set's earlier 10 used its v0 member list, which has no Cellulose; it now reads 11. Not credited: Potato Starch r2 in the aqueous Taft Schaumfestiger (NF-U), Kaolin r13 below Parfum r12 in Bali Gents Matt Paste. The three v0.2 lexicon additions occur in none of the 70. **v0.3 (node, 2026-10-12):** the lexicon v0.3 propellant reading of „aqueous" (`[RG2-23]`) flips 9 of the 70 to non-aqueous and moves one mechanism: `schwarzkopf-taft-texturspray-aloe-boost` (Dimethyl Ether r1 above Aqua r5) no longer credits Distarch Phosphate r4 (NF-1, aqueous only) and reads Tapioca Starch r8 as PT-2 ⇒ **natural_film 10** (was 11); particulate stays 11 (the record was already particulate via Cellulose r7). None of the v0.3 species placements occurs in the 70 |

### 6.2 `hold_route` (F)

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| Values | `present` · `absent` · `ambiguous` — the D6 "real hold" sense (fixative-class film), **never a strength** |
| Decision | Deterministic, above T_S. `present`: ≥ 1 FX-1…FX-4 member. `ambiguous`: no FX member, but an FX-R member (PQ-11/-16/-46, PH1-6; PQ-69, R1-T / RG-16), an FX-U member or an **uncorroborated generic Acrylates Copolymer** (lexicon FX-2c: LI L5 counts acrylates copolymers only when "used as fixatives", and the generic name also covers rheology modifiers); or an FX member that is `unpositioned` under §3.3. `absent`: otherwise. An FX member only at/below T_S ⇒ `absent` + `fixative_below_tail` note |
| Never | Starch, salt, particles, waxes, silicones (Polysilicone-29 alone = absent, LI L5 placement); a captured hold level never moves it (LI T7) |
| Counter-signals | LI L5 anionic/cationic pairing check — an identity check only |
| Evidence / confidence | E2 on every record (LI §7.7 freeze note), as §6.1 |
| Consumers | neutral fact (§7.6), gel film type, refresher/shine extras, D8b, job gate |
| Cohort (node) | present 38 (3 rest on FX-4 alone: Elnett Haarspray, Barber Club Pomade, taft x Gliss Shine Coat) · ambiguous 2 (`alcina-styling-mousse-aerosol`, PQ-11 r5; `john-frieda-frizz-ease-traumlocken-lockencreme`, uncorroborated Acrylates Copolymer r2 — review `acrylates_copolymer_function_unresolved`) · absent 30. Before the FX-2c condition: present 39 · ambiguous 1. Generic Acrylates Copolymer also sits in `gard-haarspray-extrastark-pumpspray` (pump spray, no RH-1 ⇒ corroborated; VP/VA r3 present anyway) and `kevin-murphy-killer-curls` (cream ⇒ not corroborated; Polyurethane-14 r5 keeps it `present`) |

### 6.3 `weight` (F) `[PH1-2, PH1-4, PH2-8; LI §7.5 reused]`

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| Values | production scale `light · medium · rich · unknown` (research trace LI `low · moderate · high`) |
| Decision | Structured judgment on the **LI WT anchors, unchanged in substance**, above T_S. Persistent families: (a) WL-6 oils + WL-7 esters; (b) WL-1 waxes, WL-2/WL-3 hydrocarbons, WL-4 butters, WL-5 lanolin; (c) FA-1 fatty alcohols; (d) SI-1 persistent silicones; (e) CA-3 cationic polymers and the polymeric CA-4.<br>• `light` (LI `low`): no persistent family above T_S and no LGN pair.<br>• `medium` (LI `moderate`): exactly one family; **or** ≥ 2 families with no rich member (the multi-family counter-signal is mandatory: name every family and its rank).<br>• `rich` (LI `high`): **an LGN pair** — a CA-1/CA-2 monomeric cationic **and** an FA-1 fatty alcohol both above T_S (LI §7.5 `high` row) — **and/or** ≥ 2 families with ≥ 1 rich member (lexicon WL-1, WL-2, Paraffinum Liquidum, WL-4, WL-5, the WL-6 rich band).<br>• `unknown`: incomplete INCI, T18 tier 2, or §3.3 readings that disagree |
| Ruled — PH2-8 (settles property set §9 item 12) | **Weight = the exact Leave-in WT rule, including the LGN-pair route to `high`/`rich`; no extra anhydrous route `[PH2-8]`.** (a) The property set's extra "anhydrous base with a rich member ⇒ rich" route is **not carried**: LI §7.5 has no such row, and dropping it moves no cohort value (node). (b) **Ruled — PH2-12:** the rich-member list keeps waxes, paraffin, microcrystalline wax and lanolin (Styling-local; moves into the Leave-in addendum after lock): PH1-2 adopted PS3, whose text names "petrolatum, wax, butter or castor oil", and Mask R8 placed lanolin heavy. (c) **The LI LGN-pair route to `high` is carried** (PH2-8), because PH1-4 reuses "the Leave-in WT rule" and that row is part of it. Consequence vs the property-set dry run: `balea-professional-styling-cream-traumlocken` (Cetearyl Alcohol r2 + Cetrimonium Chloride r9) and `john-frieda-frizz-ease-traumlocken-lockencreme` read `rich`, not `medium` |
| Never | form, subtype or name (LI G9; FS-1, FS-2, FS-17, FS-18); fixatives, natural films, particles, salts, humectants, volatiles; an **unresolved quat** never forms an LGN pair (LI §7.6: `maria-nila-curlicue-cream`, Quaternium-95 r11 ⇒ `medium`, review `quat_structure_unresolved`) |
| Counter-signals | rich member at `T_S − 1` ⇒ `moderate`; a C1/C2 weight claim („ohne zu beschweren", „für feines Haar") against `medium`/`rich` ⇒ `weight_claim_conflict` + review, value unchanged (LI §10.1.2 parity: `schwarzkopf-taft-schaumfestiger-volumen-halt-4-feines-haar`, `medium` via Guar HPTC r6) |
| Evidence / confidence | E2; ceiling `moderate` unless the families are fully readable above a plausible T_S (LI §7.5 ceiling) |
| Consumers | `thickness_fit` echo (a ranking signal, never an exclusion); buildup note limb 2 |
| Cohort (node) | light 32 · medium 23 · rich 15 (`molding` 10 of 11 `rich`) |

### 6.4 Buildup note (Hinweise) `[PH1-4, PH2-5; LI §8.5 reused]`

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| Values | entry in the `hinweise` record (LI §8.6): fired / not fired. **A note, not a score.** No „schwer auswaschbar" field exists (PH1-4) |
| Decision | LI §8.5 unchanged: fires when **limb 1** — LI §7.6 `permanent_cationic` above T_S (any Polyquaternium-n incl. PQ-11/-16/-46, Guar HPTC, Hydroxypropyl Guar HPTC, Silicone Quaternium-n, Quaternium-80, cationised proteins) — **or limb 2** — `weight: rich` (which always has a persistent family above T_S). **Limb 1 is reused literally `[PH2-5]`:** the lane reads the LI §7.6 persistence enumeration (`permanent_cationic`) internally, **only to feed the note**; Styling gets no PERS field and no persistence value is stored or shown. Example: Alcina Styling Mousse (PQ-11 r5) gets the note |
| Copy (LI §18, reused) | „Bleibt lange im Haar. Bei häufiger Anwendung kann ein kräftigeres Shampoo nötig sein – wie schnell sich etwas aufbaut, hängt von Menge, Häufigkeit und Shampoo ab." |
| Never | a duration, wash count or clarification schedule (LI G11); never an exclusion; never a skin claim |
| Deferred | Whether a `rich` Styling product used weekly adds +1 to the Deep-Cleansing reset load: Personal-Plan decision (PH1-4) |
| Cohort (node) | fires on 22 of 70: 10 via limb 1 (Taft Balm, Balea Pro Traumlocken, Taft Volumen Schaumfestiger, Syoss Schaumfestiger, Alcina Mousse, Alcina Föhn Lotion, Alcina Ansatz Volumen, Taft Texturspray, Wella Flowing Form, John Frieda Traumlocken) and 12 more via limb 2 only |

### 6.5 `alcohol_note` (F) `[PH1-3]`

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| Values | `fires` · `does_not_fire` |
| Decision | Deterministic: fires when an AL-1 member (Alcohol, Alcohol Denat., SD Alcohol n, Ethanol, Isopropyl Alcohol) is among the **first 3 declared ingredients** (water and propellants counted). Fatty alcohols and Benzyl Alcohol never count (lexicon AL-X) |
| Copy (LI alcohol note, reused) | „Enthält Alkohol – bei empfindlicher Kopfhaut lieber nur in die Längen geben." |
| Never | an exclusion, a ranking penalty, an „ohne Alkohol" filter, or "damages hair" (charter §10). Retailer „ohne Alkohol" chips never override it |
| Evidence / confidence | E1 observation driving a note; `high` on a complete INCI, `low` on a source conflict |
| Cohort (node) | fires on 28 of 70 (e.g. `john-frieda-volume-lift-ansatz-booster`, Alcohol Denat. r1 in the dm list, r2 in the list of record — the record is `formula_conflict`-blocked, so nothing is emitted, RG4-21; `alverde-beach-waves-spray-seaside-balance`, Alcohol denat. r3 despite the dm chip). Isopropyl Alcohol beyond rank 3 (`schwarzkopf-taft-styling-balm-locken-halt-3` r14) does not fire |

### 6.6 `ingredient_flags` (F)

As property set §2.8, on the **whole** list: `silicones` (any silicone incl. PEG-modified, volatile, Polysilicone-15; lexicon SI-1…SI-4), `oils` (WL-6 non-fragrance oils, WL-4 butters, WL-3 mineral oil; never essential oils or ethoxylated oils), `fragrance` (FR-1), `salt` (SA-1 or Sodium Chloride), `propellant` (VC-1); `fixative_family[]` ⊂ {`pvp_vp`, `acrylate`, `polyurethane`, `vinyl_acetate`, `pvm_ma`, `natural_film`} from members **above T_S**. Free-from claims and chips never override a flag (`color-wow-dream-coat-supernatural-spray` „silikonfrei" on Douglas ⇒ `silicones: true`, Polysilicone-29 r3). An unreadable token (lexicon §0.4 item 3) sets `flags_incomplete` + review; **a token that may hide a flag sets that flag to `unknown`, never `false` `[RG4-15]`** (`balea-locken-revitalizing-spray` „CITRONELLOL SODIUM CHLORIDE" ⇒ `salt: unknown`, so a salt filter never treats the product as salt-free). **`fixative_family: natural_film` comes from NF-1 and NF-U members above T_S `[RG4-27]`** (NF-U with `natural_film_unsettled`, lexicon v0.5 §1A; it follows `result_mechanisms`: `balea-men-styling-powder`, `bali-curls-strong-hold-flaxseed-gel`, `bali-gents-matt-paste`, `schwarzkopf-taft-schaumfestiger-volumen-halt-4-feines-haar` gain it, as round-4 lane B read them). Flags never predict tolerance (LI L8). Cohort (node): fragrance 66 · propellant 23 · oils 22 · silicones 12 · salt 10.

### 6.7 `provides_heat_protection` (C + F) `[PH1-15, PH2-4; LI §7.8, §13 reused]`

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| Values | `true` · `false` (binary). Research trace: LI's `not_claimed · claim_only · formula_plausible · product_tested` |
| Decision | `true` **only** when (1) a **C1/C2 heat-protection claim** exists (LI §2.4.1; `C2_cross_market_verified` allowed) **and** (2) a closed-list **HP-1 (LI L9) member sits above T_S** (trace `formula_plausible`). **The heat check reads Styling's own tail marker T_S (the Mask rule), the stricter side `[PH2-4]`**, not T_LI. Otherwise `false`; a C1/C2 claim without the L9 pass is still **shown** („Hitzeschutz laut Hersteller") but never fills the Personal-Plan heat role. No claim + L9 member ⇒ `false`, `not_claimed` (a formula never manufactures a claim) |
| Differences from LI, encoded as ruled | LI §13.3 sets `true` on a claim alone (trace `claim_only`, review); PH1-15 requires the L9 pass, so Styling's `true` is narrower. LI's `product_tested` (claim + exact-product test) is reachable in the trace but, under PH1-15's text, sets `true` only with the L9 pass as well; `product_tested` without an L9 member stays `false` until ruled (PH2-4; §13 item 5) |
| What is a heat claim | Protection from heat damage („Hitzeschutz bis 230 °C", „schützt vor Föhnschäden", „schützt vor Sprödigkeit und Haarbruch, die durch das Föhnen entstehen"). **Not** a heat claim: activation („Föhnhitze verstärkt den Volumen-Effekt", „wärmeaktiviertes Polymer") — that is §6.13 |
| Never | a grade, temperature or percentage (LI G10, FS-14); generic silicone, Polysilicone-29, generic/cationised protein, panthenol, oils (FS-7, FS-24) |
| Cohort (node) | 11 committed or held Styling records carry a C1/C2 heat claim; **0 reach `true`**. `john-frieda-volume-lift-ansatz-booster` (C2 „bis zu 220 °C") — *re-derived from the list of record `[RG4-21]`:* the record is `inci_conflict` (johnfrieda.com DE list, C2, of record; dm list as `inci_alt`) and stops at `formula_conflict`, so no Styling value is emitted. The list of record has no HP-1 member (Polyquaternium-16 r3, Polyquaternium-68 r5, Parfum r6); the HP-1 polymer VP/DMAPA Acrylates Copolymer sits only in the dm list (`inci_alt`), at r8 **above** that list's T_S (Benzalkonium Chloride r9), so the two lists would give different heat binaries (`false` vs `true`) — one more value the formula conflict leaves open; the record emits none until a capture settles the formula. *The earlier note* (HP-1 at r10 behind T_S Parfum r6, the case PH2-4 decided: heat polymer after Parfum ⇒ `false`) read an earlier capture; PH2-4's rule is unchanged. `garnier-fructis-leave-in-spray-diamond-sleek-glaettungsspray` (C1 „Hitzeschutz bis 230° C", Hydrolyzed Wheat Protein r12 above both markers) would be `true` but routes `leave_in` (§4.8) |

### 6.8 `finish` (C, F sanity) `[PH1-5]`

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| Values | `matt · natürlich · glänzend · unbekannt` |
| Decision | Lexicon on C1/C2 text incl. the confirmed name: `matt` (matt, mattierend, „kein Glanz"; **halbmatt, seidenmatt** `[RG2-11]`); `glänzend` (Glanz, glänzend, Shine, Gloss, **Glaze, Topcoat** `[R4-Q2]`, Brillanz, Wet Look; a shine qualifier in a product name is the finish, R4-Q7: Taft „Gel Wax Glanz" ⇒ `glänzend`); `natürlich` („natürliches Finish", „Natural Look", **„natürlicher Glanz"**, PH1-5). Brand shine numbers are recorded; words decide. Both a matt and a shine word ⇒ `unbekannt` + review. **Unclaimed ⇒ `unbekannt`, never formula-derived**. **Word pairs and the natürlich test `[RG-10]`:** matt + natürlich ⇒ `matt` (natürlich is the weaker, compatible word); glänzend + natürlich ⇒ `natürlich` (the PH1-5 pattern). „natürlich" is a finish word **only with Finish, Look or Glanz next to it** („natürliches Finish", „Natural Look", „natürlicher Look", „natürlicher Glanz"; also with a participle between them `[RG2-11]`: „natürlich aussehendes Finish", „natürlich wirkender Look" ⇒ `natürlich`); natürlich + a verb or another noun („natürlich gestylt aussehen", „natürlich strukturierter Look", „natürlich definierter Look", „natürliches Haargefühl") is a look/feel word ⇒ `unbekannt`. Round-1 cases: `bali-gents-matt-paste` (C2 „natürlichem Finish" + „mattem Finish" ⇒ `matt`), `wellaflex-schaumfestiger-locken-und-wellen-starker-halt` (C2 „lockiges Haar natürlich gestylt aussehen lässt" ⇒ `unbekannt`) |
| Not finish words | movement or texture words („natürlicher Haarschwung", „Sprungkraft", „geschmeidig", „seidig"); **a technology, formula or complex label („…-Technologie", „-Formel", „-Komplex") is a call-out, never a finish word `[RG4-10]`** (`loreal-paris-elnett-stylingspray-hitzeschutz-3-tage-glatt` C1 „POWER-SHINE-TECHNOLOGIE" ⇒ no finish word ⇒ `unbekannt`, §11.7) |
| Counter-signal (F sanity, property set §2.11) | `matt` on an oil/silicone-led formula with no particulate, structuring wax or fatty-acid soap, or `glänzend` on a particulate-led dry powder ⇒ `finish_formula_conflict` review; **the value never changes** |
| Evidence / confidence | E0 claim; `high` on an explicit C1/C2 word |
| Consumers | finish preference, asked only when `molding` or `texture_spray` is in play |

### 6.9 `flexibility` (C) `[PH1-5]`

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| Values | `beweglich · fest · unbekannt` |
| Decision | `beweglich`: flexibel, beweglich, elastisch, formbar, neu formbar/nachstylbar, variabel, **verformbar, Elastizität, wieder stylen / neu stylen (a restyling sequence such as „Stylen, Ausbürsten, wieder Stylen") `[RG2-12, RG2-6]`**, or a negated rigidity word („ohne zu verhärten", „ohne steif zu wirken"). Cases: `taft-styling-paste-forming-halt-5plus` (C1 „Verformbare Styles") and `loreal-paris-elnett-haarspray-extra-starker-halt` („Stylen, Ausbürsten, wieder Stylen") ⇒ `beweglich`. `fest`: Kleber, Freeze, betonhart, steif. Both ⇒ `unbekannt` + review; neither ⇒ `unbekannt`. Brand flex numbers (Alcina Flex-Faktor x/10) are recorded; words decide. **„Kleber" is a rigidity word only `[RG-14]`** (removed from the §7.4 strength list, so the two lists never overlap). **Words in the confirmed name count `[P-2]`:** a rigidity or flexibility word in the full pack name is captured as a `flexibility` claim with `from_product_name: true` (§2.2 A3); e.g. `got2b-spruehkleber-freeze-haarlack` „Haarlack Sprühkleber … Freeze" ⇒ `fest` |
| Not flexibility words | „ohne zu verkleben" (PH1-5); „ausbürstbar" **alone** (v0 convention, property set §9 item 15; PH2-11, kept by RG2-12); „Crunch" (a cast to knead out ⇒ gel `cast_expected`, never `fest`, PH1-5) |
| Never | a strength word, and vice versa (§7.4) |

### 6.10 `primary_job` + `secondary_jobs` (C + F gate)

**R10-Q1/Q2 (2026-10-14)** refines secondary eligibility and order below; R9-Q1 claim-type coverage, R9-Q2 primary selection and the formula gate remain in force. NEQI Diamond Glass All still has primary `sleek`, secondary `shine`.

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| Values | one primary (or `null` under the no-vocabulary-job rule below), ≤ 2 secondaries from `definition · volume · texture · sleek · hold_fix · shine · frizz_control · refresh` |
| No vocabulary job (R7-F, 2026-10-13) | If the lead supplies no vocabulary job but a later eligible C1/C2 promise line does, that line's first job is primary in §4.2 order. When the eligible C1/C2 lead and the other C1/C2 promise lines supply no job from the primary-job vocabulary, emit `primary_job: null` (no main job is shown; display copy is decided at integration) plus the internal review trigger `primary_job_unresolved`. Never infer a primary from subtype, formula, or typed finish/other lines. The owner-ruled route stands, and eligible secondaries are scored independently under the R10 strength-first candidate order and unchanged formula gate. **Example:** NEQI Diamond Glass Ultimate remains held `smoothing_styler`; its promise lines state styling + heat protection + heat-activated polymers + water repellence, with no vocabulary job ⇒ `primary_job: null` + `primary_job_unresolved`. |
| Primary and vocabulary | **Primary `[RG-11]`:** the first **job-bearing** phrase of the lead promise; product nouns (Gel, Gelee, Paste, Wachs) and strength phrases („mit mittlerem Halt") are not job-bearing unless nothing else is; shaping/forming wording maps to `hold_fix`. **A strength phrase is not job-bearing when it qualifies a product noun or follows another job phrase („Gel mit mittlerem Halt"); when the hold is the lead's subject („Intensiver Halt für …", „Starker Halt –") the lead states `hold_fix` `[RG2-4]`** (`syoss-professional-performance-max-hold-power-wax`: „Intensiver Halt für glatte und glänzende … Looks" ⇒ `hold_fix`). **A curl/wave noun with a shaping or setting verb („Locken formen", „Curl Shaper", „Wellen über Nacht") states `definition`; shaping without a curl/wave noun stays `hold_fix` `[RG2-5]`** (`syoss-curl-cream-gel-3in1` „Locken zu formen und zu definieren" ⇒ `definition`). **Job vocabulary `[RG2-6, RG2-7; R6-T (3)]`:** Schwung, Sprungkraft, Spannkraft, Fülle ⇒ `volume`; **an adjective before a job phrase is not a separate job `[R7-Q1]`** (Wellaflex „für schwungvolle, definierte Locken“ ⇒ primary `definition`). (Elastizität is a flexibility word, §6.9, never a job); taming or smoothing flyaways, „abstehende Haare/Härchen" or „Babyhaare" ⇒ `frizz_control`; `sleek` only for a smooth or straight result of the whole style („glatte Looks", „Sleek-Look") (`john-frieda-frizz-ease-zauberformel-seiden-finish-creme` „Glättet abstehende Haare" ⇒ `frizz_control`). **Definition `[R6-T (3), R7-Q2]`: a curl/wave word in the product's own confirmed name supplies `definition` (with §4.2's Beach Waves texture and curl-revival refresh exceptions); in descriptive sentences an explicit promise to define or shape curls/waves is required. „Definition“/„Define“ for a non-curl look and a result phrase such as „definierter Look“ never supply `definition`** (`alverde-beach-waves-spray-seaside-balance`: no definition secondary).  **R10-Q2:** „Struktur“ / „strukturiert“ ⇒ `texture`; a look description is WEAK. |
| Name vocabulary | **Names `[R4-Q2, R4-Q7, R7-Q2, R8-Q2]`:** Puder/Powder states `volume`. Redken „Powder Grip“ is a two-job name (volume + texture) and the lead line decides primary. Other product-noun mappings stay unchanged: „Glaze", „Topcoat", „Gloss" state `shine`; „Beach Waves" / „Strandwellen" / „beachy" state `texture`; a molding/hold product noun (Wachs/Wax, Paste, Clay, Gel, Pomade, Fiber, Haarspray) states `hold_fix` and its shine or strength qualifier states no second job; a true word for a different job beside it (Texture, Volume, Curl; Grip beside Powder) makes a two-job name (`got2b-styling-paste-istyler-texture-clay`: primary `hold_fix` from the lead C2 line, secondary `texture` from the name). **Brand line / collection names never supply a job (R7-Q3):** Frizz Ease, Traumlocken, Volume Lift, Diamond Sleek, Glow & Shine, Nutricurls. |
| Formula compatibility | **Formula gate** (property set §2.12): `hold_fix` ⇒ the D8b hold-mechanism set (§7.5); `volume` ⇒ fixative_film / natural_film / particulate / salt; `texture` ⇒ particulate / salt / structuring wax_lipid / a film; `sleek` ⇒ silicone_coat / wax_lipid / fixative_film; `shine` ⇒ wax_lipid / silicone_coat; `frizz_control` ⇒ silicone_coat / wax_lipid / a film; `definition` ⇒ any mechanism except humectant_only/none; `refresh` ⇒ any. A failing secondary is **dropped and recorded**; a failing primary is **kept** and tagged `job_formula_conflict` + review.  |
| Secondary decision | Follow the numbered R10 procedure below; RG-12 / RG2-16 / RG3-16 / RG4-17 / R4-H (5) are refined by it. A fixing verb without strength („fixiert die Frisur zuverlässig“) supplies an explicit `hold_fix` promise and no hold level (RG4-12). Technology/formula/complex labels never supply a job („POWER-SHINE-TECHNOLOGIE“, RG4-10). |
| Worked example (R9-Q1) | Balea Styling Spray Volumenpuder Pure Styling ⇒ primary `volume`, secondary `texture`: the C1/C2 line „griffige Textur direkt am Ansatz“, typed `other` in the claims packet, promises a texture result and therefore counts. |
| Never | Pure duration or humidity wording makes no job („bis zu 48 h", „100H" ⇒ `captured_claims`); an otherwise typed-duration refresh-between-washes promise still counts for the R8-Q1 refresh + volume tie. |
| No C1/C2 promise | for a ruling-named route (§4.1 exception): jobs empty + `claim_authority_gap`. **When the name alone meets the gate `[R4-Q1]`** (§4.1 input 1): primary = the name's one job (gate applies), no secondaries unless a C1/C2 line states one, + `claim_authority_gap` (`american-crew-fiber` ⇒ primary `hold_fix`) |

#### Signal collection and selection procedure (R10-Q1 / R10-Q2)

1. **Collect every job signal.** Read the confirmed product's own name, then **all C1/C2 claim lines** in packet order and each line in text order: `promise`, `finish`, `other`, `hold`, `flexibility`, `humidity`, `duration` and every other captured type are eligible. A field's type does not hide a job word: „Sprungkraft“ in a `flexibility` line is a `volume` signal (Taft Styling Balm Locken). Exclude pure feel-only lines without a result, ingredient/technology callouts, brand/collection names and words that fail this section’s vocabulary rules. Apply the existing authority/name-fallback rules; C3/C4 retailer copy never gains claim authority. Record the quoted signal, job, tier, source position and strength for the classification trace.
2. **Classify each signal, not the whole line.** **STRONG** = an explicit job promise, a strength word or brand number attached to the job, or an eligible job word in the confirmed product name. **WEAK** = a passing job mention without strength/emphasis, or a look description. A look description remains WEAK when merely describing a result; an explicit emphasized job promise is STRONG. A line may contain both classes. Duration or flexibility alone does not provide hold strength. **RG3-16's “hold_fix needs a strength word or number” now defines a STRONG hold signal; a bare hold mention is a WEAK hold signal**, rather than being excluded. An explicit fixing promise still supplies STRONG `hold_fix` without a grade (RG4-12). A masked strength/number still identifies a hold job, but determine its strength from the unmasked C claim packet; a generic mask is not itself strength evidence. Signal strength is an ordering label, never a hold level: no qualifying hold strength/number still gives `chaarlie_hold_level: null` under §7.2.
3. **Select primary by §4.2 / R9-Q2 as before.** The name/lead precedence, R7 no-job fallback and R7-Q1/Q2 exclusions remain in force. Where several result adjectives stand together, the one matching the named look/subtype leads; otherwise the first. `sleek` means smooth/straight overall shape and `shine` surface gloss. Do not rank primary by signal strength and do not promote a typed finish/other line to the primary fallback.
4. **Fill at most two secondary slots.** Exclude the primary's job. Stable-partition the collected signals into STRONG first, then WEAK: within STRONG, confirmed-name jobs retain name order before claim signals; within each strength class, claim order means packet order and then text order within a line (RG4-17). Walk all STRONG candidates before any WEAK candidate, deduplicating jobs as they are considered (a later STRONG mention therefore takes precedence over an earlier WEAK mention of the same job). Apply the unchanged formula gate to every candidate; drop and record failures, and continue to refill from the next candidate. Only when fewer than two compatible STRONG jobs remain may WEAK jobs fill free slots, in their original order. A primary formula conflict retains the primary and flags review. No third secondary is emitted.

The following quoted German wording illustrates signal classification; it is research evidence/examples, not UI copy. A bare look/feel word outside the job vocabulary supplies no signal at all. In particular „definierter Look“ remains excluded by R7-Q2; R10-Q2 admits „Struktur“ / „strukturiert“ only as `texture` wording for the hair result, never a description of the product's physical consistency.

| STRONG wording (7 examples) | Job and reason |
|---|---|
| „Für sichtbar volleres Haar“ | `volume`: explicit result promise (got2b Powderfull). |
| „für Volumen & Textur“ | `volume`, `texture`: explicit job promises (R10-Q1 wording). |
| „reduziert Frizz“ | `frizz_control`: explicit job promise (NEQI Diamond Glass All). |
| „starker Halt“ | `hold_fix`: strength word (R10-Q1 wording). |
| „Halt 3“ | `hold_fix`: brand number (Taft Styling Balm Locken). |
| „Volumenpuder“ | `volume`: eligible job word/product noun in the confirmed name (R8-Q2). |
| „um Volumen mit sprungkräftiger Textur aufzubauen“ | `volume`, `texture`: explicit build-up promise (Moroccanoil Volumizing Spray); the adjective adds no separate job (R7-Q1). |

| WEAK wording (6 examples) | Job and reason |
|---|---|
| „mit natürlichem Halt“ | `hold_fix`: incidental mention, no strength (got2b Powderfull). |
| „spürbaren Halt vom Ansatz bis zur Spitze“ | `hold_fix`: passing mention, no strength (Moroccanoil Volumizing Spray). |
| „Halt für bis zu 24 Stunden“ | `hold_fix`: hold mention with duration, no strength (Taft Haarpuder Volumen). |
| „einen natürlich strukturierten Look“ | `texture`: look description (Bali Gents Haarpuder; R10-Q2). |
| „glatte und glänzende, langanhaltende Looks“ | `sleek`, `shine`: look descriptions following the leading hold promise (Syoss Max Hold Power Wax). |
| „glatte Looks und Blow-Outs“ | `sleek`: look description (KMS Thermashape Straightening Creme). |

#### Worked R10 examples (frozen r6 C1/C2 evidence)

| Product | Collected signals and outcome |
|---|---|
| got2b Puder Volumen Powderfull | Name/Puder and C2 „Für sichtbar volleres Haar“ give STRONG `volume`; „mit natürlichem Halt“ (also captured as typed `hold`) gives WEAK `hold_fix`. Primary `volume`; no other STRONG job remains, so secondary `[hold_fix]` fills a free slot. Silica Silylate r2 gives particulate above the tail, passing both gates. No hold strength is stated, so the hold level remains `null`. |
| Moroccanoil Volumizing Spray | Name gives STRONG `volume` and the lead still selects primary `volume`. C2 „spürbaren Halt vom Ansatz bis zur Spitze“ is WEAK `hold_fix`, appearing before „um Volumen mit sprungkräftiger Textur aufzubauen“, a STRONG `texture` promise. Fill `[texture, hold_fix]`: STRONG texture first, then WEAK hold in the free slot, even though hold appears earlier in the paragraph. VP/VA Copolymer r3 supplies fixative_film above the tail, passing both gates. No hold strength is stated, so the hold level remains `null`. |
| Bali Gents Haarpuder (VOLUMIZING STYLING POWDER) | Name/Powder and C2 „maximales Volumen“ / „sofortige Fülle“ give STRONG `volume`; „einen natürlich strukturierten Look“ gives WEAK `texture`. Primary `volume`; secondary `[texture]` fills a free slot, with Silica Silylate r2 particulate above the tail passing the gate. The C3 grip/finish lines do not enter. R10-Q2 does not change the finish rule: „natürlich strukturierten Look“ still gives no `natürlich` finish under RG-10. |

### 6.11 `application_moment` (U)

| Field | Rule |
|---|---|
| Values | `damp_before_drying · before_heat · dry_finish · dry_restyle · second_day · unresolved`; primary = the lead instruction, others in `also[]` |
| Decision | Transcription against the property-set anchors (§2.13), from the verbatim directions (any G1 tier; not claim-gated). **Reading the two damp anchors `[operational — property set §2.13 anchor text]`:** `before_heat` when the directions make a heat step a **required** next step (blow-dry or iron named, no air-dry option) or apply to dry hair before a hot tool; `damp_before_drying` when air-drying is allowed, drying is unspecified, or blow-drying is offered as optional („für optimales Ergebnis föhnen"). This resolves property set §9 item 4: `alcina-foehn-lotion` and `balea-foehnlotion-volume-effect` both read `before_heat` if their directions require the blow-dry. `second_day` only when the **directions** lead with refreshing between washes — marketing copy („ideal zum Auffrischen") is not directions. **Spray-only directions `[RG-2]`:** directions that consist only of spraying the product onto the hair (distance, evenness, optional targeting of strands — „Aus 30 cm Abstand sprühen") with no moisture state, no drying or heat step and no shaping step read **`dry_finish`** (the product is applied last, onto the finished style), confidence `moderate`; missing or copied directions stay `unresolved`. **Equal alternatives `[RG-8]`:** when the directions offer two moments as equal options („in handtuchtrockenes oder trockenes Haar", „feucht oder trocken"), the **first-named** is primary and the others go to `also[]`. **`dry_restyle` needs a named shaping act `[RG2-8]`** (kneten, einkneten, formen, stylen, in Form bringen, toupieren; **also einarbeiten, durcharbeiten, mit den Fingern verteilen und formen `[RG3-7]`**; and **a paste, wax, cream or powder worked into dry hair by hand is `dry_restyle` `[RG3-7]`** — `got2b-styling-paste-istyler-texture-clay` „zwischen deinen Handflächen [verreiben] und … ins trockene Haar ein[arbeiten]", `osis-dust-it` „mit den Fingern in das trockene Haar einarbeiten", `syoss-professional-performance-max-hold-power-wax` „in den Handflächen verreiben … Auf trockenem Haar anwenden"): spraying or distributing on dry hair without one is `dry_finish`; a product sprayed strand by strand to revive curls is `dry_finish` (`alverde-locken-refresh-spray`), while a named shaping act such as „und nach Wunsch stylen" or „einkneten" makes it `dry_restyle` (`redken-refreshing-curl-mist` „sprühen und sanft einkneten" ⇒ `dry_restyle`). **Shaping with no moisture state `[RG2-9]`** (mirror of RG-2): directions that name a shaping step but no moisture state, no drying and no heat step read **`dry_restyle`**, confidence `moderate` (`redken-powder-grip-03` „über das Haar streuen … mit den Fingern durch das Haar kneten"; `redken-root-tease`; `got2b-kleber-4-brows-and-edges-2in1-stylinggel`); missing or copied directions stay `unresolved`. **Stroking over dry hair without a shaping act `[RG4-14]`** (a stick passed over the finished style to lay down flyaways) is `dry_finish` (`balea-styling-finishing-stick` „Nach dem Frisurenstyling sanft über die abstehenden Härchen streichen") |
| Counter-signals | missing, conflicting or copied directions ⇒ `unresolved` + review (LI G5). Never from the formula or the name |
| Confidence | `high` on pack or manufacturer directions; `moderate` on retailer-only directions |

### 6.12 `target_zone` (U)

**R6-T controls.** The main required application controls `target_zone`: an optional or conditional extra step at another site does not widen it, while required sequential sites do. Balea Volumenpuder Spray is therefore `roots` when the required application is at the roots and a later use elsewhere is optional/conditional; Redken Root Tease is `all` when the directions require roots **then** the whole hair. Two sites are `all` only when both are required.

As property set §2.14: `roots` (directions lead with Ansatz), `lengths`, `surface`, `hairline` (edges, baby hair, brows), `all`; no directions ⇒ empty + review. **„Lead with Ansatz" `[RG-9]`:** `roots` when the Ansatz is the only required site, or the **first-named required site within one equal alternative**, including an either/or that names it („in den Handflächen verreiben oder direkt auf den Haaransatz streuen" ⇒ `roots`, `bali-gents-haarpuder-volumizing-styling-powder`); `all` when the main required application covers the whole hair and Ansatz is mentioned only as a later, optional step. A main required roots application followed by optional lengths remains `roots` (R6-T (1)); required roots followed by required whole-hair application is `all` (R6-T (2)). **Ranges and emphasis `[RG2-10]`:** a range that spans the hair length in either direction („vom Ansatz bis in die Spitzen", „beginnend an den Spitzen … zum Ansatz") is `all`; an emphasis inside a whole-hair instruction („im Haar verteilen, besonders auf …") is `all`; **two sites named together** („auf den Haaransatz und die Längen") are `all` `[RG3-20]` (`osis-dust-it`; RG-9's first-named reading applies to an either/or, not to a pair); **Cases `[RG4-14]`:** „auf den feuchten Ansatz … bis in die Längen" is a range ⇒ `all` (RG2-10; `balea-styling-spray-ultra-volumen`); „vom Ansatz her ins Haar sprühen" with no other site ⇒ `roots` (RG-9; `john-frieda-volume-lift-pushed-up-dry-texture-spray`); a stick stroked over flyaways ⇒ `surface` (`balea-styling-finishing-stick`). `surface`, `lengths` and `roots` need the zone to be the only **required** application site; optional or conditional extra sites are disregarded for this field (R6-T (1)) (round-2 cases: `got2b-styling-puder-volumen-powderfull`, `schwarzkopf-taft-haarpuder-volumen`, `moroccanoil-volumizing-spray`, `bali-curls-volumizing-curl-spray-refreshing`, `john-frieda-frizz-ease-traumlocken-lockencreme`, `john-frieda-frizz-ease-zauberformel-seiden-finish-creme`). Feeds the subtype tie-break (§5), the edge branch and the cautions.

### 6.13 `heat_activated` (U, C raise)

As property set §2.15, narrowed by **R1-Q4 `[RG-3]`**: `yes` **only when the product's own text says it is heat-activated or that heat makes it work** — the text attributes the effect to heat acting on the product („hitzeaktiviert", „wärmeaktiviert", „aktiviert durch Wärme" / „durch die Föhnhitze", „durch Wärme fixiert", „Föhnhitze verstärkt den Volumen-Effekt" on `schwarzkopf-taft-schaumfestiger-volumen-halt-4-feines-haar`). An instruction to blow-dry — required, optional or offered for more volume („Für mehr Volumen trocken föhnen", „Du willst mehr Volumen? Dann einfach … trocken föhnen") — is a usage step for `application_moment` and gives **`no`** (`balea-styling-spray-ultra-volumen`, `jean-und-len-sea-salt-spray`, `balea-sea-salt-texture-spray`). `no` also when directions give heat no role; `unresolved` when activation appears only at C3/C5 (`color-wow-dream-coat-supernatural-spray`, Douglas „wärmeaktiviertes Polymer"). A C1/C2 activation statement (in a claim or in the confirmed name) raises `no` to `yes` (property set §7 crossing 1). **The record carries the raised value, and the lanes apply the raise themselves `[RG4-18]`:** a lane sees the claims packet as well as the F/U directions, so it emits the final value — C1/C2 activation ⇒ `yes`; activation only at C3/C5 ⇒ `unresolved` (`guhl-foehn-spray-langzeit-volumen`: directions „Vor dem Föhnen aufsprühen" alone ⇒ `no`, the confirmed-name word „Föhn-Aktiv" ⇒ **`yes`**; `kms-thermashape-2-in-1-spray` „Hitzereaktives Spray" and `kms-thermashape-straightening-creme` „WÄRME-AKTIVIERUNG" ⇒ `yes`; `color-wow-dream-coat-supernatural-spray` ⇒ `unresolved`). *Superseded:* RG2-26's „in the blind round `heat_activated` reads the F/U directions only; name and claim activation raise it after unblinding" (round-4 lane A followed it and lane B did not, E4-2 and RG4-18; under v0.5 lane B's value is the record value). **Never heat protection** (§6.7).

### 6.14 `captured_claims` (C)

Typed verbatim list (§2.2 A3), all tiers recorded with their tier. **No direct use** for selection, ranking or copy. Eligible job signals in these same C1/C2 lines are independently extracted under §6.10 (R9-Q1 / R10-Q1); the raw capture itself is not a selection field. A C1/C2 heat claim feeds §6.7 and its display line. Humidity follows LI T9; „bis zu 48 h" is never shown; hair-type claims never set fit; free-from claims never override flags; health-adjacent claims („Sanft zu Haar und Kopfhaut", „Dermatologisch bestätigte Hautverträglichkeit") are recorded and never repeated (charter §10).

### 6.15 Subtype extras

As property set §3; four are new fields, the rest are views:

| Subtype | Extra | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `gel` | film type (view) | `synthetic` (hold_route present) · `natural` (natural_film, no FX) · `mixed` |
| `gel` | `cast_expected` (C) | `yes` when C1/C2 text or the confirmed name promises a cast („Crunch", „Cast", „knusprig"), else `unbekannt` |
| `curl_cream` | `conditioning_co_load` (F) | `rich` = LI COND ≥ moderate (the D7 read); `light` = thin/absent (the T15 `excluded_styling_first` read) |
| `molding` | `form_label` (identity) | `wax · paste · clay · fiber · pomade · cream` from the confirmed name; display only |
| `molding` | `base` (F) | `anhydrous` (no water declared anywhere) · `water_based` |
| `smoothing_styler` | coating type (view) | silicone_coat · fixative_film · wax_lipid |
| `shine_finish` | base; fixative present (views) | wax_lipid vs silicone_coat; hold_route |
| `salt_spray` | real salt vs salt-free (view) | `salt` mechanism present or not |
| `texture_spray` / `hair_powder` | particle type (view) | PT members |
| `refresher` | type (view) | `humectant_only` vs `hold_route: present` vs lipid |
| `mousse`, `blowdry_lotion`, `hairspray` | views of `target_zone`, `heat_activated`, format, §6.8/§6.9 | — |

### 6.16 Derived echoes and cautions (D; computed last)

As property set §4 (LI T8 / Mask R9: annotations, never independently reviewed): `thickness_fit` from `weight` (ranking only), `texture_fit`, `length_fit`, the hold display and neutral fact (§7.6), the alcohol note (§6.5), the buildup note (§6.4) and the cosmetic / medically adjacent cautions, kept in separate layers (charter §10). The PH1-3 ruling drops the former alcohol + roots caution (property set §8, PS4). Matching policy owns the final tables (charter §14).

---

## 7. Hold level normalisation, D8b flag, neutral fact (lane step 6) `[SD5, D8, D8b, PH1-8 as replaced by R3-H, PH1-9]`

### 7.1 Where the level is used (D8)

| Subtypes | Role |
|---|---|
| `gel`, `curl_cream`, `mousse`, `blowdry_lotion`, `hairspray`, `molding` | A stated, fitting level ranks first; **no level ⇒ still eligible**, ranked after, shown „Halt: keine Herstellerangabe". Never excluded |
| `salt_spray`, `texture_spray`, `hair_powder`, `shine_finish`, `smoothing_styler`, `refresher` | Captured and stored; **no part in selection** (finish, weight and application decide) |

### 7.2 Assignment order (deterministic, claim-only)

1. **Only C1/C2 counts**, including house-brand C2 (§2.2 A4; the dm „Halt" attribute on Balea/alverde, PH2-7) and `C2_cross_market_verified`. Retailer copy, retailer filter chips and attributes on third-party products (dm „Halt", Douglas „Haltgrad") and C5 pages are tier-stamped traces and **never assign** (LI §2.4.1 rule 1; LI T7).
2. **Brand number on a known scale ⇒ table §7.3.** **An illegible or self-contradicting brand number is not used `[RG-19]`:** words decide, confidence `moderate`, review `hold_number_unreadable`, and the pack is queued for re-capture. Self-contradicting includes a number printed beside a display with a different maximum. Cases: `got2b-spruehkleber-freeze-haarlack` (dot scale illegible; „ultrastarke" ⇒ 4 by words); `taft-styling-paste-forming-halt-5plus` is **no longer a case `[RG3-10]`**: **re-read 2026-10-12 (recapture-r4 P-8): a five-mark Henkel display whose fifth mark reads 5+ ⇒ x/5 ⇒ 4** (§7.3; four filled dots and a larger fifth circle „5+", no maximum and no hold word printed; the claim carries this display description, P3-5).
3. **The number beats the word** on the same pack or page (Henkel words inflate: Syoss calls Haltegrad 4 „extra stark"). **Number-vs-word stays here `[R3-H4 (a)]`:** step 7 (R3-H) covers only word-vs-word and number-vs-number conflicts; a number against a word is settled by this step, never by the middle level, and is never empty either way (D8, R1-Q1): `schwarzkopf-taft-styling-balm-locken-halt-3` „HALT 3" ⇒ 2 against „Starker … Halt" stays **2**; `alcina-styling-mousse-aerosol` Hold-Faktor 5/10 ⇒ 2 against „Starker, flexibler Halt" stays **2**.
4. **Brand word ladders** ⇒ table §7.3. An unranked step ⇒ `review`.
5. **Strength words ⇒ §7.4.** Flexibility, look and unquantified words are not strength words.
6. **No strength claim ⇒ `null`** („Halt: keine Herstellerangabe"). **Never `0`** unless the product claims „kein Halt" (D8); **never derived from the formula** in either direction; **never borrowed** from a similar formula or a sibling (Balea Haarpuder keeps its own „starker Halt" although got2b's near-identical base says „natürlicher Halt"). The gap is queued for pack-photo fill.
7. **Conflicting C1/C2 strengths ⇒ the middle level, rounded up; never empty `[R3-H]`.** When two C1/C2 strength statements map to different levels after steps 2–5 — pack (C1) vs manufacturer page (C2), or two statements on one source — the level is the middle of the two levels, rounded up (2 vs 4 ⇒ 3; 3 vs 4 ⇒ 4). Both statements are kept in the trace; review `hold_claim_conflict` stays internal. **This replaces PH1-8's outcome** (a pack-vs-page conflict used to give `null`, „keine Herstellerangabe"). It covers **word vs word** and **number vs number** only; a number against a word is settled by step 3 (number beats word) and never reaches this step `[R3-H4 (a)]`. **Three or more conflicting C1/C2 statements ⇒ the median level, rounded up `[R3-H4 (b)]`** (2, 2, 4 ⇒ 2; 2, 3, 4 ⇒ 3; 2, 3, 4, 4 ⇒ median 3.5 ⇒ 4); with two statements the median is the middle level, so R3-H's rule is the two-statement case of this one. Retailer chips (C3) never enter the calculation (step 1), and a product with no C1/C2 strength statement still reads `null` (step 6, D8). Case: `bali-curls-strong-hold-flaxseed-gel`, C2 „Strong Hold" (⇒ 3) and C2 „Extra-starker Halt" (⇒ 4) ⇒ **4** (§11.19).

Confidence: `high` for a number on a known scale or an unambiguous word; `moderate` for house-brand C2 (ownership confirmed in review), cross-market C2, or a mid-ladder step. Internal only.

### 7.3 Normalisation tables (brand × format; mirror `TABLES` in `build-calibration-r2.js`)

| Brand scale (formats) | ⇒ 1 | ⇒ 2 | ⇒ 3 | ⇒ 4 | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Henkel Haltegrad x/5 — Taft, Syoss, got2b: **every Henkel leave-on format except Taft gels** (sprays, mousses, Föhnspray, pastes, waxes, balms, creams) `[R1-Q1, RG-4]` | 1–2 | 3 | 4 | 5 / 5+ („5+" = 5) | numbers shared across Henkel brands; number beats inflated words. Round-1 cases: Taft Styling Balm Locken „Halt 3" + „Starker Halt" ⇒ **2** (the round-2 capture carries C1 pack „HALT 3" and C2 „Halt 3"; both round-2 lanes 2); Taft Gel Wax „Haltegrad 2" ⇒ **1**; Syoss Max Hold Wax Haltegrad 5 + „Mega stark" ⇒ 4; Taft Matt Wax Halt 5 + „maximal" ⇒ 4; Taft Paste „5+": **re-read 2026-10-12 (recapture-r4 P-8), a five-mark Henkel display whose fifth mark reads 5+ ⇒ x/5 ⇒ 4** `[RG3-10]` |
| Wellaflex dots x/5; Maria Nila x/5 | 1–2 | 3 | 4 | 5 / 5+ | |
| Printed 1–6 — NIVEA MEN, Gard, Taft 6 | 1–2 | 3 | 4 | 5–6 | |
| **Balea printed 1–7 display** | — | — | — | — | **no legend ⇒ words only** `[RG3-23]` (`balea-styling-creme-power-flex`; both round-3 lanes `null`) |
| Alcina Hold-Faktor x/10 | 1–2 | 3–5 | 6–7 | 8–10 | Flex-Faktor recorded separately (§6.9) |
| **Taft gels** (scale 4–14, no legend) | — | — | — | — | **number not comparable ⇒ words only** („maximal" ⇒ 4); no word ⇒ `null`; a „Haltegrad x/5" printed on a Taft gel is **recorded, and the words decide** `[RG4-16]` (`schwarzkopf-taft-power-haargel-halt-5` „Haltegrad 5" with max 5 ⇒ words „maximalen … Halt", name „POWER" ⇒ 4) |
| **got2b six-mark display** (Henkel x/5 row beside a printed 1–6 row) | — | — | — | — | **words decide** `[RG4-16]`; the 1–6 row gives the same level (`got2b-spruehkleber-freeze-haarlack` „ultrastarke" ⇒ 4) |
| Elnett word ladder | — | Normal | Stark | Extra stark | „Sehr stark" unranked ⇒ `review` |
| **Wella Professionals EIMI** „Haltegrad"/„Haltestufe" x/4 (+ „4+") `[R3-H5]` | 1–2 | — | 3 | 4 / 4+ | by Wella's own words („Mistify Me Light" = 2, „leichtem Halt" with 2; „Strong" = 3; „extrastarker Halt mit Haltegrad 4"); level 2 (mittel) is not used |
| Brands with no stated maximum — American Crew Haltfaktor | — | — | — | — | **words decide**; the number is recorded |

New brand scales are added only with a published legend and maximum; until then the brand is words-only.

### 7.4 Word lexicon

| Strength words | Level |
|---|---|
| leicht, sanft, soft, weich | 1 |
| mittel, normal | 2 |
| stark | 3 |
| sehr / extra / ultra / mega / extrem stark, maximal, Max, Power; **extra Halt / Extra Hold / Max Hold** (extra, ultra, mega qualifying Halt directly) `[RG3-9]` | 4 |

**Round-4 addition `[RG4-12]`.** „Fixierung" with a strength word reads as „Halt": „leichte Fixierung" ⇒ **1** (`balea-styling-finishing-stick`). „Fixierung" or a fixing verb **without** a strength word („einfache Fixierung", „fixiert die Frisur") sets no level (⇒ `null`) and states `hold_fix` as a secondary job (§6.10).

**Round-3 addition `[RG3-9]`.** „extra Halt" in a claim line now reads like „Extra Hold" in a name: `kms-thermashape-2-in-1-spray` C2 „Sorgt für extra Halt …" ⇒ **4** (stored only: `smoothing_styler`, §7.1).

**Round-2 additions `[RG2-18]`.** „Kontrolle" with a strength word reads as „Halt": „mittlere Kontrolle" ⇒ **2** (`redken-wax-spray` C2 „fixierender Haarspray mit mittlerer Kontrolle"). „krass" ⇒ **3**; „ultra krass" / „krasser Halt" at the top of a got2b display ⇒ **4** (`got2b-styling-paste-istyler-texture-clay` C2 „ultra krassem Halt" ⇒ 4, the binding property set §5.6 row).

„Power" is present in both concept §4 and the build-script `TABLES` word list (the property set's earlier note that it was missing is obsolete). **„Kleber" is no longer a strength word `[RG-14]`:** it is a rigidity word only (§6.9); the got2b Sprühkleber level 4 rests on „ultrastarke" independently.

**Strength words in the product name `[RG-13, P-1]`.** A strength word in the confirmed name counts **only when it qualifies hold or the product as a whole** (Taft POWER Haargel, „Extra Hold", „Max Hold"). It does **not** count when it qualifies another result noun: „Ultra Volumen" (volume), „Power Flex" (flex). The freeze adds name words as hold claims only under this test. Consequences: `balea-styling-spray-ultra-volumen` takes its level from the dm house-brand attribute „sehr starker Halt" (PH2-7 ⇒ 4) once it is re-captured into `claims[]` (P-1), never from „Ultra"; `balea-styling-creme-power-flex` is re-derived from its dm house-brand „Halt" attribute once captured — until then it has no strength claim (⇒ `null` + neutral fact, §7.6).

**Never strength (⇒ `null` when nothing else is stated):** flexibility words (§6.9); look words („natürlich", „natürlich aussehend", „natürlicher Halt"); unquantified words („langanhaltend", „perfekt", „zuverlässig", „kraftvoll"); duration („bis zu 48 h", „24 h definierte Locken", „3 Tage") ⇒ `captured_claims`.

### 7.5 D8b plausibility flag (internal; **never changes a level**)

**Hold-mechanism set:** `hold_route` `present` or `ambiguous`, **or** `natural_film`, `particulate` or `salt` in `result_mechanisms`, **or** `wax_lipid` with ≥ 1 `structuring` member (lexicon WL-1, WL-2, WL-8). Non-structuring oils, butters, esters, mineral oil, silicone coats and humectants are not hold mechanisms (property set §6).

**Claims it reads:** C1/C2 claims, plus hold words in the product name **at any tier** (a confirmed name is C1; D8b names Cantu „Extra Hold"). Never retailer chips.

1. A strength claim of ≥ „stark", or **any** hold claim on a `shine_finish`, `smoothing_styler` or `refresher`, with **no** member of the hold-mechanism set above T_S ⇒ `claim_formula_conflict`, review.
2. Same brand + character-identical INCI + different stated level ⇒ `d8b_identical_inci_sibling`, verify against the pack. Neither level is borrowed. **Rule 2 runs in the pipeline across records after the lanes, not per lane `[RG4-25]`** (a blind lane sees one record and cannot see its sibling; the two lanes fired it in round 4 only on the pair this section names).

**A negated hold claim is not a hold claim for rule 1 `[RG3-11]`:** „kein Halt", „ohne Halt" claim the absence of hold, so a shine, smoothing or refresh product carrying one does not fire rule 1 (`osis-sparkler`, C2 „Kein Halt" ⇒ level 0, no flag).

**Taft Gel Wax Glanz `[R4-Q7]`:** routed `molding` with finish `glänzend`; the owner keeps its D8b flag internally (C2 „langanhaltendem Halt", Haltegrad 2 ⇒ level 1; no wax, film former or other hold-mechanism member in the formula). Rule 1's letter reads ≥ „stark", or any hold claim on a `shine_finish`, `smoothing_styler` or `refresher`, so on a level-1 `molding` it would not fire; the flag is carried as ruled: internal only, and §7.5 is applied as written `[R4-H (2)]`. An internal flag is calculation-log only (R4-L).

**Rule 1 never changes the level and never restores a dropped `hold_fix` secondary `[RG2-22]`:** on a `shine_finish` with a hold claim and no hold mechanism, the level stays as normalised, the flag fires, and the `hold_fix` secondary stays dropped by the §6.10 gate (`wella-eimi-glam-mist-shine-spray`).

Cohort (node): rule 1 fires on `cantu-shea-butter-extra-hold-edge-stay-gel` (name „Extra Hold", only Shea r7) and `loreal-paris-elnett-stylingspray-hitzeschutz-3-tage-glatt` (C1 „Starker Halt", silicone_coat only; level 3 kept, PH1-14). Rule 2 fires on `schwarzkopf-taft-haarspray-classic-halt-3` (2) vs `schwarzkopf-taft-haarspray-glanz-halt-4` (3). Duration-only claims (`balea-locken-revitalizing-spray` „bis zu 24 Stunden definierte Locken") are not hold claims and do not fire it.

### 7.6 Neutral fact `[PH1-9, D8b]`

„Enthält Stylingpolymere (z. B. ‹first FX member above T_S›)" is shown **iff** the subtype is one of the six hold-relevant subtypes (§7.1), the level is `null`, and `hold_route = present`. FX-R alone (`ambiguous`), an uncorroborated generic Acrylates Copolymer (lexicon FX-2c; `ambiguous`, and never the named example), natural films, waxes, salts and particles never trigger it. It is an E1 ingredient observation, never a rating. Cohort (node): 8 records — `syoss-curl-cream-gel-3in1` (PVP r2), `john-frieda-volume-lift-ansatz-booster` (PVP r3 in the dm list only; *re-derived `[RG4-21]`:* the list of record, johnfrieda.com DE, has no FX member — Polyquaternium-16 r3 is FX-R ⇒ `ambiguous`, no fact — and the record is `formula_conflict`-blocked, so nothing is shown), `balea-ansatzvolumen-booster` (Butyl Ester of PVM/MA Copolymer r5), `american-crew-fiber` (PVP r4), `loreal-men-expert-barber-club-styling-pomade` (VP/DMAEMA r8), `balea-styling-creme-power-flex` (PVP r3; round 1's level 4 from „Power" is not valid under RG-13 — re-derived from its dm attribute once captured), `kevin-murphy-killer-curls` (Polyurethane-14 r5; its Acrylates Copolymer r4 is uncorroborated, and the record is `provisional_boundary (evidence_gate)` under PH2-10, so nothing is emitted today), `balea-haargel-hair-jelly` (PVP r6). *Superseded:* the v0.1 draft listed 9, including `john-frieda-frizz-ease-traumlocken-lockencreme` (Acrylates Copolymer r2); under FX-2c its `hold_route` is `ambiguous`, so the fact is not shown. Whether a „pflanzliche Filmbildner" variant is wanted stays open (property set §6).

### 7.7 Comparability

Levels compare only within a hold family — film (gel, curl_cream, mousse, blowdry_lotion, hairspray, salt and texture sprays), molding, powder. The app never compares hold across families (charter §11).

### 7.8 Worked normalisations

The 20 worked rows of property set §5.6 are binding examples of this section and are not repeated here. Additions: `alcina-styling-mousse-aerosol` C2 „Starker, flexibler Halt" + Hold-Faktor 5/10 ⇒ number beats word ⇒ **2**, flexibility `beweglich`; `balea-men-pomade-haar-bart-2in1` house-brand dm attribute „mittlerer Halt" ⇒ **2** (moderate, house-brand review), „langanhaltenden Halt" is unquantified; `schwarzkopf-taft-styling-balm-locken-halt-3` C1 pack „HALT 3" (Henkel x/5, R1-Q1) + C2 „Starker … Halt" ⇒ number beats word ⇒ **2** (round 1 split 3 vs 2; the v0.2 open question whether „Halt 3" was C1/C2 is closed by the round-2 capture, both lanes 2); `redken-wax-spray` „mittlerer Kontrolle" ⇒ **2** (RG2-18). Round-3 additions: `bali-curls-strong-hold-flaxseed-gel` „Strong Hold" 3 vs „Extra-starker Halt" 4 ⇒ **4** (R3-H; both kept, review `hold_claim_conflict`); `taft-styling-paste-forming-halt-5plus` five-mark display, fifth mark „5+" ⇒ **4** (RG3-10); `kms-thermashape-2-in-1-spray` „extra Halt" ⇒ **4**, stored only (RG3-9); `osis-sparkler` „Kein Halt" ⇒ **0**, no D8b flag (RG3-11).

## 8. Review triggers

Each trigger is a named id. A record lists every trigger that fired; none of them changes a value unless its own rule says so.

| Trigger | Fires when | Section |
|---|---|---|
| `s0_evidence_gate` | no C1/C2 promise (neither a promise line nor a C1/C2 name naming exactly one job, R4-Q1), no G1-tier directions (a C5 page is none, P4-1), or incomplete INCI; also listed as a trigger beside `formula_conflict` when both block (RG4-21) | §4.1, §4.0 |
| `s0_claim_conflict` | C1 and C2 lead with different promise classes | §4.1 |
| `formula_source_conflict` / `formula_or_identity_conflict` | LI T18 tier 2 / tier 1 residual; two INCIs under one GTIN are tested for tier 1 first | §2.2 A2 |
| `s0_mixed_promise` | step 2 `mixed` and D7 did not fire — incl. every C1/C2 promise covering a care job and curl definition (R1-Q3 as narrowed by R2-Q1; manual review) | §4.2 |
| `li_g0_provisional` | LI G0 returns `provisional_boundary` | §4.3 |
| `pending_leave_in_addendum` | PH1-16 hold | §4.5 |
| `ruled_route_override` | a §4.6 registry route differs from the S0 letter — incl. the §4.1 (d) D6/D7 rows (R4-Q5) | §4.6 |
| `claim_authority_gap` | a claim-keyed field had no C1/C2 source (LI §2.4.1 rule 1) — incl. every record whose name alone met §4.1 input 1 (R4-Q1) | §2.1, §4.1 |
| `house_brand_ownership` | a C2 claim rests on LI rule 6 | §2.2 A4 |
| `care_decided_below_preservative` · `care_below_dose_yardstick` · `care_yardstick_missing` | the D6 care test decided below T_LI, below the yardstick, or found no yardstick | §4.3 D6 |
| `tail_marker_unresolved` · `tail_marker_absent` | T_S third limb or no T_S class | §3.3 |
| `tail_marker_implausible` · `tail_marker_vacuous` | T_LI clauses 5 / 6 (LI §3.1.1). **Fire only when a border rule on the record's path reads T_LI** (3.4 (iii), D6, PH1-16) `[RG-18]`; the D7 limbs of 3.4 (i) read T_S and LI COND, so a vacuous T_LI never blocks them `[R3-H4 (e)]`. Round-1 records that raised the question: `got2b-spruehkleber-freeze-haarlack` (3.4 (ii): no border rule reads T_LI), `guhl-locken-pflege-und-styling-3in1` (`mixed`: the D7 test runs first; since R3-H4 (e) it reads T_S and LI COND, not T_LI) | §3.3 |
| `candidate_below_tail` | a below-marker species would change a value | §3.4 |
| `natural_film_unsettled` | an NF-U member was credited | §6.1 |
| `pq_only_film_former` | `hold_route: ambiguous` via FX-R | §6.2 |
| `acrylates_copolymer_function_unresolved` | a generic Acrylates Copolymer without FX-2c corroboration (lexicon §1A) is the only fixative candidate, or a D6/D7/LI G0 border limb turns on it | §6.2, §4.3 |
| `hair_fixing_silicone_unresolved` | Polysilicone-9 (lexicon SI-4 + FX-U, R1-H3) is the only fixative candidate, or the D6 hold limb turns on it | §6.2, §4.3 |
| `unsettled_film_former` | `hold_route: ambiguous` via FX-U | §6.2 |
| `quat_structure_unresolved` | an opaque Quaternium-n | §6.3 |
| `weight_claim_conflict` | C1/C2 weight claim vs `medium`/`rich` | §6.3 |
| `flags_incomplete` · `transcription_defect` | an unreadable INCI token; a flag the token may hide reads `unknown` (RG4-15) | §6.6 |
| `formula_source_page_error` | a manufacturer page prints an evidently foreign INCI (A10); the consistent list is the formula of record; open until a pack INCI (C1) is captured `[R2-H1]` | §2.2 A10 |
| `inci_tail_defect` | an INCI defect confined to the tail (at or below T_S: truncated allergen block, split fragrance/oil token, missing separator in the fragrance block); S0 is not blocked `[RG2-24]` | §4.1 |
| `heat_claim_without_l9` | C1/C2 heat claim, no HP-1 above the marker (shown, not used) | §6.7 |
| `finish_formula_conflict` | §6.8 sanity check | §6.8 |
| `finish_word_conflict` · `flex_word_conflict` | both a matt and a shine word; both a flex and a rigidity word | §6.8, §6.9 |
| `job_formula_conflict` | the primary job fails the formula gate | §6.10 |
| `primary_job_unresolved` | the eligible C1/C2 lead and the other C1/C2 promise lines supply no job from the primary-job vocabulary ⇒ `primary_job: null` (no main job is shown); internal review only, the owner-ruled route stands and eligible secondaries are scored independently | §6.10 |
| `directions_unresolved` | missing / conflicting / copied directions | §6.11 |
| `hold_claim_conflict` | two C1/C2 strength statements map to different levels — pack vs manufacturer page, or two statements on one source. **The level is the middle of the two, rounded up, never `null` `[R3-H]`**; both statements kept; internal, never shown (replaces PH1-8's `null` outcome) | §7.2 step 7, §2.2 A1 |
| `hold_ladder_unranked` | an unranked brand step („Sehr stark") | §7.3 |
| `hold_number_unreadable` | a captured brand number is illegible or contradicts its own display; words decided; pack queued for re-capture `[RG-19]` | §7.2 |
| `name_from_retailer_title` | no C1/C2 name exists and the product-type noun of a retailer title served as the confirmed-name job word for the §4.2 step-1 lead or for D2 `[R3-Q2]`; open until a pack or brand-page name is captured (P3-7) | §4.2, §4.3 3.1 |
| `retailer_title_type_noun_conflict` | no C1/C2 name exists and the retailer titles captured carry different product-type nouns ⇒ no shop-title fallback; the name decides nothing `[R3-H4 (d)]` | §2.2 A3, §4.2 |
| `directions_from_brand_article` | the directions are a product-specific how-to from the manufacturer's own German advice article (C2), not a pack or product-page field `[R3-H3]` | §2.2 A12 |
| `water_based_serum_exception` | a „Serum" in the 3.1 scope passed the water-based exception test (cationic read down to the PH1-13 yardstick, R2-Q2) and went to 3.4 (iii) `[RG-15, RG2-2]` | §4.3 3.1 |
| `claim_formula_conflict` · `d8b_identical_inci_sibling` | D8b rules 1 / 2 | §7.5 |
| `species_reading_conflict` | one species at one rank read as architecture by one rule and as trace by another (LI §2.3.2 cross-rule clause) | §3 |
| `low_confidence` | any value at `low` | §3.1 |

---

## 9. False-signal register

LI FS-1…FS-27 (LI §12) apply unchanged where relevant (FS-1/2/17/18 form ≠ weight; FS-3/20 silicone-free ≠ low buildup; FS-4 volatile ≠ no residue; FS-6/15 humectants ≠ anti-frizz; FS-7/14/24 heat; FS-9 hold polymer ≠ care; FS-25 bottle rheology). Styling-specific false signals (SFS) — producing one is a rule violation:

| ID | False signal | Correct reading |
|---|---|---|
| SFS-1 | A name decides the formula or the subtype: „Gel Wax" = wax, „Texture Clay" = clay, „Fiber" = fibres, „Haaröl" = oil formula | Names route only under D2 and D7 (§4.4); the INCI decides mechanisms |
| SFS-2 | A retailer filter chip or attribute is a claim: dm „ohne Alkohol", Douglas „silikonfrei", dm/Douglas „Halt"/„Haltgrad" on a third-party product | C3/C4 trace; INCI governs flags; only C1/C2 (incl. house brand) assigns a level |
| SFS-3 | A duration is a strength: „bis zu 48 h Halt", „24 h definierte Locken", „3 Tage glatt" | The duration goes to `captured_claims` and supplies neither a hold level nor a job. Read accompanying C1/C2 job wording independently under §6.10: bare „Halt“ is a WEAK `hold_fix` signal (R10-Q1); the duration never suppresses an otherwise eligible job signal. |
| SFS-4 | A thick gel or a high carbomer rank means strong hold | RH-1 is never a hold route |
| SFS-5 | Starch, salt or silica is "real hold" for D6 | D6 clarification: fixative-class only |
| SFS-6 | PQ-11/-16/-46/-69 alone is a fixative | PH1-6, R1-T: `ambiguous` + review |
| SFS-7 | A strength can be estimated from the formula („PVP rank 3 ⇒ starker Halt") | D8/D8b: never; best formula rule tested was right 3 of 6 (`round2/hold-research-ingredients.md`) |
| SFS-8 | Brand numbers compare across brands (Taft gel „Halt 14" > Syoss „Haltegrad 5") | §7.3: tables per brand; Taft gels words-only |
| SFS-9 | „Hitzeschutz bis 230 °C" is a protection grade, or any heat claim fills the heat role | PH1-15: binary, claim **and** L9 pass; temperatures never used |
| SFS-10 | Heat activation („wärmeaktiviert", „Föhnhitze verstärkt") is heat protection | §6.13 vs §6.7 |
| SFS-11 | Fatty alcohols trigger the alcohol note | PH1-3: AL-1 only |
| SFS-12 | „Natürlicher Glanz" is a shine finish; „ohne zu verkleben" is flexibility; „Crunch" is `fest` | PH1-5 |
| SFS-13 | Marketing "refresh" copy is a second-day direction | §6.11: only directions set `second_day` |
| SFS-14 | A "Leave-In" word in a name makes a product Leave-in (Balea „Leave-In Glow&Shine Finishing Spray") | the promise class and LI G0 decide (§4.2–§4.3) |
| SFS-15 | A care or ingredient call-out („mit pflegendem Camelinaöl") makes a promise `mixed` or `care` | §4.2: only co-equal lead jobs make `mixed` |
| SFS-16 | A heat claim on a curl cream turns it into a heat protectant | primary promise decides (3.3); PH1-12 for the D7 band |
| SFS-17 | A blow-dry tip („für mehr Volumen trocken föhnen") makes a product heat-activated | R1-Q4: only the product's own text that heat activates it or makes it work; a blow-dry step is `application_moment` (§6.13) |
| SFS-18 | A care noun in the name („Pflege-Spray", „Kur") settles a care-vs-curl-definition promise as `care` | R1-Q3: such a promise is `mixed` ⇒ manual review, whatever the name (§4.2); the name's care word is the care half (R2-Q1), it never breaks the tie |
| SFS-19 | Any anti-frizz word makes a finishing spray a Leave-in function | R1-Q2: anti-frizz on dry, finished hair by the main directions is the `shine_finish` job (§4.2) |
| SFS-20 | „Ultra Volumen" or „Power Flex" in a name is a strength claim | RG-13: a name strength word counts only when it qualifies hold or the product (§7.4) |
| SFS-21 | Reviving or reactivating curls („revitalisiert und reaktiviert Locken") is curl definition | R2-Q1: revival is refresh (the `refresher` job); the definition half needs „definiert"/„Definition" (§4.2) |
| SFS-22 | A care adjective on the result („gepflegte Locken") or a feel line („Verbesserte Geschmeidigkeit") is the care half of a care + definition tie | R2-Q1, R2-H5: only a promised care job counts (§4.2); SFS-15 governs adjectives and call-outs; feel lines never count |
| SFS-23 | A pre-heat moment makes a shine spray a `smoothing_styler` | RG2-15: a shine lead stays `shine_finish` whatever the moment (§5) |
| SFS-24 | A care cream that may also be left in as a curl cream („3in1 Lockencreme, Kur & Spülung") is Styling | R2-H2: it stays care in its current category; only a fixative-class polymer or the D7 middle band makes it Styling (§4.1) |
| SFS-25 | The domain ending decides the tier (a .com.au or .eu host ⇒ C5; a .de retailer ⇒ C2) | R2-H6: a manufacturer's German-language storefront for the German market is C2 whatever its domain; other-market pages are C5; retailers are C3 (§2.2 A9) |
| SFS-26 | A care word joined to the name's one job in a lead line („Textur und Pflege" on a „Texturspray") makes the promise `mixed` | R3-Q3: a one-job name wins; the care word is a call-out (SFS-15); `mixed` stays for names that name both jobs and for care + curl definition (R1-Q3) (§4.2) |
| SFS-27 | Any word of a retailer title counts as the product name — or none does | R3-Q2: only the title's product-type noun, and only when no pack or brand-page name exists; every other title word stays C3 (§4.2) |
| SFS-28 | Two conflicting C1/C2 strength statements leave the level empty („keine Herstellerangabe") | R3-H: the middle level, rounded up; `null` only when no C1/C2 strength statement exists (§7.2 step 7) |
| SFS-29 | A product with no promise sentence has no C1/C2 promise even when its pack or German brand page names one job — or any retailer title can stand in | R4-Q1: a C1/C2 name that names exactly one job meets §4.1 input 1; a retailer title never does; claim-keyed values stay no-claim (§4.1) |
| SFS-30 | „Beach Waves" in a name is curl definition (a wave noun) | R4-Q2: it names the salt-spray texture look; Wellen-Spray / Wellenauffrischer stay curl words (§4.2) |
| SFS-31 | A result phrase („strong hair", „gesunde Locken") or a line prefix („Nutri-") is the care half of a care + definition tie | R4-Q3: only a care verb or care noun is (§4.2) |
| SFS-32 | A technology label („POWER-SHINE-TECHNOLOGIE", „-Formel", „-Komplex") is a finish word or a job | RG4-10: a call-out (SFS-15), never finish or job (§6.8, §6.10) |
| SFS-33 | A shine or strength qualifier in a product name („Gel Wax Glanz", „Glow Wax") is a second job | R4-Q7: it is the finish or grade; the product noun names the job (§4.2) |

## 10. Record and blind-lane envelope

### 10.1 Record (one per product; JSON shape, field ownership in brackets)

```jsonc
{
  "sample_id": "…", "standard_version": "styling-inci-v0.5-r10amend", "lexicon_version": "styling-lexicon-v0.5-r7amend",
  "identity": { "brand": "…", "product_name": "…", "gtin": "…", "size": "…", "format": "gel|spray_gel|jelly|cream|foam|pump_spray|aerosol|wax|paste|pomade|powder",
                "identity_status": "verified|…", "inci_raw": "…", "inci_normalised": [{"rank":1,"inci":"…","lexicon_id":"…"}],
                "inci_source": {"tier":"C1|C2|C3|C4|C5","url":"…","date":"…"}, "transcription_repairs": [], "inci_flags": [] }, // [capture; inci_flags ship in the S0 packet, RG2-24]
  "directions": { "verbatim": "…", "tier": "…", "source": "…", "date": "…" },                                     // [capture, U input]
  "claims": [ { "type": "promise|hold|flexibility|finish|cast|duration|humidity|heat_protection|heat_activation|hair_type|free_from|other",
                "raw": "…", "tier": "C1|C2|C2_house_brand|C2_cross_market_verified|C3|C4|C5", "source": "…", "date": "…",
                "number": null, "scale_as_written": null, "words": null, "from_product_name": false } ],                                       // [capture, C]
  "tail_marker": { "T_S": {"inci":"…","rank":0,"status":"plausible|unresolved|absent","alt":null},
                   "T_LI": {"inci":"…","rank":0,"status":"plausible|implausible|vacuous|none_visible"} },       // [F]
  "s0": { "route": "…", "boundary_reason": null, "lean_route": null, "promise_class": "…", "li_function": "yes|no",
          "pair_rule": "3.1|3.2|3.3|3.4(i)|3.4(ii)|3.4(iii)|3.5", "li_g0_state": null, "d6": null, "d7": null,
          "ruled_route": null, "rationale": "…" },                                                               // [R]
  "styling_subtype": "…|null",                                                                                    // [R]
  "properties": {
    "result_mechanisms": [{"value":"fixative_film","members":[{"inci":"PVP","rank":3}]}],                          // [F]
    "hold_route": "present|absent|ambiguous", "weight": "light|medium|rich|unknown", "alcohol_note": "fires|does_not_fire",
    "ingredient_flags": {"silicones":false,"oils":false,"fragrance":true,"salt":false,"propellant":false,"fixative_family":["pvp_vp"]}, // a flag an unreadable token may hide reads "unknown" (RG4-15)
    "provides_heat_protection": {"value": false, "trace": "not_claimed|claim_only|formula_plausible|product_tested"}, // [C+F]
    "chaarlie_hold_level": {"value": 4, "raw": "…", "scale": "…", "tier": "C2", "basis": "…"},                   // [C]
    "flexibility": "…", "finish": "…", "primary_job": null, /* null or a §6.10 vocabulary job */ "secondary_jobs": [], "dropped_jobs": [],               // [C]
    "application_moment": {"primary":"…","also":[]}, "target_zone": "…", "heat_activated": "yes|no|unresolved",    // [U]
    "captured_claims": [], "subtype_extras": {}                                                                   // [C]/[F]/[view]
  },
  "hinweise": { "fired": [ {"flag":"buildup|alcohol_note","basis":"…","note":"…"} ] },                         // [D] always emitted, may be empty
  "derived": { "thickness_fit": "…", "texture_fit": "…", "length_fit": "…", "hold_display": "…", "neutral_fact": null, "cautions": [] }, // [D]
  "evidence": { "<property>": {"decision_type":"…","confidence":"high|moderate|low","evidence_level":"E0|E1|E2","formula_observations":[],"counter_signals":[]} },
  "review_triggers": [], "uncertain_fields": [], "blind_to_final_changes": []
}
```

### 10.2 Blind lanes (PH1-11 (b)) — separate sealed S0 packet `[PH2-6, refined by R2-Q3]`

| Lane input | Contents | Classifies |
|---|---|---|
| **S0 packet** (sealed, PH2-6, R2-Q3) | normalised INCI with ranks and tiers, **re-joined and renumbered by the freeze, with `identity.inci_flags`** (RG2-24, P2-3, P2-4); **`identity.identity_status` in every packet, verified records included, with `identity.inci_alt` next to it `[P3-1]`** (round 3 carried them outside the `identity` block, and lane B read three formula-conflict records as verified, E3-1; a record captured with one list and no conflict ships as `verified`); **`product.confirmed_name_tier`** (C1 pack, C2 brand page, C3 retailer title — a C3 name adds whether its type noun agrees across the retailer titles captured, so §4.2's shop-title fallback can be applied blind, R3-Q2, R3-H4 (d)); verbatim directions **from a G1 tier only** (a C5 page ships as `directions_status: missing`, P4-1); the confirmed product name; the C1/C2 **promise and heat-protection** claims with tiers, each promise line with its **`position`** (`pack_front` · `headline` · `bullet` · `body` · `unknown`, P4-3), and a heat sentence inside a promise line also as its own heat-protection claim (P4-2); format. **Masks only grading words** (below). **Removes** prior routes, subtypes, hold levels, retailer chips, prices, calibration labels | `route`, `boundary_reason`, `promise_class`, `li_function`, pair rule, D6/D7 readings, lean subtype |
| **F/U packet** (sealed; property set §7). Each lane classifies F/U from the blind packet using **its own** S0 output (PH2-6) | normalised INCI; format; verbatim directions with brand, name and strength/look adjectives replaced by `[CLAIM]`; **the lane's own S0 output** | every F and U field (§1.2); `heat_activated` from these directions, raised by the lane itself from the C1/C2 activation statements it sees in the claims packet (RG4-18, replacing RG2-26's blind-only reading) |
| **Claim normalisation** (two independent passes; PH1-11 (c)) | frozen `claims[]` (promise lines with `position`, P4-3) **plus the unmasked confirmed product name** (a C1/C2 claim, §2.2 A3; R2-Q3) | `chaarlie_hold_level`, flexibility, finish, jobs, `cast_expected`, `form_label`, `captured_claims`, the C half of heat |
| **Derived last** | — | echoes, cautions, hold display, neutral fact |

**S0 masking: keep job words, mask grading words `[R2-Q3, RG2-3, P2-1]`.** Blindness hides the answers under test (hold, flexibility, finish grade), not the input the routing rule needs. In the S0 promise lines, the confirmed name and the directions:
- **Kept (job-naming nouns; R2-Q3 names them „such as", so the list is open to other nouns that name a job):** Glanz-Spray / Glanzspray, Glanz, Shine, Gloss, **Glaze, Topcoat** (R4-Q2), Finish / Finishing, Haarspray, Wachs, Puder, Gel, Schaum, Paste, **Beach Waves / Strandwellen** (R4-Q2).
- **Masked (grading words):** hold strengths and hold words with numbers or scales („starker Halt", „Halt 5", „Haltegrad …", dots), flexibility words (flexibel, fest, Kleber …) and finish grades (matt, seidig-glänzend, natürlich …; „glänzend/glänzendes" as an adjective of Finish or Haar). In a compound or a name only the grading part is masked, so the job noun stays readable (e.g. got2b „Mattpaste" keeps „paste"). **Every §7.4 strength word that grades hold is masked, also inside a compound `[Codex review 2026-10-13]`:** „extrastark", „ultrastarke", „superstark", „megastark", „sehr stark" written as one word or hyphenated, „Extra Strong", any „krass…", a strength word that qualifies the styles or the hairdo („ultrastarke Styles", „starke Frisur"), and in the name a strength run placed after the product noun („Haarspray extrastark"). A strength word that grades another result noun stays visible („maximales Volumen", „Ultra Volumen"; RG-13).
- **No editorial labels `[Codex review 2026-10-13]`:** no packet field (S0, F/U or claims) carries a rule or patch id, a round number, freeze or repair provenance or a lexicon version; the freeze strips them (the fact they annotated stays: „repaired", „rank 10") and its leak check reads every string field of every packet.
- **Re-freeze scope:** only the records whose S0 text changes under these lists are re-frozen and re-run (P2-1: in round 2, 71 records lost at least one styling job word and 5 kept none in the lead line or the name — OSiS Sparkler, gotGloss, Glam Mist, Elvital Öl Magique, NIVEA MEN Styling Cream; `round-2/diff.json` `s0_redaction_check`).

Agreement: deterministic F fields are judged like every other field — **resolved, not identical `[R3-L, R3-H4 (c), R4-L]`**: each A/B disagreement is a lane error proven against a clear rule, or a lexicon or rule gap that is closed (fixed in the lexicon, never per product); only differences in user-facing F fields block the lock, calculation-log differences are fixed in the text without a further round. *Superseded:* „deterministic F fields must reach 100 %; a disagreement is a lexicon defect". **For the lock, „the lanes agree" means resolved, not identical `[R3-L]`** (§14.1): every remaining A/B disagreement is either a lane error proven against a clear rule or traces to a rule gap that has been closed, and a final confirmation round shows only lane errors and no new rule gaps. **Which differences count `[R4-L]`:** only differences in user-facing fields block the lock — `route` and recommendability (incl. held vs committed and the evidence gate), `styling_subtype`, `chaarlie_hold_level`, `weight`, `finish`, `flexibility`, `target_zone`, `application_moment`, `primary_job` and `secondary_jobs`, `provides_heat_protection`, and the user-shown notes (alcohol note, buildup note, neutral fact, „Hitzeschutz laut Hersteller", hold display). Differences confined to the calculation log — reason code, rule path, internal review triggers, confidence, internal flags (D8b, `claim_formula_conflict`, …), lexicon placements that move no value — do not block: they are fixed in the text and documented, without a further round (§14.1 (b)). U fields report agreement with coded causes. Routes report agreement per pair rule. Every blind-to-final change is recorded with its reason (property set §7). Cohort independence: products named in this file, the property set or the charter are confirmation-grade only (Mask round-1 lesson); the unseen set (PH1-11 (d)) compensates.
